{\operatorname{pd}}_{R}(\mathfrak{n})<\infty
then
{\operatorname{pd}}_{S}({\mathsf{L}}(\mathfrak{n}))<\infty
; and

(iii)

{\mathfrak{m}}
is Gorenstein injective if and only if
{\mathsf{G}}({\mathfrak{m}})
is Gorenstein injective.

Proof.

(i)-(ii) Since
{\mathsf{L}}
and
{\mathsf{G}}
are exact adjoint functors, we have an isomorphism of abelian groups

(3.1)

{\operatorname{Ext}}^{i}_{S}({\mathsf{L}}(\mathfrak{n}),{\mathfrak{m}})\cong{\operatorname{Ext}}^{i}_{R}(\mathfrak{n},{\mathsf{G}}({\mathfrak{m}}))

for all
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
, all
\mathfrak{n}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(R)
and all
i\geq 0
. This gives that
{\operatorname{id}}_{R}({\mathsf{G}}({\mathfrak{m}}))\leq{\operatorname{id}}_{S}({\mathfrak{m}})
and also immediately implies (ii). Furthermore, given any
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
,
2.4
and (
3.1
) together give isomorphisms

(3.2)

{\operatorname{Ext}}^{i}_{S}({\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})\cong{\operatorname{Ext}}^{i}_{S}({\mathsf{L}}({\mathsf{G}}({\mathfrak{m}})),{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})\cong{\operatorname{Ext}}^{i}_{R}({\mathsf{G}}({\mathfrak{m}}),{\mathsf{G}}({\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})).

We deduce immediately that
{\operatorname{pd}}_{S}({\mathfrak{m}})\leq{\operatorname{pd}}_{R}({\mathsf{G}}({\mathfrak{m}}))
. Since
R
and
S
are assumed to be Gorenstein, finite injective dimension coincides with finite projective dimension for modules over both
R
and
S
, and (i) follows from the above two inequalities.

(iii) Recall that
{\mathfrak{m}}
is Gorenstein injective if and only if
{\operatorname{Ext}}^{i}_{S}(\mathfrak{e},{\mathfrak{m}})=0
for all
\mathfrak{e}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
of finite injective dimension (and similarly for
{\mathsf{G}}({\mathfrak{m}})
and
R
-modules of finite injective dimension). Therefore, (ii) and (
3.1
) give that if
{\mathfrak{m}}
is Gorenstein injective then so is
{\mathsf{G}}({\mathfrak{m}})
. Conversely, (i) and (
3.2
) give that if
{\mathsf{G}}({\mathfrak{m}})
is Gorenstein injective then so is
{\mathfrak{m}}
.
∎

We next show that the Gorenstein projective/injective model structures on
{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
are in fact determined by those on
{\operatorname{Mod}}(R)
. Note that since the notion of weak equivalences doesn’t depend on which model structure we choose to work with (cf.
2.12
), there is no ambiguity in the statement of part (iii) of the Proposition below.

Proposition 3.4
.

With
S
,
R
,
{\mathsf{G}}
and
{\mathsf{L}}
as in
3.1
, we have:

(i)

the Gorenstein projective model structure on
{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
is the right transfer of the Gorenstein projective model structure on
{\operatorname{Mod}}(R)
along
{\mathsf{G}}
. In particular,
{\mathsf{G}}
is a right Quillen functor for the Gorenstein projective model structures;

(ii)

the Gorenstein injective model structure on
{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
is both the left transfer and the right transfer of the Gorenstein injective model structure on
{\operatorname{Mod}}(R)
along
{\mathsf{G}}
. In particular,
{\mathsf{G}}
is both a left and a right Quillen functor for the Gorenstein injective model structures; and

(iii)

the functor
{\mathsf{L}}
preserves weak equivalences.

Proof.

We first show that
{\mathsf{G}}
both preserves and reflects weak equivalences. Since
{\mathsf{G}}
is exact, it follows from
2.8
(i)-(ii) that we only need to show that
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
is a trivial object, i.e. has finite projective dimension, if and only if
{\mathsf{G}}({\mathfrak{m}})
is a trivial object in
{\operatorname{Mod}}(R)
. This holds by
3.3
(i) and thus proves our claim. To show (i), we are therefore left to prove that for a morphism
f:{\mathfrak{m}}\to{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
,
f
is a fibration if and only if
{\mathsf{G}}(f)
is a fibration, in the Gorenstein projective model structures on
{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
and
{\operatorname{Mod}}(R)
respectively. But in this case the fibrations are the epimorphisms and the result follows because
{\mathsf{G}}
is exact and faithful.

To show (ii), we instead need to show that
f
as above is a fibration, resp. cofibration, if and only if
{\mathsf{G}}(f)
is a fibration, resp. cofibration, in the Gorenstein injective model structures on
{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
and
{\operatorname{Mod}}(R)
respectively. The case of cofibrations is dual to the above: they are the monomorphisms, so that the claim holds by exactness and faithfulness of
{\mathsf{G}}
. The fibrations on the other hand are precisely the epimorphisms with Gorenstein injective kernels. Now the claim holds instead by exactness of
{\mathsf{G}}
and
3.3
(iii).

Finally, (iii) follows from
2.8
(i) and
3.3
(ii), given that (i) above implies that
{\mathsf{L}}
is left Quillen for the Gorenstein projective model structure.
∎

Corollary 3.5
.

Continuing with
S
,
R
,
{\mathsf{G}}
and
{\mathsf{L}}
as in
3.1
, we have:

(i)

the two total right derived functors of
{\mathsf{G}}
(with respect to the Gorenstein projective and Gorenstein injective model structures respectively) and the total left derived functor of
{\mathsf{G}}
(with respect to the Gorenstein injective model structure) are all naturally isomorphic to
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{G}})
. Similarly, the two total left derived functors of
{\mathsf{L}}
are naturally isomorphic to
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{L}})
;

(ii)

the functor
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{G}})
is fully faithful; and

(iii)

{\mathsf{L}}
reflects trivial objects, i.e. the converse of
3.3
(ii) holds, if and only if
({\mathsf{L}},{\mathsf{G}})
is a Quillen equivalence (for either/both model structures).

Proof.

Part (i) is immediate from
2.8
. For the rest of this proof, we work with the Gorenstein injective model structure. This suffices to show (iii) because the derived functors of
{\mathsf{G}}
(resp.
{\mathsf{L}}
) for both model structures can be identified with
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{G}})
(resp.
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{L}})
), and moreover an adjoint pair is a Quillen equivalence precisely when the derived functors form an adjoint equivalence of categories (cf.
[
14
, Proposition 1.3.13]
).

(ii) By
2.4
we need to show that the counit of the adjunction
({\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{L}}),{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{G}}))
is an isomorphism. By the proof of
[
14
, Proposition 1.3.13]
, this holds if and only if, for any
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
, the composite

{\mathsf{L}}Q_{c}{\mathsf{G}}{\mathfrak{m}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathsf{L}}q_{c}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathsf{L}}{\mathsf{G}}{\mathfrak{m}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varepsilon_{{\mathfrak{m}}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathfrak{m}}

is a weak equivalence. Here
Q_{c}{\mathsf{G}}{\mathfrak{m}}
denotes a cofibrant replacement of
{\mathsf{G}}{\mathfrak{m}}
in
{\operatorname{Mod}}(R)
, which comes equipped with a trivial fibration
q_{c}:Q_{c}{\mathsf{G}}{\mathfrak{m}}\to{\mathsf{G}}{\mathfrak{m}}
. But by
3.4
(iii) we have that
{\mathsf{L}}q_{c}
is a weak equivalence and
\varepsilon_{{\mathfrak{m}}}
is an isomorphism (cf.
2.4
again), so that our claim holds.

(iii) By the above
\varepsilon_{{\mathfrak{m}}}{\mathsf{L}}q_{c}
is always a weak equivalence. It therefore follows from
[
14
, Corollary 1.3.16]
that
({\mathsf{L}},{\mathsf{G}})
is a Quillen equivalence if and only if
{\mathsf{L}}
reflects weak equivalences between cofibrant objects. But in the Gorenstein injective model structure all objects are cofibrant, so the latter condition says that
{\mathsf{L}}
reflects all weak equivalences. The result now follows from
2.8
(ii).
∎

Note that from
3.4
(iii) and exactness we get that
{\mathsf{L}}
preserves both fibrations and trivial fibrations in the Gorenstein projective model structure. This is somewhat unusual for a
left
Quillen functor. Also, we will see later that the Quillen equivalence as in
3.5
(iii) essentially never holds in the case of parabolic (co)induction from a standard Levi subgroup
M
unless
M=G
, cf.
3.20
. For now we only illustrate that this is clear when
M
is the maximal split torus.

Example 3.6
.

If
M=T
is the maximal split torus, then
{\mathcal{H}}_{T}=k[\widetilde{\Lambda}]
is the group algebra of
\widetilde{\Lambda}=T/(T\cap I)\cong{\mathbb{Z}}^{r_{ss}+r_{Z}}\times T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. Hence
{\mathcal{H}}_{T}
is free of finite rank, coprime with
p
, over the Laurent polynomial ring
k[x_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,x_{r_{ss}+r_{Z}}^{\pm 1}]
. The latter ring has finite global dimension, and therefore so does
{\mathcal{H}}_{T}
(cf.
[
21
, Theorem 7.5.6(iii)]
). In particular, the homotopy category
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{T}))
is zero. On the other hand,
{\mathcal{H}}
typically has infinite global dimension. Indeed, when
G
does not have root system of type
A_{1}\times\cdots\times A_{1}
or when
r_{ss}>0
and
q>2
, then
{\mathcal{H}}
has infinite global dimension and in fact has a simple module
{\mathfrak{m}}
of infinite projective dimension (cf.
[
25
, 7.1-7.3]
). So, under these hypotheses,
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})\neq 0
and
{\operatorname{Ind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{T}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}
is not a Quillen equivalence. And indeed, for this module
{\mathfrak{m}}
one automatically has
{\operatorname{pd}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{T}}({\operatorname{Loc}}_{M}(\mathfrak{n}))<\infty
even though
{\operatorname{pd}}_{{\mathcal{H}}}(\mathfrak{n})=\infty
.

Suppose that
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(R)
and
\mathfrak{n}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
. We have an isomorphism
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{R}({\mathsf{G}}(\mathfrak{n}),{\mathfrak{m}})\cong{\operatorname{Hom}}_{S}(\mathfrak{n},{\mathsf{R}}({\mathfrak{m}}))
. Since
{\mathsf{G}}
is exact we have that
{\mathsf{R}}
preserves injectives, and hence we obtain a first quadrant Grothendieck spectral sequence

(3.3)

E_{2}^{ij}={\operatorname{Ext}}_{S}^{i}(\mathfrak{n},R^{j}{\mathsf{R}}({\mathfrak{m}}))\Rightarrow{\operatorname{Ext}}_{R}^{i+j}({\mathsf{G}}(\mathfrak{n}),{\mathfrak{m}}).

Note that
3.4
(ii) implies that
{\mathsf{R}}
preserves fibrations in the Gorenstein injective model structures, i.e. surjections with Gorenstein injective kernels. Given that the functor
{\mathsf{R}}
is not necessarily right exact, this may seem surprising but it is actually fairly straightforward to show this directly using the above spectral sequence.

Lemma 3.7
.

Suppose that
R
is an
n
-Gorenstein ring. Then:

(i)

the functor
{\mathsf{R}}
has cohomological dimension bounded above by
n
;

(ii)

the Gorenstein injective modules are all acyclic for
{\mathsf{R}}
;

(iii)

an
R
-module
{\mathfrak{m}}
is
{\mathsf{R}}
-acyclic if and only if there is an isomorphism of abelian groups

{\operatorname{Ext}}_{R}^{j}({\mathsf{G}}(\mathfrak{n}),{\mathfrak{m}})\cong{\operatorname{Ext}}^{j}_{S}(\mathfrak{n},{\mathsf{R}}({\mathfrak{m}}))

for all
j\geq 0
and all
\mathfrak{n}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
; and

(iv)

{\mathsf{R}}
preserves Gorenstein injectives.

Proof.

Let
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(R)
. By plugging
\mathfrak{n}=S
in the spectral sequence (
3.3
), the
E_{2}^{ij}
term becomes zero for
i>0
, and we obtain an isomorphism

(3.4)

{\operatorname{Ext}}_{R}^{j}({\mathsf{G}}(S),{\mathfrak{m}})\cong R^{j}{\mathsf{R}}({\mathfrak{m}})

for all
j\geq 0
. But by
3.3
(i),
{\mathsf{G}}(S)
has finite projective dimension and hence
{\operatorname{pd}}_{R}({\mathsf{G}}(S))\leq n
by assumption on
R
. Thus the left hand side of (
3.4
) vanishes for
i>n
, proving (i).

For (ii), assume now that
{\mathfrak{m}}
is Gorenstein injective. Then for any module of finite injective dimension
\mathfrak{e}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(R)
and any
j>0
, we have
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{R}^{j}(\mathfrak{e},{\mathfrak{m}})=0
. Applying this to
\mathfrak{e}={\mathsf{G}}(S)
, we immediately get from (
3.4
) that
R^{j}{\mathsf{R}}({\mathfrak{m}})=0
for all
j>0
as claimed.

For (iii), the spectral sequence (
3.3
) immediately implies that the isomorphism of Ext groups holds when
{\mathfrak{m}}
is is
{\mathsf{R}}
-acyclic. Conversely, assume that
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{R}^{j}({\mathsf{G}}(\mathfrak{n}),{\mathfrak{m}})\cong{\operatorname{Ext}}^{j}_{S}(\mathfrak{n},{\mathsf{R}}({\mathfrak{m}}))
for all
\mathfrak{n}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
and all
j\geq 0
. Then combining this isomorphism with (
3.4
) we obtain

R^{j}{\mathsf{R}}({\mathfrak{m}})\cong{\operatorname{Ext}}^{j}_{S}(S,{\mathsf{R}}({\mathfrak{m}}))=0

for all
j>0
, as required.

Finally, for (iv) we can apply (ii), (iii), and
3.3
(i) again to see that if
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(R)
is Gorenstein injective and if
\mathfrak{e}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
has finite injective dimension then
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{S}^{j}(\mathfrak{e},{\mathsf{R}}({\mathfrak{m}}))\cong{\operatorname{Ext}}_{R}^{j}({\mathsf{G}}(\mathfrak{e}),{\mathfrak{m}})=0
for
j>0
, and thus
{\mathsf{R}}({\mathfrak{m}})
is Gorenstein injective.
∎

Next, we investigate properties of
{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
(resp.
{\operatorname{Ho}}(S)
) viewed as a full subcategory of
{\operatorname{Mod}}(R)
(resp.
{\operatorname{Ho}}(R)
) via the functor
{\mathsf{G}}
(resp.
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{G}}))
. We first note that
{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
is not in general closed under subquotients in
{\operatorname{Mod}}(R)
, and so in particular is not a Serre subcategory, as seen in the following example.

Example 3.8
.

When
{\mathsf{G}}={\operatorname{Ind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}
or
{\operatorname{Coind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}
for some proper Levi subgroup
M\neq G
, the essential image of
{\mathsf{G}}
is not closed under subobjects. Indeed, first let
V={\operatorname{Ind}}_{P}^{G}(\mathbf{1}_{M})\in{\operatorname{Rep}}_{k}^{\infty}(G)
. By standard properties of induction for smooth representations, the trivial representation
\mathbf{1}_{G}
embeds as a subrepresentation of
V
. By left exactness of the functor
U
and applying
[
27
, Theorem 1]
, we see that the trivial character
\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}}
of
{\mathcal{H}}
embeds as a submodule of
{\operatorname{Ind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}})
. However, there does not exist
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M})
such that
{\operatorname{Ind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}({\mathfrak{m}})\cong\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{H}}}
. This is because, by
[
29
, Lemmas 3.5 & 3.6]
, we have an isomorphism
{\operatorname{Ind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}({\mathfrak{m}})\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\oplus n}
of
k
-vector spaces, where
n=\left|W_{0}\right|/\left|W_{0,M}\right|>1
. So in particular
\dim_{k}{\operatorname{Ind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}({\mathfrak{m}})>1
whenever
{\mathfrak{m}}\neq 0
.

Hence we cannot in some cases of interest form an abelian quotient of
{\operatorname{Mod}}(R)
by
{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
via
{\mathsf{G}}
. We now show how the situation is improved by working at the homotopy level, by making use of the triangulated structures of
{\operatorname{Ho}}(S)
and
{\operatorname{Ho}}(R)
.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}
be a triangulated category with shift functor
\Sigma:{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{T}}
. Recall that a subcategory
{\mathcal{S}}
of
{\mathcal{T}}
is called a
triangulated subcategory
if
\Sigma
restricts to an autoequivalence of
{\mathcal{S}}
, if
{\mathcal{S}}
is strictly full (meaning any object of
{\mathcal{T}}
isomorphic to an object of
{\mathcal{S}}
lies in
{\mathcal{S}}
) and if whenever
X\to Y\to Z\to\Sigma X
is a distinguished triangle in
{\mathcal{T}}
with two of
X,Y,Z
lying in
{\mathcal{S}}
, then so does the third one. A subcategory
{\mathcal{S}}
is called
thick
if it is triangulated and closed under direct summands. If
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
is another triangulated category with shift functor
\Sigma^{\prime}
, an additive functor
{\mathsf{F}}:{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
is called
triangulated
if
{\mathsf{F}}\Sigma\cong\Sigma^{\prime}{\mathsf{F}}
and
{\mathsf{F}}
sends distinguished triangles to distinguished triangles. Note that the essential image of a full triangulated functor is a triangulated subcategory. Moreover, if
{\mathcal{S}}
is a triangulated subcategory then it inherits from
{\mathcal{T}}
a triangulated structure and the inclusion
{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathcal{T}}
is a triangulated functor.

Suppose now that
{\mathcal{T}}
contains all small coproducts. For example, the homotopy category of any Gorenstein ring has all small coproducts since Gorenstein projective modules are closed under arbitrary direct sums. Then recall that a triangulated subcategory
{\mathcal{S}}
is called
localising
if it is closed under arbitrary small coproducts. All such subcategories are automatically thick, cf.
[
22
, Proposition 1.6.8]
.

In general there is a Verdier quotient
{\mathcal{T}}/{\mathcal{S}}
, for
{\mathcal{T}}
a triangulated category and
{\mathcal{S}}
a triangulated subcategory, which is itself a triangulated category equipped with a triangulated quotient functor
\pi:{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{T}}/{\mathcal{S}}
, and this construction satisfies a suitable universal property (cf.
[
28
, §II.2.1-II.2.3]
).

We will now see how our setup lifts to the homotopy level. For this we need the notion of a recollement of triangulated categories, which we now recall.

Definition 3.9
.

A
recollement
of triangulated categories is a diagram of triangulated functors

\textstyle{{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{G}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{T}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{I}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{L}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{R}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{Q}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{P}}

such that the following conditions are satisfied:

•

\mathsf{Q}
(resp.
\mathsf{P}
) is the left (resp. right) adjoint of
\mathsf{I}
;

•

\mathsf{L}
(resp.
\mathsf{R}
) is the left (resp. right) adjoint of
\mathsf{G}
;

•

the functors
\mathsf{G}
,
\mathsf{Q}
and
\mathsf{P}
are fully faithful; and

•

the essential image of
\mathsf{G}
is equal to the kernel
{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathsf{I})
.

We briefly describe some properties implied by this definition (cf.
[
20
, §4.9-4.13]
). First,
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
identifies with a thick subcategory of
{\mathcal{T}}
via
{\mathsf{G}}
such that
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}\simeq{\mathcal{T}}/{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
. Moreover, the adjunction morphisms give for any
X\in{\mathcal{T}}
two functorial distinguished triangles

(3.5)

\mathsf{G}\mathsf{R}(X)\to X\to\mathsf{P}\mathsf{I}(X)\to\Sigma(\mathsf{G}\mathsf{R}(X))

and

\mathsf{Q}\mathsf{I}(X)\to X\to\mathsf{G}\mathsf{L}(X)\to\Sigma(\mathsf{Q}\mathsf{I}(X)).

In particular, we see that the smallest triangulated subcategory of
{\mathcal{T}}
containing
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}
is
{\mathcal{T}}
itself, where
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}
is identified as a subcategory of
{\mathcal{T}}
via either one of the fully faithful functors
\mathsf{Q}
and
\mathsf{P}
.

For any category
{\mathcal{C}}
and any full subcategory
{\mathcal{D}}\subseteq{\mathcal{C}}
, we define two full subcategories
{\mathcal{D}}^{\ddagger}
and
{}^{\ddagger}{\mathcal{D}}
(as the symbol
\perp
was already taken) of
{\mathcal{C}}
by

{\mathcal{D}}^{\ddagger}:=\{Y\in{\mathcal{C}}\mid{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)=0\text{ for all }X\in{\mathcal{D}}\}

and

{}^{\ddagger}{\mathcal{D}}:=\{X\in{\mathcal{C}}\mid{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)=0\text{ for all }Y\in{\mathcal{D}}\}.

Then, in the above setting of a recollement, the category
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}
is equivalent to both
({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})^{\ddagger}
and
{}^{\ddagger}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})
via the functors
\mathsf{P}
and
\mathsf{Q}
respectively, where we view
{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}
as a full subcategory of
{\mathcal{T}}
via the functor
\mathsf{G}
. Note that by the adjunctions
{\mathsf{L}}\dashv{\mathsf{G}}\dashv{\mathsf{R}}
, we have
({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})^{\ddagger}={\operatorname{Ker}}({\mathsf{R}})
and
{}^{\ddagger}({\mathcal{T}}^{\prime})={\operatorname{Ker}}({\mathsf{L}})
. In order to establish the existence of a recollement, we have the following:

Proposition 3.10

(
[
20
, Propositions 4.9.1 & 4.13.1]
)
.

Let
{\mathcal{T}}
be a triangulated category and
{\mathcal{S}}
a thick subcategory of
{\mathcal{T}}
.

(i)

The following are equivalent:

(a)

the inclusion
{\mathcal{S}}\xrightarrow{\subseteq}{\mathcal{T}}
has a right adjoint;

(b)

the quotient functor
{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{T}}/{\mathcal{S}}
has a a right adjoint; and

(c)

the inclusion
{\mathcal{S}}^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\subseteq}{\mathcal{T}}
has a left adjoint and
{{}^{\dagger}}({\mathcal{S}}^{\dagger})={\mathcal{S}}
.

(ii)

If the equivalent conditions of (i) and their dual hold, then the inclusion
{\mathcal{S}}\xrightarrow{\subseteq}{\mathcal{T}}
is part of a recollement

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 5.77779pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.77779pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.409pt\raise 6.04828pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\subseteq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.45688pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 20.00415pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 40.23051pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 60.45688pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{T}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 87.90547pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{T}}/{\mathcal{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 73.67844pt\raise 5.9164pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 73.23181pt\raise-5.55367pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

We now go back to
3.1
and apply the above to get:

Proposition 3.11
.

The functors
{\mathsf{G}}
,
{\mathsf{L}}
and
{\mathsf{R}}
induce a recollement

\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ho}}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{G}})}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ho}}(R)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{L}})}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{R}{\mathsf{R}}}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ho}}(R)/{\operatorname{Ho}}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

of triangulated categories, where we identify
{\operatorname{Ho}}(S)
with
{\operatorname{Im}}({\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{G}}))
and
\pi:{\operatorname{Ho}}(R)\to{\operatorname{Ho}}(R)/{\operatorname{Im}}({\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{G}}))
is the quotient functor.

Proof.

We view our homotopy categories as coming from the Gorenstein injective model structures. Then the existence of the adjoint triple
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{L}})\dashv{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{G}})\dashv\mathbf{R}{\mathsf{R}}
is immediate from
3.4
(ii) and
3.5
(i), and
3.5
(ii) says that
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{G}})
is fully faithful. We will be done by
3.10
(ii) if we show that these functors are triangulated and
{\operatorname{Im}}({\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{G}}))
is a thick subcategory.

For this, first recall that for a Gorenstein projective module
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
, its suspension in
{\operatorname{Ho}}(S)
is defined to be the cokernel of a cofibration to a projective module. Being an exact left Quillen functor with exact right adjoint, the functor
{\mathsf{L}}
preserves this construction so that
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{L}})\circ\Sigma\cong\Sigma\circ{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{L}})
. Taking successive right adjoints of this identity gives us that all three functors commute with the shift functor. Furthermore, since
{\mathsf{L}}
is exact and preserves the construction of
\Sigma
, we see that it in fact preserves the distinguished triangles as given in (
2.10
). Thus
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{L}})
is triangulated. A completely dual argument using fiber sequences and the loop functor shows that
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{G}})
and
\mathbf{R}{\mathsf{R}}
preserve distinguished triangles, recalling that
{\mathsf{R}}
preserves short exact sequences of Gorenstein injective modules by
3.7
. Finally,
{\operatorname{Im}}({\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{G}}))
is in fact a localising subcategory and hence is thick, since
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{G}})
commutes with arbitrary direct sums as it is a left adjoint.
∎

Remark 3.12
.

The fact that
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{L}})
,
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathsf{G}})
and
\mathbf{R}{\mathsf{R}}
are triangulated also follows immediately from general properties of stable model categories. Indeed, if
({\mathsf{F}},{\mathsf{G}})
is a Quillen adjunction between stable model categories, then the derived functors
\mathbf{L}{\mathsf{F}}
and
\mathbf{R}{\mathsf{G}}
commute with the suspension by
[
6
, Corollary 3.1.4]
, noting that the loop functor is the inverse of suspension. Moreover, in any such model category the distinguished triangles in the homotopy category are given by cofiber sequences, and left/right derived functors preserve those (cf.
[
14
, Proposition 6.4.1 & Theorem 7.1.11]
).

3.2.
Extension to several parabolics

We now extend the constructions from the previous subsection in the case where
R={\mathcal{H}}
,
S={\mathcal{H}}_{M}
for some standard Levi subgroup
M
, and
{\mathsf{G}}={\operatorname{Ind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}
with left adjoint
{\mathsf{L}}={\mathcal{L}^{G}_{M}}
and right adjoint
{\mathsf{R}}={\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}
. To simplify the notation, we will write
I^{G}_{M}
for
{\operatorname{Ind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}
. We will denote by
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{M}
the quotient triangulated category of
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})/{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M})
, and we have an adjoint triple

\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{G}_{M}}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell^{G}_{M}}
\scriptstyle{r^{G}_{M}}

with
\ell^{G}_{M}
and
r^{G}_{M}
both fully faithful, as given by the recollement in
3.11
. The following result will be our main aim in this subsection.

Theorem 3.13
.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}=\{M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}\}
be a collection of standard Levi subgroups of
G
, and let
{\mathcal{S}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
be the smallest thick subcategory of
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
containing
{\operatorname{Im}}({\operatorname{Ho}}(I_{M_{1}}^{G})),\ldots,{\operatorname{Im}}({\operatorname{Ho}}(I_{M_{n}}^{G}))
.

(i)

{\mathcal{S}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
is a localising subcategory and the inclusion
i^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}:{\mathcal{S}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}\hookrightarrow{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
has both a left and a right adjoint, which form part of a recollement

\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{L}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{M}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}}
\scriptstyle{r^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}}

of triangulated categories, where
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{M}}={\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})/{\mathcal{S}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
.

(ii)

Suppose
n>1
. Let
1\leq i\leq n
and
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
, and write
{\mathcal{M}}_{i}={\mathcal{M}}\setminus\{M_{i}\}
. Then there is a distinguished triangle

i^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}({\mathfrak{m}})\longrightarrow i^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}({\mathfrak{m}})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}\longrightarrow\Sigma i^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}({\mathfrak{m}})

where
{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}=r^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}\pi^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{G}_{M_{i}})\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M_{i}}({\mathfrak{m}})
, and similarly there is a distinguished triangle

i^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}\mathcal{L}^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}({\mathfrak{m}})\longrightarrow i^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{L}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}({\mathfrak{m}})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\Sigma i^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}\mathcal{L}^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}({\mathfrak{m}})

where
{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}^{\prime}=\ell^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}\pi^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{G}_{M_{i}}){\operatorname{Ho}}(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{M_{i}})({\mathfrak{m}})
.

(iii)

We have
{\operatorname{Im}}(\ell^{G}_{\mathcal{M}})=\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}{\operatorname{Ker}}({\operatorname{Ho}}(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{M_{i}}))
and
{\operatorname{Im}}(r^{G}_{\mathcal{M}})=\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M_{i}})
.

As a first step, we lift a result of Abe to the homotopy level.

Lemma 3.14
.

Suppose that
M
and
L
are two standard Levi subgroups. Then we have:

(i)

{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{L}^{G}_{M}})\circ{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{G}_{L})\cong{\operatorname{Ho}}(I_{M\cap L}^{M})\circ{\operatorname{Ho}}(\mathcal{L}_{M\cap L}^{L})
; and

(ii)

\mathbf{R}{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}\circ{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{G}_{L})\cong{\operatorname{Ho}}(I_{M\cap L}^{M})\circ\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}_{M\cap L}^{L}
.

Proof.

It was proved in
[
2
, Lemma 5.2]
that
{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}\circ I^{G}_{L}\cong I_{M\cap L}^{M}\circ\mathcal{R}_{M\cap L}^{L}
. By taking left adjoints, we deduce that
{\mathcal{L}^{G}_{M}}\circ I_{L}^{G}\cong I_{M\cap L}^{M}\circ\mathcal{L}_{M\cap L}^{L}
. Part (i) is now immediate, and part (ii) then follows by taking right adjoints in (i).
∎

To prove
3.13
we will argue by induction on
n:=\left|{\mathcal{M}}\right|
, the case
n=1
being
3.11
. We will need to establish some preparatory results first, under the assumption that the statement of
3.13
(i) holds for some fixed
n\geq 1
and all split reductive groups. Assuming this from now on, we let
{\mathcal{M}}=\{M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}\}
be a collection of
n
distinct standard Levi subgroups and we fix another standard Levi subgroup
L
. Then
L_{i}:=M_{i}\cap L
is a standard Levi subgroup of
L
, and we may embed fully faithfully each
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{L_{i}})
inside
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{L})
via
{\operatorname{Ho}}(I_{L_{i}}^{L})
. Thus we may consider the smallest thick subcategory
{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}^{L}
of
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{L})
which contains these essential images, and by assumption we also have a recollement

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.06255pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.06255pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}^{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 128.8416pt\raise 24.72002pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.71446pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ell^{L}_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}}$}}}\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 128.5511pt\raise-24.72002pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.71446pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{r^{L}_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 102.83023pt\raise-7.99988pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

With this notation and assumptions established, we have the following:

Lemma 3.15
.

(i)

The functors
{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{G}_{L})
,
\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}_{L}^{G}
and
{\operatorname{Ho}}(\mathcal{L}_{L}^{G})
induce a triple of triangulated functors

\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{L})_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(I^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{M}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}}
\scriptstyle{(\mathcal{R}_{L}^{G})_{\mathcal{M}}}

such that

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}\circ{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{G}_{L})

\displaystyle=(I^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}\circ\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}^{L},

\displaystyle\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}^{L}\circ{\operatorname{Ho}}(\mathcal{L}_{L}^{G})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}\circ\pi_{\mathcal{M}}^{G},

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad\quad\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}^{L}\circ\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}_{L}^{G}

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}\circ\pi_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}.

(ii)

The functor
(I^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}
is naturally isomorphic to both
\pi_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}\circ{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{G}_{L})\circ\ell^{L}_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}
and
\pi_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}\circ{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{G}_{L})\circ r^{L}_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}
. Similarly, we have natural isomorphisms

(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}\cong\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}^{L}\circ{\operatorname{Ho}}(\mathcal{L}_{L}^{G})\circ\ell_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}

and

(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}\cong\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}^{L}\circ\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}_{L}^{G}\circ r_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}.

Proof.

(i) By transitivity of parabolic induction (cf.
[
29
, Proposition 4.3]
), for each
1\leq i\leq n
we have

I^{G}_{L}\circ I^{L}_{L_{i}}\cong I^{G}_{L_{i}}\cong I^{G}_{M_{i}}\circ I^{M_{i}}_{L_{i}}

and so in particular
I^{G}_{L}\circ I^{L}_{L_{i}}
factors through
I^{G}_{M_{i}}
. Hence the same holds for the corresponding homotopy functors. As a result it follows that the composite
\pi_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}\circ{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{G}_{L}):{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{L})\to{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{M}}
kills the essential image of each
{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{L}_{L_{i}})
and hence kills
{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}^{L}
. By the universal property of quotients, this implies that there is an induced triangulated functor

(I^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}:{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{L})_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}\to{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{M}}

with the property that
\pi_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}\circ{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{G}_{L})=(I^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}\circ\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}^{L}
as required.

The construction of
(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}
is very similar. By
3.14
(ii), the composite
\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}_{L}^{G}\circ{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{G}_{M_{i}})
factors through
{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{L}_{L_{i}})
for each
1\leq i\leq n
. Hence it follows that the composite
\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}^{L}\circ\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}_{L}^{G}
kills the essential image of
{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{G}_{M_{i}})
for each
i
, and hence it kills
{\mathcal{S}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
. Again, we then have that
\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}^{L}\circ\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}_{L}^{G}
factorises as
(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}\circ\pi_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}
for some triangulated functor

(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}:{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{M}}\to{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{L})_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}.

The construction of
(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}
is completely analogous, using
3.14
(i).

(ii) The two functors
r_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}
and
\ell_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}
are both fully faithful and hence we have natural isomorphisms

\pi_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}\circ\ell_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}\cong{\operatorname{id}}_{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{M}}}\cong\pi_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}\circ r_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}

by
2.4
. So we get the identity for
(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}
by pre-composing the functor
\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}^{L}\circ\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}_{L}^{G}=(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}\circ\pi_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}
by
r_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}
and applying the above natural isomorphism. The identities for
(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}
and
(I^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}
are obtained similarly.
∎

Proposition 3.16
.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{M}}\cup\{L\}
. The triple of functors from
3.15
is an adjoint triple, and it forms part of a recollement of the form

(3.6)

\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{L})_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(I^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{M}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}}
\scriptstyle{(\mathcal{R}_{L}^{G})_{\mathcal{M}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell^{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{r^{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}}

where
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}={\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{M}}/{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{L})_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}
.

Proof.

First, we show that the functors are adjoints and that
(I^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}
is fully faithful. This follows from the identities in
3.15
(ii). Indeed, we see that
(\mathcal{R}_{L}^{G})_{\mathcal{M}}\cong\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}^{L}\circ\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}_{L}^{G}\circ r_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}
is a composite of right adjoints so is a right adjoint, with left adjoint
\pi_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}\circ{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{G}_{L})\circ\ell^{L}_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}\cong(I^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}
. Similarly,
(\mathcal{L}_{L}^{G})_{\mathcal{M}}\cong\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}^{L}\circ{\operatorname{Ho}}(\mathcal{L}_{L}^{G})\circ\ell_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}
is a composite of left adjoints, so is left adjoint to
\pi_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}\circ{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{G}_{L})\circ r^{L}_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}\cong(I^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}
as required. Finally, we saw in the proof of
3.15
that
\pi_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}^{L}\circ\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}_{L}^{G}\circ i^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}=0
. After taking left adjoints this gives that

{\operatorname{Im}}({\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{G}_{L})\circ\ell^{L}_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L})\subseteq{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}})={\operatorname{Im}}(\ell^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}).

As
(\pi_{\mathcal{M}}^{G})|_{{\operatorname{Im}}(\ell^{G}_{\mathcal{M}})}
is an equivalence, we deduce that
(I^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}\cong\pi_{\mathcal{M}}^{G}\circ{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{G}_{L})\circ\ell^{L}_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}
is a composite of fully faithful functors and so is fully faithful.

Finally, we will be done by
3.10
(ii) if we show that the essential image of
(I^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}
is localising (and in particular thick). For this, note that since
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M})
and
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
have arbitrary direct sums so do their quotient categories
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{L})_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap L}
and
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{M}}
because the quotient functors are left adjoints. Then
(I^{G}_{L})_{\mathcal{M}}
preserves these direct sums since it is also a left adjoint.
∎

Proof of
3.13
.

(i) As stated earlier, we work by induction on
n
. Assuming the statements hold for
n\geq 1
, let
M_{1},\ldots,M_{n+1}
be distinct standard Levi subgroups and write
{\mathcal{M}}=\{M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}\}
and
{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{M}}\cup\{M_{n+1}\}
as before. By setting
L=M_{n+1}
in
3.16
, we see that there is a recollement as in (
3.6
) and we write again
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}
for the quotient
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{M}}/{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M_{n+1}})_{{\mathcal{M}}\cap M_{n+1}}
.

We then define the functor
\pi^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}
to be the composite
\pi^{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}\circ\pi^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}:{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}
and let
{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}:={\operatorname{Ker}}(\pi^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}})
. Note that
\pi^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}
has fully faithful left and right adjoints, given by
\ell^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}:=\ell^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}\circ\ell^{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}
and
r^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}:=r^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}\circ r^{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}
respectively. Therefore it follows from
3.10
that the inclusion
{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}\to{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
also has both a left and a right adjoint, and that these adjunctions form a recollement. Note also that
{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}
is automatically localising, because the functor
\pi^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}
commutes with arbitrary direct sums as it is a left adjoint. We are thus only left to check that
{\mathcal{S}}^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}={\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}
.

For this, we need to show that if
{\mathcal{S}}
is an arbitrary thick subcategory of
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
containing the essential images of
{\operatorname{Ho}}(I_{M_{1}}^{G}),\ldots,{\operatorname{Ho}}(I_{M_{n+1}}^{G})
, then
{\mathcal{S}}
contains
{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}
. It suffices to show that the quotient functor
\mathcal{Q}:{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})\to{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})/{\mathcal{S}}
factors through
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}
. Certainly,
{\mathcal{S}}
contains
{\mathcal{S}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
by induction and hence
\mathcal{Q}
factors through some
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}:{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{M}}\to{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})/{\mathcal{S}}
. But
3.15
(ii) then ensures that
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
kills the essential image of
(I^{G}_{M_{n+1}})_{\mathcal{M}}
, and hence it factors through
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}}
as claimed.

For (ii), we assume
n>1
and fix
1\leq i\leq n
. We denote by
\eta^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
(resp.
\eta^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}
, resp.
\eta^{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}_{\mathcal{M}}
) the unit of the adjunction
\pi^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}}\dashv r^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}}
(resp.
\pi^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}\dashv r^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}
, resp.
\pi^{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}_{\mathcal{M}}\dashv r^{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}_{\mathcal{M}}
). Then by the recollement in (i) and (
3.5
) we have two distinguished triangles

\displaystyle i^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}({\mathfrak{m}})\longrightarrow

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{m}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eta^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}r^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}\pi^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}({\mathfrak{m}})\longrightarrow\Sigma i^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}({\mathfrak{m}})\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle i^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\mathfrak{m}})\longrightarrow

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{m}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eta^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}r^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}}\pi^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\mathfrak{m}})\longrightarrow\Sigma i^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\mathfrak{m}})

for any
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
. Recall that
\pi^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}}=\pi^{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}_{\mathcal{M}}\circ\pi^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}
and
r^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}}=r^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}\circ r^{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}_{\mathcal{M}}
. Next, we consider the triangle (
3.5
) for the object
\overline{{\mathfrak{m}}}:=\pi^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}({\mathfrak{m}})
in the setting of the recollement (
3.6
) for the collection of Levi subgroups
{\mathcal{M}}_{i}
and
L=M_{i}
. Applying the triangulated functor
r^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}
to it, we get

\mathfrak{n}\longrightarrow r^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}(\overline{{\mathfrak{m}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi}}{{\longrightarrow}}r^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}}\pi^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\mathfrak{m}})\longrightarrow\Sigma\mathfrak{n}

where
\mathfrak{n}:=r^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}(I^{G}_{M_{i}})_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M_{i}})_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}(\overline{{\mathfrak{m}}})
and
\varphi=r^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}(\eta^{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}_{\mathcal{M}})_{\overline{{\mathfrak{m}}}}
. Since we have
\eta^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}=\varphi\circ\eta^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}
, we may apply the octahedral axiom to obtain a distinguished triangle

(3.7)

i^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}({\mathfrak{m}})\longrightarrow i^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\mathfrak{m}})\longrightarrow\mathfrak{n}\longrightarrow\Sigma i^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}({\mathfrak{m}}),

cf.
[
5
, 0145]
. Since
\mathfrak{n}\cong r^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}\pi^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}{\operatorname{Ho}}(I^{G}_{M_{i}})\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M_{i}}({\mathfrak{m}})
, cf.
3.15
(i), this is the desired triangle. The construction for the left adjoints is completely dual to this.

Finally, for (iii) we only prove the formula for the essential image of
r^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
, the proof for
\ell^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
being completely analogous. We argue again by induction on
n
, the case
n=1
being immediate from
3.11
. Assume the equality holds for some
n\geq 1
and any collection of
n
standard Levi subgroups. Again, let
M_{1},\ldots,M_{n+1}
be distinct standard Levi subgroups and write
{\mathcal{M}}=\{M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}\}
and
{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{M}}\cup\{M_{n+1}\}
. Note first that
{\operatorname{Im}}(r^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}})={\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}})
and
{\operatorname{Im}}(r^{G}_{\mathcal{M}})={\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}})
. So by the induction hypothesis, the inclusion
\bigcap_{i=1}^{n+1}{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M_{i}})\subseteq{\operatorname{Im}}(r^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}})
is automatic from the triangle (
3.7
) applied to
{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
and
i=n+1
. Assuming now that
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}})
, the same application of (
3.7
) then implies that
i^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}({\mathfrak{m}})
lies in
{\operatorname{Im}}(r^{G}_{\mathcal{M}})={\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}})
. But since
i^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
is fully faithful we may apply
2.4
to obtain

0=\mathcal{R}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}i^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}({\mathfrak{m}})\cong\mathcal{R}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}({\mathfrak{m}})

and the induction hypothesis gives
{\mathfrak{m}}\in\bigcap_{j=1}^{n}{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M_{j}})
. Replacing
{\mathcal{M}}
by
{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}\setminus\{M_{n}\}
and applying (
3.7
) to
{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
and
i=n
instead, the above argument gives that
{\mathfrak{m}}\in\bigcap_{j\neq n}{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M_{j}})
as well and hence we obtain
{\mathfrak{m}}\in\bigcap_{j=1}^{n+1}{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M_{j}})
as claimed.
∎

3.3.
Supersingular modules and the derived adjoints

We must check that the recollement obtained in
3.13
(i) is non-trivial, i.e. that the quotient categories
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{M}}
are non-zero. This will follow by considering supersingular modules. In this subsection, we assume that
k
is algebraically closed.

Proposition 3.17
.

Suppose that either
G
does not have root system of type
A_{1}\times\cdots\times A_{1}
(possibly empty product) or that
r_{ss}>0
and
q>2
. Then there exists a simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-module of infinite projective dimension.

Proof.

If
G
does not have root system of type
A_{1}\times\cdots\times A_{1}
then any simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-module has infinite projective dimension by
[
19
, Theorem 7.7]
. So assume that
G
has type
A_{1}\times\cdots\times A_{1}
(non-empty product) and that
q>2
. By
[
19
, Theorem 7.7]
again, our claim amounts to showing that there exists a
k
-valued character
\xi
of the group
T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
such that
S_{\xi}\neq S
(cf. (
2.2
)). But since
p
does not divide the order of
T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
and as
T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
is abelian, the intersection of the kernels of all the non-trivial
k
-valued characters of
T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
is trivial. Let
\alpha
be a simple root and
s=s_{\alpha}\in S
, and note that the group
T_{s}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
is non-trivial by our assumption that
q>2
. Then there must exist a character
\xi
with
\xi|_{T_{s}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}\neq 1
and so
S_{\xi}\neq S
.
∎

Proposition 3.18
.

Suppose that
M
is a proper Levi subgroup of
G
and let
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
.

(i)

If
{\mathfrak{m}}
is supersingular, then
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{L}^{G}_{M}})({\mathfrak{m}})=0
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M})
.

(ii)

If
{\mathfrak{m}}
is uniformly supersingular, then
\mathbf{R}{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}({\mathfrak{m}})=0
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M})
.

Proof.

These were proved in
[
4
, Proposition 4.27]
(see also
[
2
, Proposition 5.18]
) at the abelian level. Therefore (i) follows immediately. For (ii), let
\lambda_{0}^{+}
be as in
2.1
and let
\mathcal{O}^{+}=\widetilde{W}\cdot\lambda_{0}^{+}
. Write
z:=z_{\mathcal{O}^{+}}
and fix
n\geq 1
such that
{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot z^{n}=0
. First, take an arbitrary
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
. Since the element
z\in{\mathcal{H}}
is central, multiplication by
z^{n}
is
{\mathcal{H}}
-linear on
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
and hence induces an
{\mathcal{H}}_{M}
-linear endomorphism
f\mapsto f\cdot z^{n}
of
{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}({\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})={\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}^{+}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M},{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})
(defined by
(f\cdot z^{n})(h)=f(h)\cdot z^{n}
). We claim that this is in fact an automorphism of
{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}({\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})
.

To see this, we use the fact that
T_{\lambda_{0}^{+}}^{n+1}=z^{n}T_{\lambda_{0}^{+}}
(cf.
[
2
, Lemma 5.17]
and
[
29
, Lemma 2.18]
). Now let
f\in{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}({\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})
. Suppose first that
f\cdot z^{n}=0
. Then for all
h\in{\mathcal{H}}_{M}
, we have

0=f(h)\cdot z^{n}T_{\lambda_{0}^{+}}=f(h)\cdot T_{\lambda_{0}^{+}}^{n+1}=f(h\cdot(T^{M}_{\lambda_{0}^{+}})^{n+1})

by definition of the map
j_{M}^{+}
(cf. (
2.4
)). Since
T^{M}_{\lambda_{0}^{+}}
is invertible in
{\mathcal{H}}_{M}
, this means that
f=0
. To get surjectivity, define
g:{\mathcal{H}}_{M}\to{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
by
g(h)=f(h\cdot(T^{M}_{\lambda_{0}^{+}})^{-n-1})\cdot T_{\lambda_{0}^{+}}
. This is
{\mathcal{H}}_{M^{+}}
-linear because
T^{M}_{\lambda_{0}^{+}}
is central in
{\mathcal{H}}_{M}
, and we have

\displaystyle(g\cdot z^{n})(h)

\displaystyle=f(h\cdot(T^{M}_{\lambda_{0}^{+}})^{-n-1})\cdot T_{\lambda_{0}^{+}}z^{n}

\displaystyle=f(h\cdot(T^{M}_{\lambda_{0}^{+}})^{-n-1})\cdot T_{\lambda_{0}^{+}}^{n+1}

\displaystyle=f(h)

for all
h\in{\mathcal{H}}_{M}
.

We now specialise to the case where
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}=Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}}
is any Gorenstein injective replacement of
{\mathfrak{m}}
, which by definition comes equipped with an
{\mathcal{H}}
-linear weak equivalence
i_{{\mathfrak{m}}}:{\mathfrak{m}}\to Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}}
. Since we may view the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot z^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{{\mathfrak{m}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{{\mathfrak{m}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot z^{n}}

as a square in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
, we deduce that multiplication by
z^{n}
induces the zero endomorphism of
Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}}
in the homotopy category. Thus the image of this endomorphism under the additive functor
\mathbf{R}{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}
is also zero. As
Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}}
is Gorenstein injective, i.e. fibrant, we have an isomorphism

0=\mathbf{R}{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}(Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}}\xrightarrow{\cdot z^{n}}Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}})\cong{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}(Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}}\xrightarrow{\cdot z^{n}}Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}})

in the arrow category of
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M})
. As we have seen above, the right hand map is an isomorphism, from which we deduce that
\mathbf{R}{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}({\mathfrak{m}})\cong{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}(Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}})\cong 0
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M})
, as required.
∎

Remark 3.19
.

Similarly to the above proof, take
\lambda_{0}^{\pm}
as in
2.1
and let
\mathcal{O}^{\pm}=\widetilde{W}\cdot\lambda_{0}^{\pm}
. Then part (i) of the above result holds more generally whenever every element of
{\mathfrak{m}}
is killed by a power of
z_{\mathcal{O}^{-}}
, and part (ii) holds whenever
z_{\mathcal{O}^{+}}
acts nilpotently on
{\mathfrak{m}}
. This follows from the above proof and because the abelian analogue of
3.18
holds in that higher level of generality, cf. the proof of
[
4
, Proposition 4.27]
.

Corollary 3.20
.

Suppose that either
G
does not have root system of type
A_{1}\times\cdots\times A_{1}
(possibly empty product) or that
r_{ss}>0
and
q>2
. Then for any collection
{\mathcal{M}}
of proper standard Levi subgroups, the quotient category
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{M}}
is non-zero. In particular, for any proper standard Levi subgroup
M
of
G
, we have that
I^{G}_{M}
is not a Quillen equivalence.

Proof.

By
3.17
,
3.18
and
3.13
(iii) there is a simple module
{\mathfrak{m}}
which is non-zero in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
and which lies in both
{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}})
and
{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}})
. By the recollement in
3.13
(i),
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{M}}
is equivalent to both of these via the functors
\ell^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
and
r^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
, so we are done.
∎

As another corollary, we deduce that uniformly supersingular modules are Ext-orthogonal to parabolically induced modules in large enough degree without assuming any finiteness condition on these modules. This extends to any positive degree for finite length modules (cf.
3.25
).

Corollary 3.21
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
is uniformly supersingular and let
\mathfrak{n}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M})
be any module. Then

{\operatorname{Ext}}_{{\mathcal{H}}}^{i}({\operatorname{Ind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}(\mathfrak{n}),{\mathfrak{m}})=0

for all
i>r_{ss}+r_{Z}+1
.

Proof.

Let
Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}}
be the fibrant replacement of
{\mathfrak{m}}
. It follows from
3.18
(ii) that
{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}(Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}})
has finite injective dimension. Since
{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}(Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}})
is also Gorenstein injective by
3.7
(iv), we deduce that it is injective by
[
15
, Corollary 8.5]
. Applying now
3.7
(ii)-(iii), we obtain that

{\operatorname{Ext}}_{{\mathcal{H}}}^{i}(I^{G}_{M}(\mathfrak{n}),Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}})\cong{\operatorname{Ext}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}}^{i}(\mathfrak{n},{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}(Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}}))=0

for any
i>0
. Moreover, by definition there is a short exact sequence
0\to{\mathfrak{m}}\to Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}}\to\mathfrak{e}\to 0
with
\mathfrak{e}
of finite injective dimension, bounded above by
r_{ss}+r_{Z}
. The long exact sequence on
{\operatorname{Ext}}
and the vanishing above then gives us that

{\operatorname{Ext}}_{{\mathcal{H}}}^{i-1}(I^{G}_{M}(\mathfrak{n}),\mathfrak{e})\cong{\operatorname{Ext}}_{{\mathcal{H}}}^{i}(I^{G}_{M}(\mathfrak{n}),{\mathfrak{m}})

for all
i>1
. The bound on the injective dimension of
\mathfrak{e}
gives the result.
∎

Remark 3.22
.

We note that the proofs of
3.18
and
3.21
remain valid without the assumption that
k
is algebraically closed.

Next, we compute the image of all simple modules under the adjoints of parabolic induction at the homotopy level. For that we need the corresponding formula at the level of the module categories, due to Abe, which we recall now. We refer the reader back to (
2.7
)-(
2.8
) for the construction of the simple
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules.

Theorem 3.23
.

Let
(P,\mathfrak{n},Q)
be a standard supersingular triple for
{\mathcal{H}}
. Then for
P^{\prime}=M^{\prime}N^{\prime}
another standard parabolic subgroup of
G
, we have

\mathcal{L}^{G}_{M^{\prime}}(I_{\mathcal{H}}(P,\mathfrak{n},Q))\cong\begin{cases}I_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M^{\prime}}}(P\cap M^{\prime},\mathfrak{n},Q\cap M^{\prime})&\text{if $P\subseteq P^{\prime}$ and $\Pi(\mathfrak{n})\subseteq\Pi_{Q}\cup\Pi_{P^{\prime}}$,}\\
0&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

and

\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M^{\prime}}(I_{\mathcal{H}}(P,\mathfrak{n},Q))\cong\begin{cases}I_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M^{\prime}}}(P\cap M^{\prime},\mathfrak{n},Q\cap M^{\prime})&\text{if $Q\subseteq P^{\prime}$,}\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proof.

The analogous statement was proved in
[
2
, Theorem 5.20]
for the adjoints of parabolic coinduction and the simple modules presented in the form
CI_{\mathcal{H}}(P,\mathfrak{n},Q)
. We just check that this still holds for the adjoints of parabolic induction and the simple modules in the form
I_{\mathcal{H}}(P,\mathfrak{n},Q)
. Write
\sigma
for the
{\mathcal{H}}_{M^{\prime}}
-module
\mathcal{L}^{G}_{M^{\prime}}(I_{\mathcal{H}}(P,\mathfrak{n},Q))
. First, we have by (
2.9
) and
loc. cit.
that

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle\cong\iota_{0}^{w_{0}(M^{\prime})}{\operatorname{Loc}}_{w_{0}(M^{\prime})}(CI_{\mathcal{H}}(w_{0}(P),\iota_{0}^{M}\mathfrak{n},w_{0}(Q)))

\displaystyle\cong\begin{cases}\iota_{0}^{w_{0}(M^{\prime})}CI_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M^{\prime}}}(w_{0}(P\cap M^{\prime}),\iota_{0}^{M}\mathfrak{n},w_{0}(Q\cap M^{\prime}))&\text{if $P\subseteq P^{\prime}$ and $\Pi(\mathfrak{n})\subseteq\Pi_{Q}\cup\Pi_{P^{\prime}}$,}\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

So we may assume that
P\subseteq P^{\prime}
and
\Pi(\mathfrak{n})\subseteq\Pi_{Q}\cup\Pi_{P^{\prime}}
. Next, for any standard parabolic
Q\cap M^{\prime}\subseteq Q^{\prime}\subseteq(P\cap M^{\prime})(\mathfrak{n})
of
M^{\prime}
with Levi subgroup
L^{\prime}
we have
e_{{\mathcal{H}}_{w_{0}(L^{\prime})}}(\iota_{0}^{M}\mathfrak{n})\cong\iota_{0}^{L^{\prime}}e_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L^{\prime}}}(\mathfrak{n})
, cf.
[
4
, Lemma 4.22]
. From this and the above we get

\displaystyle\iota_{0}^{w_{0}(M^{\prime})}{\operatorname{Coind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{w_{0}(L^{\prime})}}^{{\mathcal{H}}_{w_{0}(M^{\prime})}}\left(e_{{\mathcal{H}}_{w_{0}(L^{\prime})}}(\iota_{0}^{M}\mathfrak{n})\right)

\displaystyle\cong\iota_{0}^{w_{0}(M^{\prime})}{\operatorname{Coind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{w_{0}(L^{\prime})}}^{{\mathcal{H}}_{w_{0}(M^{\prime})}}\left(\iota_{0}^{L^{\prime}}e_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L^{\prime}}}(\mathfrak{n})\right)

\displaystyle\cong\iota_{0}^{w_{0}(M^{\prime})}{\operatorname{Ind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{w_{0,M^{\prime}}(w_{0}(L^{\prime}))}}^{{\mathcal{H}}_{w_{0}(M^{\prime})}}\left(\iota_{w_{0}(M^{\prime})}^{w_{0}(L^{\prime})}\iota_{0}^{L^{\prime}}e_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L^{\prime}}}(\mathfrak{n})\right)

\displaystyle\cong{\operatorname{Ind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L^{\prime}}}^{{\mathcal{H}}_{M^{\prime}}}\left(\iota_{0,w_{0}(M^{\prime})}^{w_{0}(L^{\prime})}\iota_{w_{0}(M^{\prime})}^{w_{0}(L^{\prime})}\iota_{0}^{L^{\prime}}e_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L^{\prime}}}(\mathfrak{n})\right)

\displaystyle\cong{\operatorname{Ind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L^{\prime}}}^{{\mathcal{H}}_{M^{\prime}}}\left(\iota_{0}^{w_{0}(L^{\prime})}\iota_{0}^{L^{\prime}}e_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L^{\prime}}}(\mathfrak{n})\right)

\displaystyle\cong{\operatorname{Ind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L^{\prime}}}^{{\mathcal{H}}_{M^{\prime}}}\left(e_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L^{\prime}}}(\mathfrak{n})\right),

where the second, third and fourth isomorphisms hold by
2.3
,
[
29
, Lemma 4.9]
and (
2.5
), respectively. By exactness of
\iota_{0}^{w_{0}(M^{\prime})}
and from the definitions of both
I_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M^{\prime}}}(P\cap M^{\prime},\mathfrak{n},Q\cap M^{\prime})
and
CI_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M^{\prime}}}(w_{0}(P\cap M^{\prime}),\iota_{0}^{M}\mathfrak{n},w_{0}(Q\cap M^{\prime}))
, it follows that we have an isomorphism

I_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M^{\prime}}}(P\cap M^{\prime},\mathfrak{n},Q\cap M^{\prime})\cong\iota_{0}^{w_{0}(M^{\prime})}CI_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M^{\prime}}}(w_{0}(P\cap M^{\prime}),\iota_{0}^{M}\mathfrak{n},w_{0}(Q\cap M^{\prime}))

as required. The proof for the right adjoint is completely analogous.
∎

We now show that the analogue of the above holds at the homotopy level as well. For this, we need a couple of preliminary results.

Proposition 3.24
.

Let
{\mathfrak{m}}
be a finite length
{\mathcal{H}}
-module. Then
{\mathfrak{m}}
is
{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}
-acyclic for any standard Levi subgroup
M
of
G
. In particular,
R^{i}{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}(I_{\mathcal{H}}(P,\mathfrak{n},Q))=0
for any
i>0
and any standard supersingular triple
(P,\mathfrak{n},Q)
.

Proof.

Fix a standard Levi subgroup
M
. It was shown in
[
3
, Proposition 3.6(2)]
that for any
{\mathcal{H}}_{M}
-module
\mathfrak{n}
there are isomorphisms

{\operatorname{Ext}}^{j}_{\mathcal{H}}({\operatorname{Coind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}(\mathfrak{n}),{\mathfrak{m}})\cong{\operatorname{Ext}}^{j}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}}(\mathfrak{n},({\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}})^{\prime}({\mathfrak{m}}))

for all
j\geq 0
, where
({\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}})^{\prime}
denotes the right adjoint of parabolic coinduction. By applying
3.7
(iii), we deduce that finite length modules are acyclic for
({\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}})^{\prime}
. Since
({\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}})^{\prime}\cong\iota_{0}^{w_{0}(M)}\circ\mathcal{R}^{G}_{w_{0}(M)}
, cf.
2.3
(i), and
\iota_{0}^{w_{0}(M)}
is an exact equivalence, we get that finite length modules are acyclic for
\mathcal{R}^{G}_{w_{0}(M)}
. As
M
was arbitrary we are done.
∎

Remark 3.25
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
is supersingular and of finite length, and let
\mathfrak{n}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M})
be any module. Then we have isomorphisms

{\operatorname{Ext}}_{{\mathcal{H}}}^{i}(I_{M}^{G}(\mathfrak{n}),{\mathfrak{m}})\cong{\operatorname{Ext}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}}^{i}(\mathfrak{n},{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}({\mathfrak{m}}))=0

for all
i\geq 0
, by applying
3.24
,
3.7
(iii) and using the fact that
{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}({\mathfrak{m}})=0
by
[
2
, Proposition 5.18]
. This strengthens
3.21
in the finite length case.

Lemma 3.26
.

Let
M
be a standard Levi subgroup of
G
and let
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
be
{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}
-acyclic. Then there is an isomorphism
\mathbf{R}{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}({\mathfrak{m}})\cong{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}({\mathfrak{m}})
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M})
.

Proof.

Taking a fibrant replacement of
{\mathfrak{m}}
in the Gorenstein injective model structure, we get a short exact sequence

0\to{\mathfrak{m}}\to Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}}\to\mathfrak{e}\to 0

where
\mathfrak{e}
has finite injective dimension. As both
{\mathfrak{m}}
and
Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}}
are
{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}
-acyclic, cf.
3.7
(ii), so is
\mathfrak{e}
. By applying
3.7
(iii), we then get isomorphisms

{\operatorname{Ext}}^{j}_{\mathcal{H}}(I^{G}_{M}(\mathfrak{n}),\mathfrak{e})\cong{\operatorname{Ext}}^{j}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}}(\mathfrak{n},{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}(\mathfrak{e}))

for all
\mathfrak{n}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M})
and all
j\geq 0
. It then follows that
{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}(\mathfrak{e})
also has finite injective dimension. By acyclicity of
{\mathfrak{m}}
, the sequence

0\to{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}({\mathfrak{m}})\to{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}(Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}})\to{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}(\mathfrak{e})\to 0

is exact. The above therefore implies that the map
{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}({\mathfrak{m}})\to{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}(Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}})
is a weak equivalence. As
\mathbf{R}{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}({\mathfrak{m}})\cong{\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}}(Q_{f}{\mathfrak{m}})
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M})
, we are done.
∎

Proposition 3.27
.

Let
(P,\mathfrak{n},Q)
be a standard supersingular triple for
{\mathcal{H}}
. Then for
P^{\prime}=M^{\prime}N^{\prime}
another standard parabolic subgroup of
G
, we have

{\operatorname{Ho}}(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{M^{\prime}})(I_{\mathcal{H}}(P,\mathfrak{n},Q))\cong\begin{cases}I_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M^{\prime}}}(P\cap M^{\prime},\mathfrak{n},Q\cap M^{\prime})&\text{if $P\subseteq P^{\prime}$ and $\Pi(\mathfrak{n})\subseteq\Pi_{Q}\cup\Pi_{P^{\prime}}$,}\\
0&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

and

\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M^{\prime}}(I_{\mathcal{H}}(P,\mathfrak{n},Q))\cong\begin{cases}I_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M^{\prime}}}(P\cap M^{\prime},\mathfrak{n},Q\cap M^{\prime})&\text{if $Q\subseteq P^{\prime}$,}\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proof.

The statement for the left adjoints follows immediately from
3.23
. The statement for the right adjoints follows additionally from
3.24
and
3.26
.
∎

It was shown in
[
19
, Theorem 8.5]
that, when
p\nmid\left|\Omega_{\text{tor}}\right|
, a simple module
I_{\mathcal{H}}(P,\mathfrak{n},Q)
has finite projective dimension if and only if
\mathfrak{n}
has finite projective dimension. We now give a new proof of the ‘only if’ direction without any assumptions on the prime
p
and without using any Bruhat-Tits theory. This result is not new, in that the proof of the equivalence in
loc. cit.
only uses their assumption on
p
in the ‘if’ direction, although they never state this explicitly. Using their restriction on
p
, we can also essentially reduce our aim of studying the isomorphism classes of simple modules in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
to the supersingular case.

Corollary 3.28
.

Let
{\mathfrak{m}}=I_{\mathcal{H}}(P,\mathfrak{n},Q)
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}=I_{\mathcal{H}}(P^{\prime},\mathfrak{n}^{\prime},Q^{\prime})
be two simple
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules and write
P=MN
for the Levi decomposition of
P
.

(i)

Assume
{\mathfrak{m}}
has finite projective dimension. Then
\mathfrak{n}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M})
has finite projective dimension.

(ii)

Assume that
p\nmid\left|\Omega_{\text{tor}}\right|
and that
{\operatorname{pd}}_{{\mathcal{H}}}({\mathfrak{m}})={\operatorname{pd}}_{{\mathcal{H}}}({\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})=\infty
. If
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
then
P=P^{\prime}
,
Q=Q^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{n}\cong\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M})
.

Proof.

(i) Let
L
denote the Levi subgroup of
Q
. Then we have
0=\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{L}(I_{\mathcal{H}}(P,\mathfrak{n},Q))\cong I_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}(P\cap L,\mathfrak{n},L)
by
3.27
, so that we may assume that
Q=G
. Applying the left adjoint instead, we now have

\mathfrak{n}\cong I_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}}(M,\mathfrak{n},M)\cong{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{L}^{G}_{M}})(I_{\mathcal{H}}(P,\mathfrak{n},G))=0

in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M})
, as required.

(ii) First suppose that
Q\neq Q^{\prime}
. Up to swapping them we may assume that
Q\nsubseteq Q^{\prime}
. Write
L^{\prime}
for the Levi subgroup of
Q^{\prime}
. Then applying
\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M^{\prime}}
to
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
, we obtain
0\cong I_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L^{\prime}}}(P^{\prime}\cap L^{\prime},\mathfrak{n}^{\prime},L^{\prime})
by
3.27
, contradicting our assumption that
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
has infinite projective dimension by
[
19
, Theorem 8.5]
. Thus
Q=Q^{\prime}
, and applying
\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{L^{\prime}}
again we may assume that
Q=Q^{\prime}=G
as above. Similarly now, if
P\neq P^{\prime}
then without loss of generality we have
P\nsubseteq P^{\prime}
and, writing
M^{\prime}
for the Levi subgroup of
P^{\prime}
, we then obtain a contradiction from
3.27
by applying
{\operatorname{Ho}}(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{M^{\prime}})
.
∎

Going back to our recollement, we write ‘all’ instead of ‘
{\mathcal{M}}
’ in the subscripts of all the functors and categories featuring in
3.13
(i) when the collection
{\mathcal{M}}
of standard Levi subgroups considered is that of
all
proper standard Levi subgroups. We now characterise precisely which simple modules of infinite projective dimension lie in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\text{all}}
, viewed as a full subcategory of
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
via either
\ell^{G}_{\text{all}}
or
r^{G}_{\text{all}}
.

Corollary 3.29
.

Suppose that
p\nmid\left|\Omega_{\text{tor}}\right|
, and let
\mathfrak{s}=I_{\mathcal{H}}(P,\mathfrak{n},Q)\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
be a simple module of infinite projective dimension. Then we have:

(i)

\mathfrak{s}\in{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{\text{\emph{all}}})
if and only if
\Pi=\Pi(\mathfrak{n})
and
P=Q
; and

(ii)

\mathfrak{s}\in{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{\text{\emph{all}}})
if and only if
Q=P(\mathfrak{n})=G
.

In particular,
\mathfrak{s}\in{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{\text{\emph{all}}})\cap{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{\text{\emph{all}}})
if and only if it is supersingular.

Proof.

First observe that for any standard parabolic subgroup
P^{\prime}=M^{\prime}N^{\prime}
of
G
, we have that
I_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M^{\prime}}}(P,\mathfrak{n},Q\cap P^{\prime})
and
I_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M^{\prime}}}(P,\mathfrak{n},Q)
also have infinite projective dimension, whenever these modules are well-defined. Indeed, this follows from
[
19
, Theorem 8.5]
by our assumption on
p
and because
\mathfrak{s}
is assumed to have infinite projective dimension. The statement of (ii) then follows from
3.27
and
3.13
(iii), and for (i) we get

(3.8)

\mathfrak{s}\in{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{\text{all}})\iff\text{ for all }P\subseteq P^{\prime}\subsetneqq G,\;\Pi(\mathfrak{n})\nsubseteq\Pi_{Q}\cup\Pi_{P^{\prime}}.

So we have to check that the right hand side is equivalent to
\Pi=\Pi(\mathfrak{n})
and
P=Q
. One direction is clear. For the other, note that if
\Pi\neq\Pi(\mathfrak{n})
then
P^{\prime}=P(\mathfrak{n})
makes the right hand side of (
3.8
) fail. Moreover, if
\Pi=\Pi(\mathfrak{n})
and
P\neq Q
then note that when
Q=G
the right hand side of (
3.8
) fails for all choices of
P^{\prime}
. So assume that
\Pi=\Pi(\mathfrak{n})
and
P\subsetneqq Q\subsetneqq G
, in which case we let
P^{\prime}
be the standard parabolic corresponding to
\Pi_{P}\cup(\Pi\setminus\Pi_{Q})\subsetneqq\Pi
. Once again
P^{\prime}
makes the right hand side of (
3.8
) fail. This completes the proof of (i).
∎

Remark 3.30
.

The corresponding result at the abelian level, without any assumption on the projective dimension, is also true with the same proof, using
3.23
instead of its homotopy variant. Hence a simple module, or any finite length module more generally, is supersingular if and only if it is killed by the functors
\mathcal{L}^{G}_{M}
and
\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M}
for every proper standard Levi subgroup
M
.

Assuming that the root system of
G
is irreducible, we can extend the above to all modules of finite length.

Proposition 3.31
.

Suppose that
p\nmid\left|\Omega_{\text{tor}}\right|
. When the root system of
G
is irreducible, the following are equivalent for an
{\mathcal{H}}
-module
{\mathfrak{m}}
of finite length:

(i)

{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{\text{\emph{all}}})
;

(ii)

{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{\text{\emph{all}}})
; and

(iii)

the inclusion
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{ss}}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}
is a weak equivalence, where
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{ss}}
denotes the largest supersingular submodule of
{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Proof.

From
3.18
, both (iii)
\Rightarrow
(i) and (iii)
\Rightarrow
(ii) are clear. For the converse, fix an
{\mathcal{H}}
-module
{\mathfrak{m}}
of finite length and let
M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}
denote all the proper Levi subgroups of
G
. Note that
{\mathfrak{m}}
is finite dimensional over
k
, cf.
[
25
, Lemma 6.9]
. Let
\epsilon\in\{-,+\}
. For each
1\leq i\leq n
, we let
\lambda_{i}^{\epsilon}\in\widetilde{\Lambda}
be chosen as in
2.1
for the Levi subgroup
M_{i}
and we write
\mathcal{O}^{\epsilon}_{i}=\widetilde{W}\cdot\lambda_{i}^{\epsilon}
and
z_{i}^{\epsilon}:=z_{\mathcal{O}^{\epsilon}_{i}}
. Then let
{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}^{\epsilon}=\{x\in{\mathfrak{m}}\mid(z^{\epsilon}_{i})^{l}x=0\text{ for some $l>0$}\}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{\leq i}:=\bigcap_{j\leq i}{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{j}=({\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{\leq i-1})^{\epsilon}_{i}
. Note that
z^{\epsilon}_{i}
then acts invertibly on the quotient
{\mathfrak{m}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{i}
for any
i
, so that any simple subquotient of
{\mathfrak{m}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{\epsilon}_{i}
is not supersingular. Hence we see iteratively that any simple subquotient of
{\mathfrak{m}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{+}_{\leq n}
is not supersingular and similarly that any simple subquotient of
{\mathfrak{m}}^{+}_{\leq n}/({\mathfrak{m}}^{+}_{\leq n})^{-}_{\leq n}
is not supersingular. But we have that
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{ss}}=({\mathfrak{m}}^{+}_{\leq n})^{-}_{\leq n}
by
3.30
and
3.19
. Thus we see that every simple subquotient of
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{non-ss}}:={\mathfrak{m}}/{\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{ss}}
is not supersingular. We will show that either (i) or (ii) implies that
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{non-ss}}
has finite projective dimension, and thus that (iii) holds by
[
15
, Lemma 5.8]
.

Suppose first that
\mathfrak{s}=I_{\mathcal{H}}(P,\mathfrak{n},Q)
is a simple subquotient of
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{non-ss}}
. If
P(\mathfrak{n})\neq G
then
\mathfrak{s}
is in the essential image of
I^{G}_{M(\mathfrak{n})}
. If instead
P(\mathfrak{n})=G
then
P=B
since
P\neq G
and the root system is irreducible, and thus
\mathfrak{s}
has finite projective dimension by
[
19
, Theorem 8.5]
and the fact that
{\mathcal{H}}_{T}
has finite global dimension (cf.
3.6
). Either way, it follows that
\mathfrak{s}\in{\mathcal{S}}^{G}_{\text{all}}
and hence that
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{non-ss}}\in{\mathcal{S}}^{G}_{\text{all}}
.

Next, by applying the functors
\mathcal{L}^{G}_{\text{all}}
and
\mathcal{R}^{G}_{\text{all}}
to the distinguished triangle
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{ss}}\to{\mathfrak{m}}\to{\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{non-ss}}\to\Sigma{\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{ss}}
, we obtain from the above and
3.18
that

(3.9)

\mathcal{R}^{G}_{\text{all}}({\mathfrak{m}})\cong\mathcal{R}^{G}_{\text{all}}({\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{non-ss}})\text{ and }\mathcal{L}^{G}_{\text{all}}({\mathfrak{m}})\cong\mathcal{L}^{G}_{\text{all}}({\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{non-ss}}).

But since
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{non-ss}}\in{\mathcal{S}}^{G}_{\text{all}}
we see from the recollement in
3.13
that

(3.10)

{\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{non-ss}}\in{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{\text{all}})\iff{\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{non-ss}}\in{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{\text{all}})\iff{\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{non-ss}}=0,

where the latter equality is in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
. Thus, combining (
3.9
) and (
3.10
), we obtain that either one of (i) or (ii) implies that
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\text{non-ss}}
has finite projective dimension as claimed.
∎

When the hypotheses of
3.31
are satisfied we tentatively call the quotient category
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\text{all}}
the
supersingular homotopy category
of
{\mathcal{H}}
. We will see later that this triangulated category is in fact the homotopy category of a model structure on
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
.

Remark 3.32
.

If we write
{\mathcal{T}}
for
{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{\text{all}})
, then
3.31
tells us that the modules of finite length in
{\mathcal{T}}
are, up to weak equivalence, the supersingular ones whenever the root system of
G
is irreducible and
p\nmid\left|\Omega_{\text{tor}}\right|
. Moreover we have
{\mathcal{T}}^{\ddagger}={\mathcal{S}}^{G}_{\text{all}}
and
{\mathcal{T}}={{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{S}}^{G}_{\text{all}}
. This is similar to the torsion pair constructed at the abelian level in
[
26
]
where the authors showed that when
\mathbb{G}=\text{SL}_{2}
,
p>2
and
\mathfrak{F}\neq\mathbb{Q}_{p}
