, then the supersingular modules are in a torsion pair with a certain class of modules which they call ‘non-supersingular’ and which contains all parabolically induced modules, cf. Theorems 3.26 & 3.27 in
loc. cit
. Since our Hom-orthogonality boils down to Ext-orthogonality between suitable replacements at the abelian level, the two questions are not immediately related. But we hope to study elsewhere the relationship between the Ollivier-Schneider torsion pair and the adjoints of parabolic induction.

4.
Bousfield localisations from recollements

4.1.
Constructing model structures from a recollement

It is natural to ask whether the various quotient categories
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{M}}
that were constructed in the last section are actually the homotopy category of some model structure on
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
. To answer this we work in as general a framework as we can.

Setup 4.1
.

We let
{\mathcal{A}}
be a Grothendieck abelian category equipped with a cofibrantly generated, abelian model structure
({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{A}})
. We will work in one of the following two situations:

(i)

the model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
is injective, i.e.
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{A}}
; or

(ii)

the model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
is projective, i.e.
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{A}}
.

Recall that the model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
is then automatically hereditary and thus stable, and we let
{\mathcal{S}}
be a thick subcategory of
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
. We now assume additionally that in either situation the inclusion
i:{\mathcal{S}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\subseteq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
is part of a recollement

(4.1)

\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{\pi_{\mathcal{S}}}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathsf{L}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathsf{R}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})/{\mathcal{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell_{\mathcal{S}}}
\scriptstyle{r_{\mathcal{S}}}

where none of the functors are a priori assumed to be the total derived functor of some Quillen functors.

We recall that the class of trivially fibrant objects in an injective abelian model category equals the class
\mathrm{Inj}({\mathcal{A}})
of injective objects, and dually the class of trivially cofibrant objects in a projective abelian model category equals the class
\mathrm{Proj}({\mathcal{A}})
projective objects (cf.
[
7
, Corollary 1.1.9]
).

Our aim is to localise the model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
at
{\mathcal{S}}
in a suitable way, or more precisely to construct an abelian model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
whose homotopy category is the quotient
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})/{\mathcal{S}}
. Our main result is:

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be a Grothendieck abelian category equipped with a cofibrantly generated, abelian model structure which is either injective or projective. Suppose that
{\mathcal{S}}
is a thick subcategory of
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
satisfying
4.1
. Let
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
, resp.
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}
, resp.
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}}
, denote the class of objects of
{\mathcal{A}}
whose image in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
lies in
{\mathcal{S}}
, resp.
{\operatorname{Ker}}({\mathsf{R}}_{{\mathcal{S}}})
, resp.
{\operatorname{Ker}}({\mathsf{L}}_{{\mathcal{S}}})
.

(i)

If the model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
is injective then
{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}}=({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}})
defines another cofibrantly generated, injective, abelian model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
such that there is a canonical equivalence
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}})\cong{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})/{\mathcal{S}}
, the adjunction
{\operatorname{id}}:{\mathcal{A}}\rightleftarrows{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}}:{\operatorname{id}}
is Quillen and the induced total derived adjunction is naturally isomorphic to the adjoint pair
\pi_{\mathcal{S}}\dashv r_{\mathcal{S}}
.

(ii)

Dually, if the model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
is projective then
{\mathcal{A}}^{p}_{\mathcal{S}}=({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}})
defines another cofibrantly generated, projective, abelian model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
such that there is a canonical equivalence
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}}^{p}_{\mathcal{S}})\cong{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})/{\mathcal{S}}
, the adjunction
{\operatorname{id}}:{\mathcal{A}}^{p}_{\mathcal{S}}\rightleftarrows{\mathcal{A}}:{\operatorname{id}}
is Quillen and the induced total derived adjunction is naturally isomorphic to the adjoint pair
\ell_{\mathcal{S}}\dashv\pi_{\mathcal{S}}
.

Remark 4.3
.

We note that Becker had a similar aim when he constructed the singular projective/injective model structures on categories of modules over a DG-algebra in
[
7
]
, see in particular Propositions 1.4.2 & 1.4.6 in
loc. cit.
The situation there was more specific as Becker assumed the existence of two model structures on
{\mathcal{A}}
and localised one with respect to the others, although with weaker assumptions on these model structures and on
{\mathcal{A}}
than the ones we have above. Meanwhile, we do not assume that
{\mathcal{S}}
is the homotopy category of some other model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
. In fact, the above result will allow us to apply Becker’s constructions, see
4.7
, and our construction in
4.8
generalises Proposition 2.2.1 in
loc. cit.
, which was only concerned with modules over a DG-algebra.

To prove the theorem, we need to check that the conditions in
2.7
are satisfied. First we have:

Lemma 4.4
.

The classes of objects
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}}
,
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}
and
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
defined in
4.2
are thick.

Proof.

Since distinguished triangles arise from short exact sequences, the result follows immediately from the fact that
{\mathcal{S}}
,
{\operatorname{Ker}}({\mathsf{R}}_{{\mathcal{S}}})
, and
{\operatorname{Ker}}({\mathsf{L}}_{{\mathcal{S}}})
are thick subcategories of the triangulated category
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
.
∎

Next, we characterise the trivially fibrant/cofibrant objects in the proposed new model structures.

Lemma 4.5
.

Suppose that we are in
4.1
. When
{\mathcal{A}}
is injective then
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}={\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{A}}
, so that
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}={\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}=\mathrm{Inj}({\mathcal{A}})
. Dually, when
{\mathcal{A}}
is projective then
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}}={\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{A}}
, so that
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}}={\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}=\mathrm{Proj}({\mathcal{A}})
.

Proof.

We only prove the injective case, the other one being dual. Note that
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}=\mathrm{Inj}({\mathcal{A}})
is immediate from the injectivity of the model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
, so that the first equality immediately implies the second. For the first equality, we have
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{A}}\subseteq{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}
by definition of the right hand side. Conversely, assume that
X\in{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}
. Then
X\cong i(Y)
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
, for some
Y\in{\mathcal{S}}
, and
X\in{\operatorname{Ker}}({\mathsf{R}}_{{\mathcal{S}}})
. But then we obtain from
2.4
that
Y=0
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
, and hence that
X\in{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{A}}
as required.
∎

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be a Grothendieck abelian category. Recall that a cotorsion pair
({\mathcal{D}},{\mathcal{E}})
on
{\mathcal{A}}
is said to be
cogenerated
by a set
\mathcal{G}\subseteq{\mathcal{D}}
if
{\mathcal{E}}=\mathcal{G}^{\perp}
. Furthermore, by definition
{\mathcal{A}}
has a generating set
\mathscr{S}
(or equivalently a generator), which is a set of objects of
{\mathcal{A}}
such that every object of
{\mathcal{A}}
is a quotient of a direct sum of objects in
\mathscr{S}
. The cotorsion pair
({\mathcal{D}},{\mathcal{E}})
is called
small
if it is cogenerated by a set and if
{\mathcal{D}}
contains a generating set for
{\mathcal{A}}
. We note that small cotorsion pairs are automatically functorially complete and that an abelian model structure
({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{W}})
on
{\mathcal{A}}
is cofibrantly generated if and only if both
({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{F}}\cap{\mathcal{W}})
and
({\mathcal{C}}\cap{\mathcal{W}},{\mathcal{F}})
are small, cf.
[
15
, Corollary 6.6]
and
[
7
, Proposition 1.2.7]
.

We will also need a notion of generating set for a triangulated category
{\mathcal{T}}
. A set of objects
\mathcal{G}
of
{\mathcal{T}}
will be called a
set of weak generators
if an object
X\in{\mathcal{T}}
is zero if and only if
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{T}}(G,\Sigma^{n}X)=0
for all
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and all
G\in\mathcal{G}
. The relevance of this notion for us is the result
[
14
, Theorem 7.3.1]
, which in particular implies that the homotopy category
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
of a hereditary, abelian, cofibrantly generated model category
{\mathcal{A}}
has a set of weak generators given by the cokernels of the generating cofibrations. The proof of
loc. cit.
requires some input from the homotopy theory of simplicial sets, so we deemed it worthwhile to provide a more elementary proof in the case where
{\mathcal{A}}
is the module category of a Gorenstein ring. In fact we obtain a stronger result than weak generation.

Proposition 4.6
.

If
S
is a Gorenstein ring, then a module
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
has infinite projective dimension if and only if there exists a right ideal
J
such that
[S/J,{\mathfrak{m}}]_{S}\neq 0
. In particular, the cyclic modules
\{S/J\mid J\text{ a right ideal of }S\}
form a set of weak generators for
{\operatorname{Ho}}(S)
.

Proof.

We view
{\operatorname{Ho}}(S)
as the homotopy category of the Gorenstein injective model structure. One direction is clear, so suppose that
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
has the property that
[S/J,{\mathfrak{m}}]_{S}=0
for all right ideals
J
. Taking a fibrant replacement if necessary, we may assume
{\mathfrak{m}}
to be Gorenstein injective. We first claim that if
\mathfrak{n}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(S)
is finitely generated then
[\mathfrak{n},{\mathfrak{m}}]_{S}=0
. This follows by induction on the number
l
of generators of
\mathfrak{n}
. Indeed,
\mathfrak{n}
fits into a short exact sequence
0\to\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{n}\to\mathfrak{n}^{\prime\prime}\to 0
where
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}
is generated by
l-1
elements and
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime\prime}
is cyclic, and we may view this exact sequence as a distinguished triangle in
{\operatorname{Ho}}(S)
and apply the cohomological functor
[-,{\mathfrak{m}}]_{S}
to get the claim.

We now claim that
{\mathfrak{m}}
is an injective module. Indeed, if
J
is any right ideal of
S
and
f:J\to{\mathfrak{m}}
is any map, then we must show that
f
extends to
S
. But the Noetherianity of
S
implies that
J
is finitely generated and thus, by the above,
f
must be zero in
{\operatorname{Ho}}(S)
. This implies that
f
factors through a map
g:J\to\mathfrak{e}
for some injective module
\mathfrak{e}
by
2.14
. Since
\mathfrak{e}
is injective it follows that
g
extends to
S
, and thus so does
f
as required.
∎

Proof of
4.2
.

We first note that
4.5
implies that
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}})=({\mathcal{A}},\mathrm{Inj}({\mathcal{A}}))
in case (i) and
({\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{A}})=(\mathrm{Proj}({\mathcal{A}}),{\mathcal{A}})
in case (ii) are small cotorsion pairs, cf.
[
7
, Example 1.2.6]
. Since
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
is thick by
4.4
, we are only left to show that
({\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}})
and
({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}})
are small cotorsion pairs in order to get our claimed model structures (cf.
2.7
and the discussion preceding
4.6
).

(i) We need to construct a set
\mathcal{G}
of objects of
{\mathcal{A}}
such that
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}=\mathcal{G}^{\perp}
and
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}={{}^{\perp}}(\mathcal{G}^{\perp})
. By assumption on
{\mathcal{A}}
, we have
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}=\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\perp}
for some set
\mathcal{G}_{1}
of objects in
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{A}}
. By enlarging
\mathcal{G}_{1}
if necessary, we may assume that it contains a generating set for
{\mathcal{A}}
, since
({\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}})
is small. We now let
\mathscr{S}
be a set of weak generators for
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
and, for each
S\in\mathscr{S}
and each
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we choose
X_{n,S}\in{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
X_{n,S}\cong\Sigma^{-n}i{\mathsf{L}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}(S)
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
and let
\mathcal{G}_{2}=\{X_{n,S}\mid S\in\mathscr{S}\text{ and }n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\}
. Then we claim that
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{G}_{1}\cup\mathcal{G}_{2}
satisfies our requirements. To see this, first note that if
Y\in\mathcal{G}^{\perp}
then in particular
Y\in\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\perp}={\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}
. So in order to obtain
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}=\mathcal{G}^{\perp}
it suffices to show that for
Y\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}
, we have
Y\in\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\perp}
if and only if
Y\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}
. Indeed, by
2.16
we have

\displaystyle Y\in\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\perp}

\displaystyle\iff{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})}(\Sigma^{-n}i{\mathsf{L}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}(S),\Sigma Y)=0\text{ for all }n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\text{ and }S\in\mathscr{S}

\displaystyle\iff{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})}(S,\Sigma^{n+1}i{\mathsf{R}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}(Y))=0\text{ for all }n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\text{ and }S\in\mathscr{S}

\displaystyle\iff i{\mathsf{R}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}(Y)=0\iff Y\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}

as required. Next, note that every object of
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}
is isomorphic in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
to an object of
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}
. Thus we see by applying
2.16
again that an object
X\in{\mathcal{A}}
lies in
{}^{\perp}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}})
if and only if the image of
X
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
lies in
{}^{\ddagger}{\operatorname{Ker}}({\mathsf{R}}_{{\mathcal{S}}})={\mathcal{S}}
.

This shows that
({\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}})
is a small cotorsion pair, and thus we have that
{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}}
defines an injective, cofibrantly generated, abelian model structure as required. By
4.5
we have that the trivial fibrations in
{\mathcal{A}}
and in
{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}}
are the same, and thus
{\operatorname{id}}:{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
is right Quillen. Furthermore, since the trivially fibrant objects in both model structures are the same we have that
\mathbf{R}{\operatorname{id}}:{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}})\to{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
identifies, after restricting to the cofibrant-fibrant objects, with the canonical embedding
({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}})/\mathrm{Inj}({\mathcal{A}})\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}/\mathrm{Inj}({\mathcal{A}})
with image
{\operatorname{Ker}}({\mathsf{R}}_{{\mathcal{S}}})\simeq{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})/{\mathcal{S}}
, where the latter equivalence is induced by
r_{\mathcal{S}}
. Thus, after these identifications, the left adjoint of
\mathbf{R}{\operatorname{id}}
is naturally isomorphic to
\pi_{\mathcal{S}}
.

(ii) As above, we let
\mathscr{S}
be a set of weak generators for
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
. Then for each
S\in\mathscr{S}
and each
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we choose
X_{n,S}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
X_{n,S}\cong\Sigma^{-n}\ell_{\mathcal{S}}\pi_{\mathcal{S}}(S)
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
and let
\mathcal{G}=\{X_{n,S}\mid S\in\mathscr{S}\text{ and }n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\}
. Since the model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
is projective, there is a projective generator
Z
for
{\mathcal{A}}
and we may apply
[
15
, Corollary 6.9]
to see that
\mathcal{G}
cogenerates a small cotorsion pair
({\mathcal{D}},{\mathcal{E}})
, where
{\mathcal{D}}
is the closure of
\mathcal{G}\cup\{Z\}
under direct summands and certain colimits called transfinite compositions. As
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}
is closed under those (cf. Lemma 6.2 in
loc. cit.
), it follows that
{\mathcal{D}}\subseteq{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}
. We first show that
{\mathcal{E}}=\mathcal{G}^{\perp}={\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
. This is similar to the argument in (i). As every object of
\mathcal{G}
is cofibrant, we may apply
2.16
to see that

\displaystyle Y\in\mathcal{G}^{\perp}

\displaystyle\iff{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})}(S,\Sigma^{n+1}r_{\mathcal{S}}\pi_{\mathcal{S}}(Y))=0\text{ for all }n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\text{ and }S\in\mathscr{S}

\displaystyle\iff r_{\mathcal{S}}\pi_{\mathcal{S}}(Y)=0\iff Y\in{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}

as required, where the last equivalence holds because
{\operatorname{Ker}}(\pi_{\mathcal{S}})={\mathcal{S}}
. Next, since we have
{\mathcal{D}}\subseteq{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}
it suffices to show that a cofibrant
X\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}
lies in
{}^{\perp}{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
if and only if it lies in
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}}
, in order to obtain that
{\mathcal{D}}={\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}}
. This is now analogous to (i): we have by
2.16
that
X\in{{}^{\perp}}{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
if and only if the image of
X
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
lies in
{}^{\ddagger}{\mathcal{S}}={\operatorname{Ker}}({\mathsf{L}}_{{\mathcal{S}}})
, i.e. if and only if
X\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}}
, as required. This concludes the proof that the abelian model structure
{\mathcal{A}}^{p}_{\mathcal{S}}
exists and is cofibrantly generated. The last part is completely dual to (i).
∎

The construction of these model structures now gives us the input necessary to apply the techniques of Becker in
[
7
]
. We then obtain the following result.

Corollary 4.7
.

We keep the same assumptions as in
4.2
.

(i)

If the model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
is injective, then
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\mathcal{S}}=({\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}})
defines a hereditary, cofibrantly generated, abelian model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
whose homotopy category is canonically equivalent to
{\mathcal{S}}
. Furthermore, the adjunction
{\operatorname{id}}:{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\mathcal{S}}\rightleftarrows{\mathcal{A}}:{\operatorname{id}}
is Quillen and its total derived adjunction is naturally isomorphic to the adjoint pair
(i,{\mathsf{R}}_{{\mathcal{S}}})
.

(ii)

If the model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
is projective, then
{\mathcal{A}}_{p}^{\mathcal{S}}=({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}})
defines a hereditary, cofibrantly generated, abelian model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
whose homotopy category is canonically equivalent to
{\mathcal{S}}
. Furthermore, the adjunction
{\operatorname{id}}:{\mathcal{A}}\rightleftarrows{\mathcal{A}}_{p}^{\mathcal{S}}:{\operatorname{id}}
is Quillen and its total derived adjunction is naturally isomorphic to the adjoint pair
({\mathsf{L}}_{{\mathcal{S}}},i)
.

Proof.

Everything follows immediately from
4.2
and
[
7
, Propositions 1.4.2 & 1.4.6]
, except the cofibrant generation for which we need to check that the associated cotorsion pairs are small. In the injective case we have
({\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}})=({\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}})
and in the projective case we have
({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}})=({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{A}})
, cf.
4.5
, and so it follows from our assumptions that these are small. Furthermore, the proof of
4.2
shows that
({\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}})
, in the injective case, and
({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}})
, in the projective case, are small as well.
∎

We would like to be able to recover the entire recollement in one go, while the above only gives us one half of it in either situation. Furthermore, the model structures constructed in
4.7
are no longer injective/projective. We now see how to fix this by adding one extra hypothesis.

Proposition 4.8
.

Suppose that
{\mathcal{A}}
is a Grothendieck abelian category equipped with both an injective model structure
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{A}})
and a projective model structure
({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{A}})
with the same classes of trivial objects, and assume that these are both cofibrantly generated. Let
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
denote their common homotopy category and assume
{\mathcal{S}}
is a thick subcategory of
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
satisfying the requirements of
4.1
. Let
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
,
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}}
be as in
4.2
.

(i)

{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}}=({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}})
defines an injective, cofibrantly generated, abelian model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
. Furthermore, the identity
{\operatorname{id}}:{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\mathcal{S}}
is a right Quillen equivalence, the adjunction
{\operatorname{id}}:{\mathcal{A}}\rightleftarrows{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}}:{\operatorname{id}}
is Quillen (for the injective model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
) and its total derived adjunction is naturally isomorphic to the adjoint pair
({\mathsf{L}}_{{\mathcal{S}}},i)
.

(ii)

{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{p}_{\mathcal{S}}=({\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}})
defines a projective, cofibrantly generated, abelian model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
. Furthermore, the identity
{\operatorname{id}}:{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{p}_{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{p}^{\mathcal{S}}
is a left Quillen equivalence, the adjunction
{\operatorname{id}}:{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{p}_{\mathcal{S}}\rightleftarrows{\mathcal{A}}:{\operatorname{id}}
is Quillen (for the projective model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
) and its total derived adjunction is naturally isomorphic to the adjoint pair
(i,{\mathsf{R}}_{{\mathcal{S}}})
.

Finally, the identity
{\operatorname{id}}:{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}}\to{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{p}_{\mathcal{S}}
is a right Quillen equivalence.

Proof.

(i) By
4.5
, the pair
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}})=({\mathcal{A}},\mathrm{Inj}({\mathcal{A}}))
is a small cotorsion pair. Furthermore, by assumption and by
4.7
(i) we have that both
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}
are of the form
\mathcal{G}^{\perp}
, hence so is their intersection. Thus we only need to show that
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}}={{}^{\perp}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}})
to get a small cotorsion pair. Since every object of
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
is isomorphic in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
to an object of
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}
and since those are fibrant in
{\mathcal{A}}
, we may apply
2.16
to deduce that an object lies in
{{}^{\perp}}({\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}})
if and only if its image in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
lies in
{}^{\ddagger}{\mathcal{S}}={\operatorname{Ker}}({\mathsf{L}}_{{\mathcal{S}}})
as required.

The functors
{\operatorname{id}}:{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\mathcal{S}}
and
{\operatorname{id}}:{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
both clearly preserve fibrant and trivially fibrant objects so are right Quillen. In fact, since the trivially fibrant objects in
{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\mathcal{S}}
and
{\mathcal{A}}
are the same one gets, after restricting to cofibrant-fibrant objects, that
\mathbf{R}{\operatorname{id}}:{\operatorname{Ho}}({{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}})\to{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\mathcal{S}})
identifies with the identity functor on
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}/\mathrm{Inj({\mathcal{A}})}
and
\mathbf{R}{\operatorname{id}}:{\operatorname{Ho}}({{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}})\to{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
identifies with the canonical inclusion
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}/\mathrm{Inj({\mathcal{A}})}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}/\mathrm{Inj({\mathcal{A}})}
, as required.

(ii) Again, by
4.5
the pair
({\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{A}})=(\mathrm{Proj}({\mathcal{A}}),{\mathcal{A}})
is a small cotorsion pair. Furthermore, by
4.7
(i) there exists a set
\mathcal{G}\subseteq{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
such that
\mathcal{G}^{\perp}={\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}
. Now define a set
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\subseteq{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}
by letting the objects of
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
be a choice of cofibrant replacements in
{\mathcal{A}}
for the objects of
\mathcal{G}
. Since these are cofibrant, we may apply
2.16
again to deduce that
(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})^{\perp}={\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}
. Furthermore, the pair
({{}^{\perp}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}})
is now a small cotorsion pair, such that
{{}^{\perp}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}
is the closure of
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\cup\{Z\}
under transfinite compositions and direct summands by
[
15
, Lemma 6.9]
, where
Z
is a choice of projective generator for
{\mathcal{A}}
. Since both
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}
are closed under those by Lemma 6.2 in
loc. cit.
and
4.7
(i), we have
{{}^{\perp}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}\subseteq{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}
. To show equality it therefore suffices to show that an object of
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}
lies in
{{}^{\perp}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}
if and only if it lies in
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
. This is proved via the usual argument using
2.16
. The statements on the Quillen functors are proved dually to (i).

Finally,
{\operatorname{id}}:{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}}\to{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{p}_{\mathcal{S}}
will by definition be a right Quillen equivalence if, for
X\in{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
Y\in{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}}
, a map
f:X\to Y
is a weak equivalence in
{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{p}_{\mathcal{S}}
if and only if it is a weak equivalence in
{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}}
. Note that the trivial cofibrations in
{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{p}_{\mathcal{S}}
are precisely the monomorphisms with projective cokernel, and so are still weak equivalences in
{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}}
. Thus, by factorising
f
in
{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{p}_{\mathcal{S}}
as a trivial cofibration followed by a fibration we may assume that
f
is a fibration. Then it is a weak equivalence in
{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{p}_{\mathcal{S}}
if and only if its kernel lies in
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}
. But since both
X
and
Y
lie in
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
by assumption and
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
is thick, this happens if and only if
\ker(f)\in{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}={\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{A}}
, cf.
4.5
. Similarly,
f
is a weak equivalence in
{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}}
if and only if
\ker(f)\in{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}}={\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{A}}
.
∎

By applying Becker’s construction
[
7
, Propositions 1.4.2 & 1.4.6]
to
{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{i}_{\mathcal{S}}
and
{{}^{\ddagger}}{\mathcal{A}}^{p}_{\mathcal{S}}
, we obtain the last piece of the recollement.

Corollary 4.9
.

Keep the same assumptions as in
4.8
. Then the triple
({\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\mathcal{S}})^{\ddagger}=({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}})
defines a hereditary, cofibrantly generated, abelian model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
whose homotopy category is canonically equivalent to
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})/{\mathcal{S}}
, and the adjunction
{\operatorname{id}}:({\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\mathcal{S}})^{\ddagger}\rightleftarrows{\mathcal{A}}:{\operatorname{id}}
is Quillen (for the injective model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
) with total derived adjunction naturally isomorphic to
(\ell_{\mathcal{S}},\pi_{\mathcal{S}})
. Dually, the triple
({\mathcal{A}}_{p}^{\mathcal{S}})^{\ddagger}=({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}})
defines a hereditary, cofibrantly generated, abelian model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
whose homotopy category is canonically equivalent to
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})/{\mathcal{S}}
, and the adjunction
{\operatorname{id}}:{\mathcal{A}}\rightleftarrows({\mathcal{A}}_{p}^{\mathcal{S}})^{\ddagger}:{\operatorname{id}}
is Quillen (for the projective model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
) with total derived adjunction naturally isomorphic to
(\pi_{\mathcal{S}},r_{\mathcal{S}})
.

Hence, sticking with the projective model structures,
4.2
(ii),
4.7
(ii),
4.8
(ii) and
4.9
together say that if we are in
4.1
in such a way that both an injective and a projective model structure exist in
{\mathcal{A}}
with the same class of trivial objects, then by using only the projective model structure we may construct four new abelian model structures on
{\mathcal{A}}
such that the four adjunctions from the recollement (
4.1
) are obtained as total derived functors of the various identity adjunctions between our new model structures and the projective model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
.

In particular, by considering
{\mathcal{A}}={\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
with its Gorenstein projective and injective model structures and by letting
{\mathcal{M}}=\{M_{1},\ldots,M_{n}\}
be some fixed collection of standard Levi subgroups of
G
, the above tells us that the recollement in
3.13
(i) may be reconstructed from various model structures on
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
by only using the Gorenstein projective model structure. If we define

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})\mid{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\mathcal{S}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}\right\},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}:

\displaystyle=\left\{{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})\mid{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}})\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})\mid{\operatorname{pd}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M_{i}}}(\mathcal{L}^{G}_{M_{i}}({\mathfrak{m}}))<\infty\text{ for all }1\leq i\leq n\right\},\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}:

\displaystyle=\left\{{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})\mid{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathcal{R}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}})\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})\mid{\mathfrak{m}}\in\bigcap_{1\leq i\leq n}{\operatorname{Ker}}(\mathbf{R}\mathcal{R}^{G}_{M_{i}})\right\},

where the second equalities for
{\mathcal{C}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
both follow from
3.13
(iii), then in particular
4.2
(ii) and
4.8
(ii) say in this case that there are two cofibrantly generated, projective abelian model structures
{{}^{\ddagger}}{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
and
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
with cofibrant objects
{\mathcal{C}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}\cap\mathrm{GProj}({\mathcal{H}})
and
{\mathcal{W}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}\cap\mathrm{GProj}({\mathcal{H}})
, respectively, and trivial objects
{\mathcal{W}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
, respectively, so that
{\operatorname{Ho}}({{}^{\ddagger}}{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})^{G}_{\mathcal{M}})\simeq{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})_{\mathcal{M}}
and
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})^{G}_{\mathcal{M}})\simeq{\mathcal{S}}^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
.

Next, fix a standard parabolic
P=MN
of
G
. Recall that there is an adjunction

F_{M}:{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M})\rightleftarrows{\operatorname{Rep}}_{k}^{\infty}(M):U_{M}

where
F_{M}({\mathfrak{m}}):={\mathbb{X}}_{M}\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}}{\mathfrak{m}}
and
U_{M}(V):={\operatorname{Hom}}_{M}({\mathbb{X}}_{M},V)\cong V^{I_{M}}
for any
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{M})
and
V\in{\operatorname{Rep}}_{k}^{\infty}(M)
. When
M=G
, we drop the subscript and write
F
and
U
for the corresponding functors. On the smooth representations side, the parabolic induction functor
{\operatorname{Ind}}_{P}^{G}:{\operatorname{Rep}}_{k}^{\infty}(M)\to{\operatorname{Rep}}_{k}^{\infty}(G)
is defined by first inflating smooth representations of
M\cong P/N
to
P
and then applying the smooth induction functor. This functor has both a left and right adjoint (cf.
[
31
, Proposition 4.3]
), and its left adjoint can be described explicitly as the Jacquet functor
V\mapsto J_{P}^{G}(V):=V_{N}
where
V_{N}
denotes the
N
-coinvariants of
V
. This construction is compatible with the parabolic induction for
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules by a result of Ollivier-Vignéras.

Theorem 4.10

(
[
27
, Theorem 1]
)
.

Let
M
be a standard Levi subgroup of
G
. Then we have a natural isomorphism of functors
U\circ{\operatorname{Ind}}_{P}^{G}\cong{\operatorname{Ind}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{M}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}\circ U_{M}
.

We note in passing that the above immediately implies the analogue of
3.4
(i) on the smooth representations side.

Corollary 4.11
.

The
I
-Gorenstein projective model structure on
{\operatorname{Rep}}_{k}^{\infty}(M)
is the right transfer of the
I
-Gorenstein projective model structure on
{\operatorname{Rep}}_{k}^{\infty}(G)
along
{\operatorname{Ind}}_{P}^{G}
.

Proof.

We need to show that for a morphism
f
in
{\operatorname{Rep}}_{k}^{\infty}(M)
,
f
is a fibration (resp. weak equivalence) if and only if
{\operatorname{Ind}}_{P}^{G}(f)
is a fibration (resp. weak equivalence). But these notions are by definition completely characterised by passing to pro-
p
Iwahori invariants. So the result follows immediately from
4.10
and
3.4
(i).
∎

We finish by right transferring our new model structures on
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
to
{\operatorname{Rep}}_{k}^{\infty}(G)
.

Proposition 4.12
.

For any collection
{\mathcal{M}}
of standard Levi subgroups of
G
, the right transfer of the model structures
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
and
{{}^{\ddagger}}{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
to
{\operatorname{Rep}}_{k}^{\infty}(G)
along
U
both exist.

Proof.

Since
{\operatorname{id}}:{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})^{\text{GP}}\to{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
and
{\operatorname{id}}:{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})^{\text{GP}}\to{{}^{\ddagger}}{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
are right Quillen, they in particular preserve path objects. Hence path objects in
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
and
{{}^{\ddagger}}{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})^{G}_{\mathcal{M}}
can be computed exactly as in the Gorenstein projective model structure. Therefore, the arguments of
[
9
, Proposition 4.10]
apply mutatis mutandis to yield the result.
∎

It is unclear to us at this stage whether the two other model structures constructed in
4.7
(ii) and
4.9
, applied to
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
, can be right transferred to
{\operatorname{Rep}}_{k}^{\infty}(G)
as well. We note that these model structures are not projective and thus the standard techniques used in
[
9
]
in order to establish the existence of right transfers now require one to construct a fibrant replacement functor as well as path objects for these right transfers. An in-depth study of this question and of the right transfers obtained in
4.12
and their relationship to the derived adjunction
(\mathbf{L}J_{P}^{G},\mathbf{R}{\operatorname{Ind}}_{P}^{G})
will be done elsewhere.

4.2.
Bousfield localisation

We finish this section by explaining how the projective model structures constructed in the previous subsection are Bousfield localisations of the projective model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
. Our approach here is very similar to
[
7
, §1.5]
, and in fact Proposition 1.5.3 in
loc. cit
. shows that this is automatically true for the model structures constructed in
4.7
and
4.9
. We therefore focus on the model structures constructed in
4.2
(ii) and
4.8
(ii). First we recall the notion of Bousfield localisation from
[
13
]
. We only state the definition in the context of pointed model categories to make the presentation simpler, but this notion can be defined for all model categories.

Definition 4.13
.

Let
\mathscr{M}
be a pointed model category and
\mathrm{S}
be a class of morphisms in
\mathscr{M}
.

•

An object
X\in\mathscr{M}
is called an
\mathrm{S}
-colocal object
if it is cofibrant and for every morphism
f:A\to B
in
\mathrm{S}
, the induced map
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{Ho}}(\mathscr{M})}(\Sigma^{n}X,A)\to{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{Ho}}(\mathscr{M})}(\Sigma^{n}X,B)
is a bijection for all
n\geq 0
.

•

A morphism
f:A\to B
in
\mathscr{M}
is called an
\mathrm{S}
-colocal equivalence
if for every
\mathrm{S}
-colocal object
X
, the induced map
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{Ho}}(\mathscr{M})}(\Sigma^{n}X,A)\to{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{Ho}}(\mathscr{M})}(\Sigma^{n}X,B)
is a bijection for all
n\geq 0
.

The notions of
\mathrm{S}
-local object
and
\mathrm{S}
-local equivalence
are defined dually to the above.

We note that (co)local objects and equivalences are usually defined in terms of homotopy function complexes in the literature (cf.
[
13
, Definition 3.1.4]
). The above definition is equivalent to that because for pointed model categories the homotopy groups of these function complexes are precisely the
{\operatorname{Hom}}
sets we considered above, see
[
14
, Lemma 6.1.2]
. With this notion at hand, we have the following definition of Bousfield localisation.

Definition 4.14

(
[
13
, Definition 3.3.1]
)
.

Let
\mathscr{M}
be a pointed model category and
\mathrm{S}
be a class of morphisms in
\mathscr{M}
. The
right Bousfield localisation
of
\mathscr{M}
with respect to
\mathrm{S}
is, if it exists, the model structure
\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{S}}\mathscr{M}
on the same underlying category as
\mathscr{M}
, with fibrations the same as in
\mathscr{M}
, weak equivalences the
\mathrm{S}
-colocal equivalences, and cofibrations the maps satisfying the left lifting property with respect to the trivial fibrations. The notion of
left Bousfield localisation
of
\mathscr{M}
with respect to
\mathrm{S}
is defined dually.

Lemma 4.15
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be a hereditary abelian model category,
X
an object of
{\mathcal{A}}
, and
f
a morphism in
{\mathcal{A}}
.

(i)

In the arrow category of
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
,
f
is isomorphic to a fibration between fibrant objects.

(ii)

If
f
is a fibration between fibrant objects, then
X
is
f
-colocal if and only if

{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})}(\Sigma^{n}X,\ker(f))=0

for all
n\geq 0
.

Proof.

These statements are proved in steps (1)-(3) of the proof of
[
7
, Proposition 1.5.3]
for injective model structures and the proof carries through mutatis mutandis in our more general setting.
∎

Proposition 4.16
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be an abelian category equipped with a projective abelian model structure
({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{W}})
, and let
{\mathcal{S}}
be a thick subcategory of
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
. Let
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
denote the class of objects of
{\mathcal{A}}
whose image in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
lies in
{\mathcal{S}}
.

(i)

Suppose that the inclusion
{\mathcal{S}}\subset{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
has a left adjoint
{\mathsf{L}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}
. Let
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}}
denote the class of objects of
{\mathcal{A}}
whose image in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
lies in
{\operatorname{Ker}}({\mathsf{L}}_{{\mathcal{S}}})
, and assume that
({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}})
defines an abelian model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
. Then this model structure is the right Bousfield localisation of
{\mathcal{A}}
with respect to
\mathrm{S}:=\{0\to X\mid X\in{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}\}
; and

(ii)

Suppose that the inclusion
{\mathcal{S}}\subset{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
has a right adjoint
{\mathsf{R}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}
. Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}
denote the class of objects of
{\mathcal{A}}
whose image in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
lies in
{\operatorname{Ker}}({\mathsf{R}}_{{\mathcal{S}}})
, and assume that
({\mathcal{C}}\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}})
defines an abelian model structure on
{\mathcal{A}}
. Then this model structure is the right Bousfield localisation of
{\mathcal{A}}
with respect to
\mathrm{S}^{\prime}:=\{0\to X\mid X\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{S}}\}
.

Proof.

We only prove (i), the proof of (ii) being entirely analogous. By the dual of
3.10
(i), we have
{}^{\ddagger}{\mathcal{S}}={\operatorname{Ker}}({\mathsf{L}}_{{\mathcal{S}}})
and
{\mathcal{S}}={\operatorname{Ker}}({\mathsf{L}}_{{\mathcal{S}}})^{\ddagger}
. By definition of
\mathrm{S}
, the class of
\mathrm{S}
-colocal objects is that of those cofibrant objects
X
whose image in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
lies in
{}^{\ddagger}{\mathcal{S}}={\operatorname{Ker}}({\mathsf{L}}_{{\mathcal{S}}})
, i.e. the class of
\mathrm{S}
-colocal objects is
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}
. Then
4.15
implies that an
\mathrm{S}
-colocal equivalence is a morphism which, in the arrow category of
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
, is isomorphic to an epimorphism with kernel in
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
.

Now assume that
({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}})
defines an abelian model structure, which we denote by
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{S}}
. Then we must show that the weak equivalences in
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{S}}
are the
\mathrm{S}
-colocal equivalences. For this, note first that
{\operatorname{id}}:{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{S}}
is right Quillen since
{\mathcal{W}}\subseteq{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
, and we get a functor
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{id}}):{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})\to{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{S}})
by
2.8
. Hence one gets that an
\mathrm{S}
-colocal equivalence is also isomorphic to an epimorphism with kernel in
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
in the arrow category of
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{S}})
, and hence is a weak equivalence in
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{S}}
. For the converse, suppose that
f
is a weak equivalence in
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{S}}
. Note first that
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{S}}\cap{\mathcal{C}}\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}=\mathrm{Proj}({\mathcal{A}})={\mathcal{C}}\cap{\mathcal{W}}
, so that the trivial cofibrations in
{\mathcal{A}}
and in
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{S}}
coincide. By factorising
f
in
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{S}}
as a composite of a trivial cofibration and a trivial fibration, we therefore see that
f
is isomorphic in the arrow category of
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{A}})
to a trivial fibration in
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{S}}
, i.e. an epimorphism with kernel in
{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathcal{S}}
, as required.
∎

Corollary 4.17
.

The projective model structures constructed in
4.2
and
4.8
are right Bousfield localisations.

Of course the above has completely dual analogues. In particular, the injective model structures constructed in
4.2
and
4.8
are left Bousfield localisations.

5.
Simple supersingular Hecke modules in the homotopy category

For the remainder of this paper, we assume that
k
is algebraically closed. Our main goal is to study the isomorphism classes of simple
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
. In light of
3.28
(ii), we focus our attention on the case of simple supersingular modules.

5.1.
The affine Hecke algebra and simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules

We first recall some properties of the affine Hecke algebra
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
, the classification of simple characters over it, and the classification of simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules.

Fix the set
\{\hat{s}\mid s\in S\}\subseteq\widetilde{W}_{\operatorname{aff}}
of lifts of the simple affine reflections defined as in
[
25
, §4.8]
. Then
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
is generated as a
k
-algebra by
\{T_{t}\mid t\in T(\mathbb{F}_{q})\}
and
\{T_{\hat{s}}\mid s\in S\}
. The ring structure of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
is determined by the braid relations and

(5.1)

T_{\hat{s}}^{2}=T_{\hat{s}}\cdot\left(\sum_{t\in T_{s}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}T_{t}\right)

for all
s\in S
, cf.
[
25
, §4.8, eq. (4.10)]
. In addition, each irreducible component
\Phi^{\prime}
of
\Phi
gives rise to a subset
S^{\prime}\subseteq S
and a subalgebra of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
generated by
k[T(\mathbb{F}_{q})]
and
\{T_{\hat{s}}\mid s\in S^{\prime}\}
. We will refer to these subalgebras as the irreducible components of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
. We will also need a notion of irreducible components of the finite Hecke algebra
{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
associated to a face
F\subseteq\overline{C}
. First, since
S
is in bijection with the nodes of the affine Dynkin diagram of
G
, we may associate to the set
S_{F}
the subgraph of the affine Dynkin diagram spanned by the corresponding nodes. Each connected component of that subgraph corresponds under the aforementioned bijection to a subset
S_{F^{\prime}}
of
S_{F}
, itself corresponding to a face
F^{\prime}
such that
F\subseteq\overline{F^{\prime}}
by (
2.3
). We call the associated subalgebras
{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}
of
{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
arising in this way the irreducible components of
{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
.

Next, we recall the Ollivier-Schneider resolutions for
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules. For each
0\leq i\leq d=r_{ss}
, we write
\mathscr{F}_{i}
for the set of
i
-dimensional faces
F
of
\mathscr{X}
which are contained in
\overline{C}
. Note that by
[
18
, Lemma 1.2]
this is a set of representatives for the
G_{\operatorname{aff}}
-orbits of the
i
-dimensional faces in
\mathscr{X}
. We also fix for each
i
a set
\mathscr{F}_{(i)}\subseteq\mathscr{F}_{i}
of representatives for the
G
-orbits of the
i
-dimensional faces in
\mathscr{X}
. Then
[
25
, Theorem 3.12]
implies that for every
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
there is an exact sequence of
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules of the form

(5.2)

0\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{F\in\mathscr{F}_{(d)}}{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{F}}({\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{F}}(\epsilon_{F}){\mathcal{H}}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{F\in\mathscr{F}_{(0)}}{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{F}}({\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{F}}(\epsilon_{F}){\mathcal{H}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{m}}\longrightarrow 0,

where we note that our Hecke algebras are opposite to the ones in
loc. cit.
and hence our tensors products are reversed. Here, for a face
F\subseteq\overline{C}
, the twist
(\epsilon_{F}){\mathcal{H}}
by the orientation character
\epsilon_{F}
is the one defined in
loc. cit.
, §3.3.1. We now see that there is a similar resolution for
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
-modules, cf.
[
19
, Remark 4.1]
.

Proposition 5.1
.

There is a resolution of
({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}},{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
-bimodules of the form

(5.3)

0\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{F\in\mathscr{F}_{d}}{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{F\in\mathscr{F}_{0}}{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\longrightarrow 0.

This gives a free resolution of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
as a left and as a right
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
-module.

Proof.

The proof is mostly analogous to that of
[
25
, Theorem 3.12]
, we only sketch how it goes through for
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
instead of
{\mathcal{H}}
. First, recall that to any
V\in{\operatorname{Rep}}_{k}^{\infty}(G)
, we may functorially associate a certain
G
-equivariant coefficient system
\underline{\underline{V}}
as in
[
18
, Example 2.2]
. We refer the reader to §2.1 of
loc. cit.
for the relevant definitions, we only point out that this construction also applies, with the same definitions, to smooth representations of
G_{\operatorname{aff}}
and that there is a functorial oriented chain complex
{\mathcal{C}}^{or}_{c}(\mathscr{X}_{(\bullet)},\underline{\underline{V}})\to V
for
V
a smooth representation of one of
G
,
G_{\operatorname{aff}}
(cf. equations (2.1)-(2.2) of
loc. cit.
). If
{\mathbb{X}}
and
{\mathbb{X}}_{\operatorname{aff}}
are the representations from §2.2, then note that extending functions by zero gives a
G_{\operatorname{aff}}
-equivariant split embedding
{\mathbb{X}}_{\operatorname{aff}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{X}}
(with splitting given by restricting functions to
G_{\operatorname{aff}}
). Thus we see that the complex
0\to{\mathcal{C}}^{or}_{c}(\mathscr{X}_{(\bullet)},\underline{\underline{{\mathbb{X}}_{\operatorname{aff}}}})\to{\mathbb{X}}_{\operatorname{aff}}\to 0
is a
G_{\operatorname{aff}}
-equivariant retract of
0\to{\mathcal{C}}^{or}_{c}(\mathscr{X}_{(\bullet)},\underline{\underline{{\mathbb{X}}}})\to{\mathbb{X}}\to 0
. Since the latter is exact on
I
-invariants by
[
25
, Theorem 3.4]
it follows that the same holds for the former. We are therefore left to show that the
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
-bimodule
{\mathcal{C}}^{or}_{c}(\mathscr{X}_{(i)},\underline{\underline{{\mathbb{X}}_{\operatorname{aff}}}})^{I}
is as given in the statement for each
0\leq i\leq d
.

This is done by arguing exactly as in
[
25
]
. The proof that

{\mathcal{C}}^{or}_{c}(\mathscr{X}_{(i)},\underline{\underline{{\mathbb{X}}_{\operatorname{aff}}}})\cong\bigoplus_{F\in\mathscr{F}_{i}}{\operatorname{ind}}_{P_{F}}^{G_{\operatorname{aff}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\operatorname{aff}}^{I_{F}})

is done exactly as in §3.3.2 of
loc. cit.
We only point out that the orientation character
\epsilon_{F}
doesn’t appear here because each parahoric
P_{F}
fixes
F
pointwise and thus also fixes any chosen orientation on
F
, and that
\mathscr{F}_{i}
is a set of representatives for the
G_{\operatorname{aff}}
-orbits of the
i
-dimensional faces in
\mathscr{X}
. The proof of Proposition 4.25 in
loc. cit.
then adapts to give an isomorphism
{\mathbb{X}}_{F}\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\cong{\mathbb{X}}_{\operatorname{aff}}^{I_{F}}
of
(P_{F},{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
-bimodules. Therefore, one obtains

{\operatorname{ind}}_{P_{F}}^{G_{\operatorname{aff}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\operatorname{aff}}^{I_{F}})^{I}\cong{\operatorname{ind}}_{P_{F}}^{G_{\operatorname{aff}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{F}\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})^{I}\cong{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}

exactly as in Proposition 3.10 of
loc. cit
.
∎

The methods of
[
25
, §1]
then show that
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
is
n
-Gorenstein for some
n\leq d
. Thus the category
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
has a Gorenstein projective model structure and the homotopy category
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
is well-defined. Furthermore, we obtain by tensoring with (
5.3
) an exact resolution

(5.4)

0\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{F\in\mathscr{F}_{d}}{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}({\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{F\in\mathscr{F}_{0}}{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}({\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{m}}\longrightarrow 0

for any
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
.

Recall from
[
32
, Proposition 2.2]
that the characters of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
are parametrized by pairs
(J,\xi)
where
\xi
is a character of
k[T(\mathbb{F}_{q})]
and
J\subseteq S_{\xi}
where
S_{\xi}
is as in (
2.2
). The character
\chi
corresponding to such a pair is defined by

\displaystyle\chi(T_{t})

\displaystyle=\xi(t)\quad\text{for all $t\in T(\mathbb{F}_{q})$}

\displaystyle\chi(T_{\hat{s}})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}0&\text{if $s\notin J$}\\
-1&\text{if $s\in J$}\end{cases}.

We will sometimes write
\chi=(J,\xi)
to mean that
\chi
is defined as above. When
S_{\xi}=S
, the character
\chi
will be called a
twisted trivial character
, resp.
twisted sign character
, if it additionally satisfies
J=\emptyset
, resp.
J=S
. There is a notion of supersingularity for characters of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
, and we have that a character
\chi
is supersingular if and only if its restriction to each irreducible component of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
does not equal the restriction of a twisted sign or twisted trivial character (cf. Theorem 6.15 in
loc. cit
). We will use this result as a definition of supersingularity for characters of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
. By convention, if
G=T
then every character as above is supersingular.

Next, the group
\widetilde{\Omega}
acts on the characters of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
as follows. Given a character
\chi
of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
and
\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}
, we let
\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}(h):=\chi(T_{\tilde{\omega}}hT_{\tilde{\omega}^{-1}})
for
h\in{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
. We denote by
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}
the stabiliser of
\chi
under this action. This is a subgroup of finite index in
\widetilde{\Omega}
, containing
T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. We also let
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}
denote the subalgebra of
{\mathcal{H}}
generated by
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
and
\{T_{\tilde{\omega}}\mid\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}\}
. It follows from the braid relations that
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}
is free over
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
and that
{\mathcal{H}}
is free over
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}
, both as left and right modules. For future use, we record here the following:

Lemma 5.2
.

For any character
\chi
of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
, the algebra
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}
is Gorenstein.

Proof.

Since
{\mathcal{H}}
is free over
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}
, the functor
{\mathcal{H}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}(-)
is exact and preserves projectives. Since
{\mathcal{H}}
is
(r_{ss}+r_{Z})
-Gorenstein it follows that for each
\mathfrak{n}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\chi})
and for any
i>r_{ss}+r_{Z}
we have

{\operatorname{Ext}}^{i}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}(\mathfrak{n},{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}({\mathcal{H}}))\cong{\operatorname{Ext}}^{i}_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathfrak{n}\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}},{\mathcal{H}})=0.

Thus the injective dimension of
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}({\mathcal{H}})
is bounded above by
r_{ss}+r_{Z}
. Since
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}
is a direct summand of
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}({\mathcal{H}})
, it follows that it has finite selfinjective dimension as a right module over itself. An analogous argument shows the corresponding statement for left modules.

It remains to show that
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}
is (left and right) Noetherian. Write
\Omega_{\chi}
for the image of
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}
in
\Omega
. This group decomposes as
\Omega_{\chi}=\Omega_{\chi}^{\text{free}}\times\Omega_{\chi}^{\text{tor}}
where
\Omega_{\chi}^{\text{free}}
is a free abelian group on some generators
\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{r_{Z}}
and
\Omega_{\chi}^{\text{tor}}
is finite. If we let
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\text{free}}
denote the subalgebra of
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}
generated by
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
and all
T_{\hat{\omega}_{i}^{\pm 1}}
, then the braid relations show that
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\text{free}}
is an iterated skew Laurent polynomial algebra over
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
, and hence is Noetherian because
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
is Noetherian (cf.
[
32
, Theorem 5.1]
and
[
21
, Theorem 1.4.5]
). Since
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}
is finite over
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\text{free}}
, it must be Noetherian as well.
∎

We now suppose that
\chi=(J,\xi)
is a supersingular character of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
. Given a finite dimensional, irreducible (right) representation
(V,\rho)
of
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}
on which
T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
acts by the character
\xi
, we let
\chi\otimes V
be the
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}
-module whose underlying vector space is
V
and on which
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
acts by
\chi
and
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}
acts via
\rho
. Then every simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-module is isomorphic to one of the form
(\chi\otimes V)\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}}
, and furthermore two pairs
(\chi,V)
and
(\chi^{\prime},V^{\prime})
give rise to isomorphic simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules if and only if the two pairs are
\widetilde{\Omega}
-conjugate (cf.
[
32
, Theorem 6.18]
). Here we say that
(\chi,V)
and
(\chi^{\prime},V^{\prime})
are
\widetilde{\Omega}
-conjugate if
\chi^{\prime}=\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}
for some
\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}
and
V^{\prime}\cong V^{\tilde{\omega}}
, where
V^{\tilde{\omega}}
is the representation of
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}}=\tilde{\omega}^{-1}\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}\tilde{\omega}
with underlying vector space
V
obtained by letting
v\cdot\tilde{\omega}^{-1}\tilde{\omega}^{\prime}\tilde{\omega}:=v\cdot\tilde{\omega}^{\prime}
for all
v\in V
and
\tilde{\omega}^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}
.

Remark 5.3
.

Suppose that
\tilde{\omega},\tilde{\omega}^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\Omega}
. Since
\Omega
is abelian, we have that
\tilde{\omega}\tilde{\omega}^{\prime}\tilde{\omega}^{-1}=t\tilde{\omega}^{\prime}
for some
t\in T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. In particular, we deduce that every subgroup of
\widetilde{\Omega}
which contains
T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
is automatically normal. This applies in particular to the stabiliser subgroups
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}
as above, so that we have
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}={\mathcal{H}}_{\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}}
for all
\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}
.

We next recall a theorem of Koziol, which describes when simple supersingular modules are trivial in the Gorenstein projective model structure. The statement in
loc. cit.
doesn’t actually contain (ii) as below, but their proof actually shows the statement as written here.

Theorem 5.4

(
[
19
, Theorem 7.7]
)
.

Suppose that
{\mathfrak{m}}:=(\chi\otimes V)\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}}
is a simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-module as above, and let
\chi
be parametrized by
(J,\xi)
. Then the following are equivalent:

(i)

{\operatorname{pd}}_{{\mathcal{H}}}({\mathfrak{m}})<\infty
;

(ii)

{\operatorname{pd}}_{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}(\chi)<\infty
; and

(iii)

\Phi
is of type
A_{1}\times\cdots\times A_{1}
(possibly empty product) and
S_{\xi}=S
.

We now describe
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}({\mathfrak{m}})
, where
{\mathfrak{m}}=(\chi\otimes V)\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}}
is a simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-module. Observe first that for any
\mathfrak{n}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\chi})
we obtain from the braid relations a direct sum decomposition

\mathfrak{n}\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}}=\bigoplus_{\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}\backslash\widetilde{\Omega}}\mathfrak{n}\otimes T_{\tilde{\omega}}.

This is in fact a decomposition as an
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}
-module. Indeed, for all
h\in{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
and all
x\in\mathfrak{n}
we have
(x\otimes T_{\tilde{\omega}})\cdot h=x(T_{\tilde{\omega}}hT_{\tilde{\omega}}^{-1})\otimes T_{\tilde{\omega}}
. Furthermore, the group
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}}=\tilde{\omega}^{-1}\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}\tilde{\omega}
naturally acts on
\mathfrak{n}\otimes T_{\tilde{\omega}}
(cf.
5.3
). Note that these actions do not depend, up to isomorphism, on the choice of coset representatives. When
\mathfrak{n}=\chi\otimes V
, the summand
\mathfrak{n}\otimes T_{\tilde{\omega}}
naturally identifies with
\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}\otimes(V^{\prime})^{\tilde{\omega}}
as an
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}
-module. We therefore get a decomposition

(5.5)

{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}({\mathfrak{m}})\cong\bigoplus_{\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}\backslash\widetilde{\Omega}}\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}\otimes V^{\tilde{\omega}}.

We finish by recalling that to any character
\xi:T(\mathbb{F}_{q})\to k^{\times}
we may attach a central idempotent

(5.6)

e_{\xi}=\left|T(\mathbb{F}_{q})\right|^{-1}\sum_{t\in T(\mathbb{F}_{q})}\xi(t)T_{t^{-1}}\in k[T(\mathbb{F}_{q})].

Then we get that
e_{\xi}T_{\hat{s}}=T_{\hat{s}}e_{s\cdot\xi}
for all
s\in S
, where
s\cdot\xi
denotes the lift to
W
of the
W_{0}
-action on characters of
T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
by conjugation of the argument. If we let
S_{\xi}
be as in (
2.2
), then we have
s_{\alpha}\cdot\xi=\xi
for all
s_{\alpha}\in S_{\xi}
by definition (since then
\langle\xi,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle=0
), and it follows straightforwardly from (
5.1
) that

(5.7)

(e_{\xi}T_{\hat{s}})^{2}=\begin{cases}-e_{\xi}T_{\hat{s}}&\text{if $s\in S_{\xi}$}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}.

For later use we record the following immediate consequence of the above:

Lemma 5.5
.

Suppose that
\xi:T(\mathbb{F}_{q})\to k^{\times}
is a character and let
F\subseteq\overline{C}
be a face such that
S_{F}\subseteq S_{\xi}
. Then
e_{\xi}
is central in
{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
and
(e_{\xi}T_{\hat{s}})^{2}=-e_{\xi}T_{\hat{s}}
for all
s\in S_{F}
.

5.2.
Supersingular characters in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})

Our first aim is to understand morphisms between supersingular characters in the homotopy category
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
. Fix a face
F\subseteq\overline{C}
. Recall that
{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
is a Frobenius algebra and in particular selfinjective (cf.
[
9
, Example 4.1(ii)]
), and thus the Gorenstein projective model structure on
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{F})
has all objects cofibrant and fibrant. In particular, we see from
2.14
that

(5.8)

[{\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{n}]_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}={\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{F})}({\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{n})\cong{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}({\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{n})/{\sim}

where two morphisms
f
,
g
are equivalent under
\sim
if and only if
f-g
factors through a projective
{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
-module. We collect the facts we need about the relations between the Gorenstein projective model structures on the categories
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{F})
,
F\subseteq\overline{C}
, and
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
below:

Lemma 5.6
.

(i)

Let
F\subseteq\overline{C}
. If
{\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{n}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{F})
are simple then

[{\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{n}]_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}\cong\begin{cases}0&\text{if ${\mathfrak{m}}$ is projective}\\
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}({\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{n})&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(ii)

The functors
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
and
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
(for
F^{\prime}\subseteq\overline{F}\subseteq\overline{C}
) are right Quillen. They and their left adjoints preserve weak equivalences.

(iii)

The ‘diagonal’ functor

\Delta:=({\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}})_{F\subseteq\overline{C}}:{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})\to\prod_{F\subseteq\overline{C}}{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{F})

is right Quillen. Moreover, the Gorenstein projective model structure on
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
is the right transfer of the product model structure on
\prod_{F\subseteq\overline{C}}{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{F})
(i.e. with all classes of morphisms defined termwise) along
\Delta
. In particular,
{\operatorname{pd}}_{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}({\mathfrak{m}})<\infty
if and only if
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}({\mathfrak{m}})
is projective for all
F\subseteq\overline{C}
.

Proof.

For (i), we have that
[{\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{n}]_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}=0={\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}({\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{n})
if the simple modules
{\mathfrak{m}}
and
\mathfrak{n}
are not isomorphic (cf. (
5.8
)). If on the other hand
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong\mathfrak{n}
, then by Schur’s lemma we have that
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}({\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{m}})\cong k
is one-dimensional and hence either its quotient
[{\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{m}}]_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
is zero or the homotopy relation is trivial. But if
[{\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{m}}]_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}=0
then
{\operatorname{id}}:{\mathfrak{m}}\to{\mathfrak{m}}
factors through a projective, from which we deduce that
{\mathfrak{m}}
is projective. We thus get our desired isomorphism.

Part (ii) is immediate from
2.13
. For (iii), we obtain as a consequence of (ii) that
\Delta
preserves fibrations and weak equivalences as those are defined termwise in the target category. Since
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
is also faithful for each
F\subseteq\overline{C}
and all objects are fibrant, we in fact get that
\Delta
reflects fibrations. We are therefore left to show that
\Delta
has a left adjoint and reflects weak equivalences. For the former, the left adjoint is given by
({\mathfrak{m}}_{F})_{F\subseteq\overline{C}}\mapsto\bigoplus_{F\subseteq\overline{C}}{\mathfrak{m}}_{F}\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
. For the latter, by
2.8
it suffices to show that
\Delta
reflects trivial objects, i.e. that if
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
is such that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}({\mathfrak{m}})
is projective for all
F\subseteq\overline{C}
then
{\operatorname{pd}}_{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}({\mathfrak{m}})<\infty
. But this is the reverse implication shown in
[
19
, Lemma 4.2]
.
∎

5.6
(i) has the following immediate consequence which will be helpful later.

Corollary 5.7
.

If
{\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{n}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{F})
are semisimple such that each simple summand of
{\mathfrak{m}}
is non-projective, then
[{\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{n}]_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}={\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}({\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{n})
, i.e. the homotopy relation on
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}({\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{n})
is trivial.

A priori, in order to determine the morphism sets between two supersingular characters
\chi\neq\chi^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
we would be required to compute some explicit Gorenstein projective/injective replacements of these characters. However, there are no known techniques to do this that we are aware of. We will instead compute the image of these morphisms under the functor
{\operatorname{Ho}}(\Delta)
. Our aim is to show the following:

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
\chi\neq\chi^{\prime}
be two supersingular characters of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
of infinite projective dimension, given by the pairs
(J,\xi)
and
(J^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})
respectively. Let
\Phi=\sqcup_{i=1}^{r}\Phi_{i}
be the decomposition of the root system
\Phi
into irreducible components, ordered such that
\mathrm{rk}(\Phi_{1})\geq\cdots\geq\mathrm{rk}(\Phi_{r})
.

(i)

Suppose that
\Phi_{1}
is of rank two and
\Phi_{i}
is of rank 1 for all
i\geq 2
,
\xi=\xi^{\prime}
,
S=S_{\xi}
and
J\cap S_{i}=J^{\prime}\cap S_{i}
for all
i\geq 2
. Writing
S_{1}=\{s,s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\}
, assume further that, up to permuting the elements of
S_{1}
and
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle(\chi(T_{\hat{s}}),\chi(T_{\hat{s^{\prime}}}),\chi(T_{\hat{s^{\prime\prime}}}))

\displaystyle=(-1,-1,0),

\displaystyle(\chi^{\prime}(T_{\hat{s}}),\chi^{\prime}(T_{\hat{s^{\prime}}}),\chi^{\prime}(T_{\hat{s^{\prime\prime}}}))

\displaystyle=(0,-1,0)

and
s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}
are adjacent in the affine Dynkin diagram of
\Phi
. Then
{\operatorname{Ho}}(\Delta)([\chi,\chi^{\prime}]_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
has dimension 1 over
k
and doesn’t contain any isomorphisms.

(ii)

In every other case
{\operatorname{Ho}}(\Delta)([\chi,\chi^{\prime}]_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})=0
.

Remark 5.9
.

Later we will consider simple supersingular modules for the whole pro-
p
Iwahori-Hecke algebra
{\mathcal{H}}
, for which the action of
\widetilde{\Omega}
on supersingular characters comes into play. Suppose that
\omega\in\Omega
and fix a lift
\hat{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}
. Recalling that the group
\Omega
normalises
S
, if
\chi
is any character of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
corresponding to a pair
(J,\xi)
then
\chi^{\hat{\omega}}
corresponds to the pair
(J^{\hat{\omega}},\xi^{\hat{\omega}})
where
J^{\hat{\omega}}=\omega^{-1}J\omega
and
\xi^{\hat{\omega}}(t)=\xi(\hat{\omega}t\hat{\omega}^{-1})
(
t\in T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
). It follows in particular that
\left|J^{\hat{\omega}}\right|=\left|J\right|
. We therefore see that the two supersingular characters of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
from
5.8
(i) are not conjugate under the
\widetilde{\Omega}
-action.

We start preparing for the proof. Suppose that
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
are as given in
5.8
. Given any
f\in[\chi,\chi^{\prime}]_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
we obtain a collection
(f_{F})\in\prod_{F\subseteq\overline{C}}{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi,\chi^{\prime})
by letting each
f_{F}
be a choice of representative of
{\operatorname{Ho}}(\Delta)(f)_{F}={\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}})(f)
, and this satisfies the property that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(f_{F^{\prime}})\sim f_{F}
whenever
F^{\prime}\subseteq\overline{F}
. Note that either
f_{F}\sim 0
or otherwise the choice of
f_{F}
must be unique, cf.
5.6
(i). Our strategy will be to show that any such collection of morphisms must in fact be zero, up to homotopy, unless we are in the specific circumstances of part (i) of the Proposition. We therefore first need a method for determining whether some
f_{F}
arising as above is homotopic to zero. To that end, we have the following elementary but key result.

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
\chi\neq\chi^{\prime}
be two characters of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
, and suppose that we are given
(f_{F})\in\prod_{F\subseteq\overline{C}}{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi,\chi^{\prime})
such that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(f_{F^{\prime}})\sim f_{F}
whenever
F^{\prime}\subseteq\overline{F}
.

(i)

If
F^{\prime}\subseteq\overline{F}
and
f_{F^{\prime}}\sim 0
then
f_{F}\sim 0
.

(ii)

If
F^{\prime}\subseteq\overline{F}
and
f_{F}\sim 0
then either
f_{F^{\prime}}\sim 0
or
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
is projective.

(iii)

Assume we have three faces
F_{1},F_{2},F_{3}
with
F_{i}\subseteq\overline{F_{3}}
for
i=1,2
such that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{3}}}(\chi)
is not projective. Then
f_{F_{1}}\sim 0
if and only if
f_{F_{2}}\sim 0
.

Proof.

Part (i) is immediate from
5.6
(ii) because right Quillen functors preserve right homotopies. For part (ii), assume that
f_{F}\sim 0
and
f_{F^{\prime}}\not\sim 0
. Then by
5.6
(i) we must have
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}}(\chi)={\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}}(\chi^{\prime})
and
f_{F^{\prime}}=\lambda{\operatorname{id}}_{\chi}
for some
\lambda\in k^{\times}
. Then we have
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\lambda{\operatorname{id}}_{\chi})\sim f_{F}\sim 0
from which we deduce that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
is projective. For (iii), the condition that
f_{F_{1}}\sim 0
implies by (i) that
f_{F_{3}}\sim 0
. Since
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{3}}}(\chi)
is not projective it then follows from (ii) that
f_{F_{2}}\sim 0
as required. We get the converse by swapping
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
.
∎

In order to apply
5.10
, we need some criterion to establish when the restriction of a character of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
to some
{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
is not projective. Note that projectivity always holds when
F=C
since
{\mathcal{H}}_{C}=k[T(\mathbb{F}_{q})]
is semisimple. In general, we have the next result below which we gathered from the arguments of Koziol in
[
19
, §7]
. To prepare for its proof, we recall that the
0
-Hecke algebra
over
k
associated to a finite Coxeter group
W_{\text{gen}}
with set of Coxeter generators
S_{\text{gen}}
is the
k
-algebra with generating set
\{H_{s}\mid s\in S_{\text{gen}}\}
such that the braid relations hold and
H_{s}^{2}=-H_{s}
for all
s\in S_{\text{gen}}
(cf.
[
23
, Definition 1.1]
). The simple modules for such algebras are all characters and are indexed by the subsets
S_{\text{gen}}
, where for
L\subseteq S_{\text{gen}}
the associated character
\lambda_{L}
is defined by
\lambda_{L}(H_{s})-1
if
s\in L
and
\lambda_{L}(H_{s})=0
otherwise (cf.
[
23
, §3]
). When
L=\emptyset
, resp.
L=S_{\text{gen}}
, we call
\lambda_{L}
the trivial, resp. sign, character.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
F\subseteq\overline{C}
be a face, and let
\chi=(J,\xi)
be a character of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
.

(i)

If
S_{F}\nsubseteq S_{\xi}
then
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
is not projective.

(ii)

Assuming instead that
S_{F}\subseteq S_{\xi}
, then the following are equivalent:

(a)

{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
is not projective;

(b)

there exists an irreducible component
{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}
of
{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
such that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}}(\chi)
is not equal to a twisted sign or twisted trivial character; and

(c)

there exist
s,s^{\prime}\in S_{F}
corresponding to adjacent nodes in the affine Dynkin diagram such that
\chi(T_{\hat{s}})\neq\chi(T_{\hat{s^{\prime}}})
.

Proof.

To prove non-projectivity, it suffices to find
F\subseteq\overline{F^{\prime\prime}}\subseteq\overline{C}
such that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime\prime}}}(\chi)
is not projective, because
{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
is free over
{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime\prime}}
. For (i), we pick
F^{\prime\prime}
to be given by
S_{F^{\prime\prime}}=\{s\}
, where
s\in S_{F}\setminus S_{\xi}
. That
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime\prime}}}(\chi)
is not projective is then the content of the proof of
[
19
, Lemma 7.5]
. For (ii), we consider the idempotent
e_{\xi}
(cf. (
5.6
)). First note that
e_{\xi}
is central in
{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
and that the algebra
e_{\xi}{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
is the
0
-Hecke algebra associated to the finite subgroup
W_{F}\leq W
generated by
S_{F}
, cf.
5.5
. Also, from
{\mathcal{H}}_{F}=e_{\xi}{\mathcal{H}}_{F}\times(1-e_{\xi}){\mathcal{H}}_{F}
we see that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
factors through the algebra
e_{\xi}{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
. Hence, to show that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
is projective is equivalent to showing that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{e_{\xi}{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
is projective. By the braid relations,
e_{\xi}{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
is the tensor product of the
0
-Hecke algebras
e_{\xi}{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}
corresponding to the irreducible components of
{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
, and
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{e_{\xi}{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
decomposes similarly as the tensor product of its restrictions to each component.

Now, suppose
\mathcal{C}
is the 0-Hecke algebra of an irreducible Coxeter system
(W_{\text{gen}},S_{\text{gen}})
. For a character
\lambda_{L}
as above, we denote by
P_{L}
its indecomposable projective cover. It is shown in
[
23
, Theorem 5.1 & Theorem 5.2]
that the trivial and sign characters of
\mathcal{C}
are projective, and that for any
L\neq\emptyset
,
S_{\text{gen}}
there is
L^{\prime}\neq L
such that
\lambda_{L}
is a composition factor of
P_{L^{\prime}}
. In particular,
\lambda_{L}
is never projective for
L\neq\emptyset
,
S_{\text{gen}}
. Going back to our situation, if the restriction of
\chi
to each irreducible component of
{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
is a twisted sign or trivial character it then follows that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{e_{\xi}{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
is projective, since it is a tensor product of projectives. This shows (a)
\Rightarrow
(b). Moreover, (b)
\Rightarrow
(c) is clear by definition.

Finally, suppose that there exist
s,s^{\prime}\in S_{F}
adjacent in the affine Dynkin diagram with
\chi(T_{\hat{s}})\neq\chi(T_{\hat{s^{\prime}}})
. Then define
F^{\prime\prime}
