by
S_{F^{\prime\prime}}=\{s,s^{\prime}\}
, and once again note that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime\prime}}}(\chi)
factors through the algebra
e_{\xi}{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime\prime}}
. The latter is isomorphic to a 0-Hecke algebra of type
A_{2},

B_{2}
or
G_{2}
, and hence it follows from
loc. cit.
that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{e_{\xi}{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime\prime}}}(\chi)
is not projective, since it is neither a sign nor a trivial character by assumption. This gives (c)
\Rightarrow
(a).
∎

Suppose that we have a collection of morphisms as in
5.10
and fix a face
F\subseteq\overline{C}
. Our aim is to show that unless we are in the setting of
5.8
(i) and
F
is defined by
S_{F}=\{s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\}
(with
s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}
as in the statement of the Proposition), then
f_{F}\sim 0
. We will do this in a sequence of reductions where we will impose more conditions on
\chi
,
\chi^{\prime}
,
F
and
\Phi
as we go, and show that
f_{F}\sim 0
whenever those conditions fail. For this, first note that if
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
is projective then
f_{F}\sim 0
. Similarly, if there is a face
F^{\prime}\subseteq\overline{F}
such that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}}(\chi)\neq{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}}(\chi^{\prime})
then
f_{F}\sim 0
by
5.6
(i) and
5.10
(i). To compute
(f_{F})_{F\subseteq\overline{C}}
, we are thus left to consider a face
F
such that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
is not projective and
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}}(\chi)={\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}}(\chi^{\prime})
for all
F^{\prime}\subseteq\overline{F}
. In particular, note that this last condition implies that
\xi=\xi^{\prime}
since
k[T(\mathbb{F}_{q})]={\mathcal{H}}_{C}\subseteq{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
.

Assume from now on that
\xi=\xi^{\prime}
and that
F
is as above. Then the set

S(\chi,\chi^{\prime}):=\{s\in S\mid\chi(T_{\hat{s}})\neq\chi^{\prime}(T_{\hat{s}})\}

must be non-empty since
\chi\neq\chi^{\prime}
and
\xi=\xi^{\prime}
, and we denote its elements by
s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}
. For each
1\leq j\leq n
, we then write
S_{i_{j}}
for the irreducible component containing
s_{j}
. Observe further that our assumptions on
F
,
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
imply that for each
j
there is no face
F^{\prime}\subseteq\overline{F}
with
S_{F^{\prime}}=S_{F}\cup\{s_{j}\}
. In other words, we must have
S_{F}\cap S_{i_{j}}=S_{i_{j}}\setminus\{s_{j}\}
for every
1\leq j\leq n
by (
2.3
). In particular, the corresponding components
S_{i_{1}},\ldots,S_{i_{n}}
are all distinct.

Lemma 5.12
.

Suppose
\chi\neq\chi^{\prime}
are supersingular characters of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
and that
\underline{f}:=(f_{F})\in\prod_{F\subseteq\overline{C}}{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi,\chi^{\prime})
is as in
5.10
. Assume
F\subseteq\overline{C}
is a face such that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
is not projective and
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}}(\chi)={\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}}(\chi^{\prime})
whenever
F^{\prime}\subseteq\overline{F}
. With the above notation, we have:

(i)

for each
1\leq j\leq n
, the component
\Phi_{i_{j}}
is not of type
A_{1}
; and

(ii)

if
S_{F}\not\subseteq S_{\xi}
then
f_{F}\sim 0
.

Proof.

(i) As explained above we have
S_{F}\cap S_{i_{j}}=S_{i_{j}}\setminus\{s_{j}\}
for all
1\leq j\leq n
. Suppose
\Phi_{i_{j}}
is of type
A_{1}
for some
j
. Then
S_{i_{j}}
consists of two simple affine reflections
s_{j}
and
s^{\prime}_{j}
, say, with the same associated coroot. Since
\chi(T_{\hat{s_{j}}})\neq\chi^{\prime}(T_{\hat{s_{j}}})
we must have
s_{j}\in S_{\xi}
and so
S_{i_{j}}\subseteq S_{\xi}
by definition of
S_{\xi}
. Without loss of generality, we assume
\chi(T_{\hat{s_{j}}})=-1
and
\chi^{\prime}(T_{\hat{s_{j}}})=0
. Then by supersingularity of
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
we must have
\chi(T_{\hat{s^{\prime}_{j}}})=0
and
\chi^{\prime}(T_{\hat{s^{\prime}_{j}}})=-1
. But we also have that
s^{\prime}_{j}\in S_{F}
so that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)\neq{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi^{\prime})
, contradicting our assumptions.

(ii) Let
s^{\prime}\in S_{F}\setminus S_{\xi}
and note as above that we necessarily have
s^{\prime}\neq s_{j}
for any
1\leq j\leq n
. We can now define
F_{1},F_{2}\subseteq\overline{F_{3}}
by
F=F_{1}
,
S_{F_{2}}=\{s_{1},s^{\prime}\}
and
S_{F_{3}}=\{s^{\prime}\}
. Note that such a face
F_{2}
exists because of (i).
5.11
(i) then tells us that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{3}}}(\chi)
is not projective. As
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{2}}}(\chi)\neq{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{2}}}(\chi^{\prime})
,
5.10
(iii) gives us that
f_{F}\sim 0
as required.
∎

Lemma 5.13
.

Suppose that
\chi
,
\chi^{\prime}
,
\underline{f}
and
F\subseteq\overline{C}
are given as in
5.12
, and assume further that
S_{F}\subseteq S_{\xi}
.

(i)

There are
s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\in S_{F}
, adjacent in the affine Dynkin diagram of the root system
\Phi
, such that
\chi(T_{\hat{s^{\prime}}})\neq\chi(T_{\hat{s^{\prime\prime}}})
.

(ii)

Assume that one of the following holds:

(a)

there are
s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\in S_{F}
as in (i) such that
s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\notin S_{i_{j}}
for any
1\leq j\leq n
;

(b)

\left|S_{i_{j}}\right|>3
for some
1\leq j\leq n
; or

(c)

n=\left|S(\chi,\chi^{\prime})\right|>1
.

Then
f_{F}\sim 0
.

Proof.

The existence of
s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\in S_{F}
as in (i) follows from
5.11
(ii) because
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
is not projective by assumption. For (ii), we let
F_{1},F_{2}\subseteq\overline{F_{3}}
with
F=F_{1}
,
S_{F_{2}}=\{s_{j},s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\}
and
S_{F_{3}}=\{s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\}
, for a suitably chosen
1\leq j\leq n
so that such an
F_{2}
exists. In case (a), any choice of
j
works. In case (b), we pick
j
such that
\left|S_{i_{j}}\right|>3
. In case (c), we pick
j
such that
s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\notin S_{i_{j}}
. Either way, as
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{2}}}(\chi)\neq{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{2}}}(\chi^{\prime})
, it follows from
5.6
(i) and
5.10
(iii) that we only need to show that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{3}}}(\chi)
is not projective, which itself holds by
5.11
(ii).
∎

We now strengthen further our assumptions on
\chi
,
\chi^{\prime}
and the root system. We write
\Phi=\sqcup_{i=1}^{r}\Phi_{i}
as before and assume that
S_{1}=\{s,s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\}\subseteq S_{\xi}
, where
\chi(T_{\hat{s^{\prime}}})\neq\chi(T_{\hat{s^{\prime\prime}}})
and
s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}
are adjacent in the affine Dynkin diagram of
\Phi
. Furthermore, we assume that
s\in S_{1}
is the unique simple affine reflection for which
\chi(T_{\hat{s}})\neq\chi^{\prime}(T_{\hat{s}})
. By
5.12
and
5.13
(ii), we have
f_{F}\sim 0
for every
F\subseteq\overline{C}
unless these assumptions hold.

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
\chi
,
\chi^{\prime}
,
\underline{f}
be given as in
5.13
, and suppose that
\Phi
,
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
satisfy the assumptions above. Let
F\subseteq\overline{C}
be any face with
S_{F}\cap S_{1}=\{s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\}
.

(i)

If
S_{\xi}\neq S
then
f_{F}\sim 0
.

(ii)

If
r>1
and
\Phi_{i}
is not of type
A_{1}
for some
i\geq 2
, then
f_{F}\sim 0
.

Proof.

For (i), pick
t\in S\setminus S_{\xi}
and note that by assumption
t\notin S_{1}
. Define
F_{1},F_{2}\subseteq\overline{F_{3}}
by
F_{1}=F
,
S_{F_{2}}=\{t,s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\}
and
S_{F_{3}}=\{s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\}
. By
5.11
(ii),
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{3}}}(\chi)
is not projective so that by applying
5.10
(iii) we are reduced to showing that
f_{F_{2}}\sim 0
. For this, define
F^{\prime}_{1},F^{\prime}_{2}\subseteq\overline{F^{\prime}_{3}}
by
F^{\prime}_{1}=F_{2}
,
S_{F^{\prime}_{2}}=\{t,s,s^{\prime}\}
and
S_{F^{\prime}_{3}}=\{t,s^{\prime}\}
.
5.11
(i) gives that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}_{3}}}(\chi)
is not projective. Since
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}_{2}}}(\chi)\neq{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}_{2}}}(\chi^{\prime})
,
5.6
(i) and another application of
5.10
(iii) imply
f_{F_{2}}=f_{F^{\prime}_{1}}\sim 0
as required.

For (ii), we may therefore assume that
S_{\xi}=S
. Suppose that
\Phi_{i}
is not of type
A_{1}
for some
i\geq 2
. Since
\chi
is supersingular we may find
t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\in S_{i}
, adjacent in the affine Dynkin diagram, such that
\chi(T_{\hat{t^{\prime}}})\neq\chi(T_{\hat{t^{\prime\prime}}})
. Again appealing to
5.11
(ii), we have that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{3}}}(\chi)
is not projective where
S_{F_{3}}=\{t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\}
. Define
F_{1},F_{2}\subseteq\overline{F_{3}}
by
S_{F_{1}}=\{s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\}
and
S_{F_{2}}=\{s,t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}\}
. Once again
5.10
(iii) gives that
f_{F_{1}}\sim 0
since
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{2}}}(\chi)\neq{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{2}}}(\chi^{\prime})
. Hence
f_{\tilde{F}}\sim 0
by
5.10
(i), where
S_{\tilde{F}}=\{s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\}
. We already saw that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\tilde{F}}}(\chi)
is not projective and thus
5.10
(ii) implies that
f_{F}\sim 0
.
∎

Finally, in order to deal with part (i) of
5.8
and for later use we will need the following two lemmas.

Lemma 5.15
.

Suppose that
R_{1},R_{2}
are two Gorenstein
k
-algebras such that the tensor product algebra
R=R_{1}\otimes_{k}R_{2}
is also Gorenstein. Let
{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(R_{1})
and let
{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(R_{2})
with
{\operatorname{pd}}_{R_{2}}({\mathfrak{m}}_{2})<\infty
. If
{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}(R_{1})
, then
{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\otimes_{k}{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}_{1}\otimes_{k}{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}(R)
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the exact functor
-\otimes_{k}{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}:{\operatorname{Mod}}(R_{1})\to{\operatorname{Mod}}(R)
preserves weak equivalences, since then
{\operatorname{Ho}}(-\otimes_{k}{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}):{\operatorname{Ho}}(R_{1})\to{\operatorname{Ho}}(R)
is well-defined and preserves isomorphisms. By
2.8
(i), it suffices to show that this functor preserves trivial objects, i.e. that if
\mathfrak{e}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(R_{1})
has finite projective dimension over
R_{1}
then
\mathfrak{e}\otimes_{k}{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}
has finite projective dimension over
R
. But since
{\operatorname{pd}}_{R_{2}}({\mathfrak{m}}_{2})<\infty
by assumption, this is clear.
∎

Lemma 5.16
.

Suppose that
\Phi
is irreducible of rank 2 and let
\chi=(\xi,J)
be a supersingular character with
S_{\xi}=S
.

(i)

The augmentation map
\bigoplus_{F\in\mathscr{F}_{0}}\chi\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\to\chi
in (
5.4
) is a trivial fibration.

(ii)

Let
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
be a simple supersingular module such that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}({\mathfrak{m}})
contains
\chi
. If
p\nmid|\Omega_{\text{tor}}|
then the augmentation
\bigoplus_{F\in\mathscr{F}_{(0)}}{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{F}}({\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{F}}(\epsilon_{F}){\mathcal{H}}\to{\mathfrak{m}}
from (
5.2
) is a trivial fibration.

Proof.

It suffices to show that all the terms of higher degrees in the relevant complexes have finite projective dimension. For (i), since
\Phi
has rank 2 we have that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
is projective for any face
F
of positive dimension because
S=S_{\xi}
and
|S_{F}|\leq 1
, cf.
5.11
(ii). For (ii), we similarly get that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi^{\omega})
is projective for any face
F
of positive dimension and any
\omega\in\Omega
. Thus we deduce from (
5.5
) that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}({\mathfrak{m}})
is projective for all such
F
as well. By our condition on
p
, this is equivalent to saying that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{F}}({\mathfrak{m}})
is projective for all
F
of positive dimension by
[
19
, Lemma 3.4]
and we are done since
(\epsilon_{F}){\mathcal{H}}
is free for all
F\subseteq\overline{C}
(cf.
[
19
, Lemma 3.5]
).
∎

Proof of
5.8
.

Given a map map
f:\chi\to\chi^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
, set
f_{F}
to be any lift of
{\operatorname{Ho}}(\Delta)(f)_{F}={\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}})(f)
to
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi,\chi^{\prime})
.
5.12
,
5.13
and
5.14
imply that if we’re not in the precise situation of (i), then
f_{F}\sim 0
for all
F\subseteq\overline{C}
. This shows (ii). In the setting of (i), we still have that
f_{F^{\prime}}\sim 0
for all faces
F^{\prime}
for which
S_{F^{\prime}}\cap S_{1}\neq\{s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\}
. If on the other hand
S_{F^{\prime}}\cap S_{1}=\{s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\}
then
F^{\prime}\subseteq\overline{F_{1}}
, where
S_{F_{1}}=\{s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\}
, so that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{1}}}(f_{F^{\prime}})\sim f_{F_{1}}
. As
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{1}}}(\chi)={\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{1}}}(\chi^{\prime})
is not projective, cf.
5.11
(ii), it follows from
5.6
(i) that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{1}}}(f_{F^{\prime}})=f_{F_{1}}
and thus that
{\operatorname{Ho}}(\Delta)(f)
is uniquely determined by
f_{F_{1}}
. This gives that
\dim_{k}{\operatorname{Ho}}(\Delta)([\chi,\chi^{\prime}]_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})\leq 1
by
5.6
(i) again. Moreover, we see that
{\operatorname{Ho}}(\Delta)(f)
is never an isomorphism. Indeed, swapping
s^{\prime}
and
s^{\prime\prime}
if necessary, we may assume that
s
and
s^{\prime}
correspond to adjacent nodes in the affine Dynkin diagram and we let
F^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\overline{C}
be defined by
S_{F^{\prime\prime}}=\{s,s^{\prime}\}
. Then
f_{F^{\prime\prime}}\sim 0
is not a weak equivalence since
5.11
(ii) again shows that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime\prime}}}(\chi^{\prime})
is not projective.

We are left to show that there exists a map
f:\chi\to\chi^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
such that
f_{F_{1}}={\operatorname{id}}
. Suppose first that
r=1
and so that
\Phi=\Phi_{1}
is irreducible of rank 2. Then the augmentation

\varepsilon:C_{0}:=\bigoplus_{F\in\mathscr{F}_{0}}\chi\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\to\chi

is a trivial fibration, cf.
5.16
. Note that
C_{0}
is in fact a cofibrant replacement of
\chi
by
5.6
(ii) and that
F_{1}\in\mathscr{F}_{0}
. Now, the map
{\operatorname{id}}:{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{1}}}(\chi)\to{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{1}}}(\chi^{\prime})
has an adjoint
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
-linear morphism
g:\chi\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{1}}}{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\to\chi^{\prime}
. Extending
g
by zero on the other summands of
C_{0}
, this gives a map
f:\chi\xrightarrow{\varepsilon^{-1}}C_{0}\xrightarrow{g}\chi^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
which does satisfy
f_{F_{1}}={\operatorname{id}}
. Indeed, this follows because the map
\chi\to\chi\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{1}}}{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\subseteq C_{0}
,
x\mapsto x\otimes 1
, is an
{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{1}}
-linear splitting of
\varepsilon
and thus is equal to its inverse in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{F_{1}})
, and therefore
f_{F_{1}}
must be the adjoint morphism of
g
which is the identity by construction.

When
r>1
, first note that
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
factor through the algebra
{\mathcal{H}}^{0}_{\operatorname{aff}}:=e_{\xi}{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
(where
e_{\xi}
is central in
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
since
S=S_{\xi}
). The latter has generators
\{e_{\xi}T_{\hat{s}}\mid s\in S\}
satisfying the braid relations and such that
(e_{\xi}T_{\hat{s}})^{2}=-e_{\xi}T_{\hat{s}}
(cf. (
5.7
)). From the algebra decomposition
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}={\mathcal{H}}^{0}_{\operatorname{aff}}\oplus(1-e_{\xi}){\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
we get that
{\mathcal{H}}^{0}_{\operatorname{aff}}
is also a Gorenstein algebra and that a Gorenstein projective module, resp. module of finite projective dimension, over
{\mathcal{H}}^{0}_{\operatorname{aff}}
is still Gorenstein projective, resp. of finite projective dimension as an
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
-module. Hence any cofibrant replacement in
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}^{0}_{\operatorname{aff}})
is also a cofibrant replacement in
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
. It thus suffices to construct a map
f:\chi\to\chi^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}^{0}_{\operatorname{aff}})
whose restriction to
e_{\xi}{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{1}}
is the identity.

By the braid relations, we have a decomposition
{\mathcal{H}}^{0}_{\operatorname{aff}}={\mathcal{H}}_{1}\otimes_{k}{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
, where
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
is the subalgebra generated by
\{e_{\xi}T_{\hat{s}}\mid s\in S_{1}\}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
is the subalgebra generated by
\{e_{\xi}T_{\hat{s}}\mid s\in S\setminus S_{1}\}
. We correspondingly have decompositions
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}_{\operatorname{aff}}}(\chi)=\chi_{1}\otimes_{k}\chi_{2}
,
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}_{\operatorname{aff}}}(\chi^{\prime})=\chi^{\prime}_{1}\otimes_{k}\chi^{\prime}_{2}
where
\chi_{i},\chi^{\prime}_{i}
is a character of
{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
(
i=1,2
) and
\chi_{2}=\chi^{\prime}_{2}
by assumption, and the sets
J,J^{\prime}\subseteq S
also decompose as
J=J_{1}\sqcup J_{2}
and
J^{\prime}=J^{\prime}_{1}\sqcup J^{\prime}_{2}
with
J_{2}=J^{\prime}_{2}\subseteq S\setminus S_{1}
and
J_{1},J^{\prime}_{1}\subseteq S_{1}
. We note that
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
depend only on the root systems
\Phi_{1}
and
A_{1}\times\cdots\times A_{1}
(
r-1
terms) respectively. We thus let
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
be the
\mathfrak{F}
-rational points of two split semisimple, simply-connected groups of type
\Phi_{1}
and
A_{1}\times\cdots\times A_{1}
respectively, with corresponding pro-
p
Iwahori (affine) Hecke algebras
{\mathcal{H}}_{\operatorname{aff}}^{1}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{\operatorname{aff}}^{2}
. Then we get that
{\mathcal{H}}_{i}\cong e_{\mathbf{1}}{\mathcal{H}}_{\operatorname{aff}}^{i}
, say, and that
\chi_{i}
and
\chi^{\prime}_{i}
come from two supersingular characters of
{\mathcal{H}}_{\operatorname{aff}}^{i}
by taking the pairs
(J_{i},\mathbf{1})
and
(J^{\prime}_{i},\mathbf{1})
(
i=0,1
).

We now choose a cofibrant replacement
q_{c}:Q_{c}\chi_{1}\to\chi_{1}
in
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{1})
. The argument for
r=1
and
2.14
then show that there is a map
g:Q_{c}\chi_{1}\to\chi^{\prime}_{1}
such that
g\circ q_{c}^{-1}
goes to the identity on
\chi_{1}
under restriction of scalars to
e_{\xi}{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{1}}
. But since
\chi_{2}=\chi^{\prime}_{2}
has finite projective dimension over
{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
(cf.
5.4
), it follows from
5.15
that
q=(q_{c}\otimes{\operatorname{id}}):Q_{c}\chi_{1}\otimes_{k}\chi_{2}\to\chi_{1}\otimes_{k}\chi_{2}=\chi
is a weak equivalence in
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}^{0}_{\operatorname{aff}})
. Hence
g\otimes 1:Q_{c}\chi_{1}\otimes_{k}\chi_{2}\to\chi^{\prime}_{1}\otimes_{k}\chi^{\prime}_{2}=\chi^{\prime}
gives in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}^{0}_{\operatorname{aff}})
a map
f=(g\otimes 1)\circ q^{-1}
such that
f_{F_{1}}={\operatorname{id}}
, as required.
∎

Corollary 5.17
.

Suppose that
\chi\neq\chi^{\prime}
are two supersingular characters of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
of infinite projective dimension. Then they are not isomorphic in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
. In particular, if
\mathbb{G}
is semisimple and simply-connected then two supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules are isomorphic in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
if and only if they are isomorphic in
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
.

Proof.

Any isomorphism
f:\chi\to\chi^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
would induce an isomorphism
(f_{F})={\operatorname{Ho}}(\Delta(f))
in
\prod_{F\subseteq\overline{C}}{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{F})
. But it follows immediately from
5.8
that this cannot occur. The last part follows immediately because
{\mathcal{H}}={\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
if
\mathbb{G}
is semisimple and simply-connected.
∎

Remark 5.18
.

In case (i) of
5.8
, when
r=1
, the above proof actually tells us that

\displaystyle[\chi,\chi^{\prime}]_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{F\in\mathscr{F}_{0}}[\chi\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}},\chi^{\prime}]_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{F\in\mathscr{F}_{0}}[\chi,\chi^{\prime}]_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}

\displaystyle\cong{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{1}}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{1}}}(\chi),{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F_{1}}}(\chi^{\prime}))

where
F_{1}
is as in the proof. The second isomorphism follows from the Quillen adjunction in
5.6
(ii) and the third isomorphism holds by
5.6
(i) because
\chi\neq\chi^{\prime}
for any other face in
\mathscr{F}_{0}
. Thus
\dim_{k}[\chi,\chi^{\prime}]_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}=1
in this case. We suspect that
{\operatorname{Ho}}(\Delta)
might be faithful in general, perhaps identifying bijectively a morphism set
[{\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{n}]_{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
with the set of all
(f_{F})\in\prod_{F\subseteq\overline{C}}[{\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{n}]_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
such that
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}^{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}})(f_{F^{\prime}})=f_{F}
whenever
F^{\prime}\subseteq\overline{F}
. When the group
\mathbb{G}
has semisimple rank 1, this is in fact true. Indeed, as the complex (
5.4
) is a short exact sequence in that case and since
{\mathcal{H}}_{C}
is always a semisimple algebra, we have that the augmentation again realises
\bigoplus_{F\in\mathscr{F}_{0}}{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}{\mathfrak{m}}
as a Gorenstein projective replacement of
{\mathfrak{m}}
, for any
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
. After chasing through the adjunctions one deduces, for any
{\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{n}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
, that the isomorphism
[{\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{n}]_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\cong\bigoplus_{F\in\mathscr{F}_{0}}[{\mathfrak{m}},\mathfrak{n}]_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
obtained from the augmentation agrees with the map induced by
{\operatorname{Ho}}(\Delta)
. Finally, the compatibility condition between the
f_{F}
’s with
F\in\mathscr{F}_{0}
and
f_{C}
is empty here because
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{C})=0
.

We now study morphisms between simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
. Recall the parametrisation of these modules by pairs
(\chi,V)
as in the discussion preceding
5.3
.

Proposition 5.19
.

Suppose that
{\mathfrak{m}}=(\chi\otimes V)\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}=(\chi^{\prime}\otimes V^{\prime})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}}
are two simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules satisfying
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
. Assume that
{\operatorname{pd}}_{{\mathcal{H}}}({\mathfrak{m}})=\infty
and that
\chi,\chi^{\prime}
and
\Phi
are not as in
5.8
(i). Then
\chi^{\prime}=\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}
for some
\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}
.

Proof.

By
2.13
, we have that restriction of scalars
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}_{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}
is a right Quillen functor which preserves all weak equivalences. If
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
we therefore have that
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}_{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}})({\mathfrak{m}})
and
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}_{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}})({\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})
are also isomorphic in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
. By (
5.5
) we have

{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}_{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}({\mathfrak{m}})\cong\left(\bigoplus_{\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}/\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}}\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}\right)^{\oplus\dim_{k}V}.

Assume that
\chi^{\prime}\neq\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}
for all
\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}
. Since we get from the above that

(5.9)

[{\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}]_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\cong\left(\prod_{(\tilde{\omega},\tilde{\omega}^{\prime})\in\widetilde{\Omega}/\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}\times\widetilde{\Omega}/\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi^{\prime}}}[\chi^{\tilde{\omega}},(\chi^{\prime})^{\tilde{\omega}^{\prime}}]_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\right)^{\oplus(\dim_{k}V)(\dim_{k}V^{\prime})},

it then follows from
5.8
(ii) that
[{\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}]_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
has image zero under
{\operatorname{Ho}}(\Delta)
. If
f:{\mathfrak{m}}\to{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
denotes an isomorphism in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
, then this implies that
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}})(f)
is both zero and an isomorphism for every
F\subseteq\overline{C}
. We deduce that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}({\mathfrak{m}})
, and thus
{\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
, is projective for every
F\subseteq\overline{C}
. Applying
5.6
(iii) and
5.4
it follows that
{\operatorname{pd}}_{{\mathcal{H}}}({\mathfrak{m}})<\infty
, contradicting our assumptions.
∎

The conclusion of
5.19
is false when we are in the setting of
5.8
(i), as is visible from the following examples.

Example 5.20
.

Suppose that
G=\mathrm{PGL}_{3}(\mathfrak{F})
and assume that
p\neq 3
. Then
\Omega=\langle\omega\rangle\cong{\mathbb{Z}}/3{\mathbb{Z}}
where
\omega
is the image of
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0&\varpi\\
1&0&0\\
0&1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
in
W
. Similarly, let
\hat{\omega}
denote the image of the same matrix in
\widetilde{W}
. We have
S=\{s,s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\}
and let
\chi\neq\chi^{\prime}
be two supersingular characters of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
as in
5.8
(i), i.e. corresponding to the pairs
(J,\xi)
and
(J^{\prime},\xi)
with
S_{\xi}=S
,
J=\{s,s^{\prime}\}
and
J^{\prime}=\{s^{\prime}\}
. We will use the notation
\chi=(-1,-1,0)_{\xi}
and
\chi^{\prime}=(0,-1,0)_{\xi}
for these characters, the entries of the tuple corresponding to the value of the character at
T_{\hat{s}}
,
T_{\hat{s^{\prime}}}
and
T_{\hat{s^{\prime\prime}}}
, taken in that order. Note that
S=S_{\xi}
forces the character
\xi
to be fixed by
\omega
(cf.
5.29
later, viewing
\xi
here as a character of the torus of
\mathrm{GL}_{3}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
which is trivial on scalar matrices). Also, the element
\omega
acts on the set
S
by permuting its elements cyclically and correspondingly we see that
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi^{\prime}}=T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}={\mathcal{H}}_{\chi^{\prime}}={\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
. If we let
V
and
V^{\prime}
be two irreducible finite dimensional representations of
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi^{\prime}}
on which
T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
acts by
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
respectively, we then automatically have
V=\chi
and
V^{\prime}=\chi^{\prime}
. Thus we obtain two nonisomorphic simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules by taking
{\mathfrak{m}}=\chi\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}{\mathcal{H}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}=\chi^{\prime}\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}}{\mathcal{H}}
. Furthermore, those have infinite projective dimension (cf.
5.4
). We claim that
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
.

To see this, first note that (
5.5
) gives in this example that

(5.10)

{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}{\mathfrak{m}}\cong\chi_{0}\oplus\chi_{1}\oplus\chi_{2}:=(-1,-1,0)_{\xi}\oplus(0,-1,-1)_{\xi}\oplus(-1,0,-1)_{\xi},

and similarly that

(5.11)

{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}\cong\chi^{\prime}_{0}\oplus\chi^{\prime}_{1}\oplus\chi^{\prime}_{2}:=(0,-1,0)_{\xi}\oplus(0,0,-1)_{\xi}\oplus(-1,0,0)_{\xi}.

Next, observe that for every face
F\subseteq\overline{C}
with
F\neq C
the stabiliser
\Omega_{F}
of
F
in
\Omega
is trivial. Thus, for such faces,
{\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{F}={\mathcal{H}}_{F}
by
[
25
, Lemma 4.20(i)]
. Let
x
denote the vertex of
\overline{C}
with
S_{x}=\{s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\}
. By our assumption on
p
and since all faces in
\overline{C}
of a given dimension are
\Omega
-conjugate to each other, we obtain by applying
5.16
(ii) that the augmentation
\varepsilon:{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}({\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}{\mathcal{H}}\to{\mathfrak{m}}
in the Ollivier-Schneider resolution is a trivial fibration. Here we dropped the twist by orientation
\epsilon_{x}
character from the notation as it is trivial (since the stabiliser of
x
automatically fixes it pointwise). Completely analogously,
\varepsilon:{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}({\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}{\mathcal{H}}\to{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
is also a trivial fibration.

Now, by appealing to
5.11
(ii) we see that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}(\chi_{1})
and
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}(\chi^{\prime}_{2})
are projective, while
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}(\chi_{0})={\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}(\chi^{\prime}_{0})
and
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}(\chi_{2})={\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}(\chi^{\prime}_{1})
are not projective. By plugging all this into (
5.10
) and (
5.11
), we get a sequence of isomorphisms

{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}({\mathfrak{m}})\cong{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}(\chi_{0})\oplus{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}(\chi_{2})={\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}(\chi^{\prime}_{0})\oplus{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}(\chi^{\prime}_{1})\cong{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}({\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})

in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{x})
. Tensoring with
{\mathcal{H}}
, we obtain from the above the claimed isomorphism

{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}({\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}{\mathcal{H}}\cong{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}({\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}{\mathcal{H}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}

in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
.

Example 5.21
.

Suppose that
G=\mathrm{GL}_{3}(\mathfrak{F})
. Then
\Omega=\langle\omega\rangle\cong{\mathbb{Z}}
where
\omega
is once again the image of
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0&\varpi\\
1&0&0\\
0&1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
in
W
. We keep the same notation as in the previous example, so we let
\hat{\omega}
be the same lift to
\widetilde{\Omega}
and we let
\chi=(-1,-1,0)_{\xi}
and
\chi^{\prime}=(0,-1,0)_{\xi}
. Note that
S_{\xi}=S
forces
\xi=\det^{a}
for some
a\in{\mathbb{Z}}
(cf.
5.29
later). We now have
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi^{\prime}}=T(\mathbb{F}_{q})\times\langle\hat{\omega}^{3}\rangle
. Once again we let
V
and
V^{\prime}
be irreducible, finite dimensional representations of
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi^{\prime}}
on which
T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
acts by
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
respectively. Note that they are one dimensional because
\hat{\omega}^{3}
is central in
\widetilde{W}
, and thus are uniquely determined by the scalars
\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\in k^{\times}
by which
\hat{\omega}^{3}
acts on
V
and
V^{\prime}
respectively. Assume that
\lambda=\lambda^{\prime}
. We obtain two nonisomorphic,
3
-dimensional simple modules
{\mathfrak{m}}=(\chi\otimes V)\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}=(\chi^{\prime}\otimes V^{\prime})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}}
of infinite projective dimension. We claim that
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
.

The maps
\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{m}}:{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}({\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{x}}{\mathcal{H}}\to{\mathfrak{m}}
and
\varepsilon_{{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}}:{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{x}}({\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{x}}{\mathcal{H}}\to{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
from the Ollivier-Schneider resolution are trivial fibrations, cf.
5.16
(ii), so that it again suffices to show that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{x}}({\mathfrak{m}})\cong{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{x}}({\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{x})
. Note that
\Omega_{x}=\langle\omega^{3}\rangle
. Similarly to the previous example we have decompositions
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{x}}({\mathfrak{m}})\cong{\mathfrak{m}}_{0}\oplus{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}
and
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{x}}({\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}_{0}\oplus{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}_{1}\oplus{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}_{2}
. Here, all
{\mathfrak{m}}_{j}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}_{j}
are one-dimensional, and
{\mathcal{H}}_{x}
acts on
{\mathfrak{m}}_{0}
,
{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}
via the characters
(-1,-1,0)_{\xi}
,
(0,-1,-1)_{\xi}
and
(-1,0,-1)_{\xi}
respectively, and on
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}_{0}
,
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}_{1}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}_{2}
via the characters
(0,-1,0)_{\xi}
,
(0,0,-1)_{\xi}
and
(-1,0,0)_{\xi}
respectively. Furthermore,
\hat{\omega}^{3}
acts as
\lambda{\operatorname{id}}
on both
{\mathfrak{m}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
. Thus we see that
{\mathfrak{m}}_{0}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}_{0}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}_{1}
, and
5.11
(ii) together with
[
19
, Lemma 3.4]
imply that
{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}_{2}
have finite projective dimension over
{\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{x}
. Putting everything together, this gives

{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{x}}({\mathfrak{m}})\cong{\mathfrak{m}}_{0}\oplus{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}_{0}\oplus{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}_{2}\cong{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{x}}({\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})

in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}^{\dagger}_{x})
, as required.

5.3.
The
\widetilde{\Omega}
-action on morphisms

We recall the action of subgroups of
\Omega
on
{\operatorname{Hom}}
spaces for various subalgebras of
{\mathcal{H}}
as in
[
3
, §4.2]
. Let
A,B\subseteq{\mathcal{H}}
be two Gorenstein
k
-subalgebras such that
k[T(\mathbb{F}_{q})]\subseteq A\subseteq B
and
B
is free over
A
, both as a left and as a right module. Furthermore, we also let
\widetilde{\Omega}_{B}:=\widetilde{\Omega}\cap B
and denote by
\widetilde{\Omega}_{A}
the normaliser of
A
in
\widetilde{\Omega}_{B}
. One example to have in mind is
A={\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
,
\widetilde{\Omega}_{A}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{B}=\widetilde{\Omega}
and
B={\mathcal{H}}
, but we will see other situations where this applies as well. We will denote by
\Omega_{A}
the image of
\widetilde{\Omega}_{A}
under the canonical surjection
\widetilde{\Omega}\twoheadrightarrow\Omega
. We also denote by
{\mathcal{H}}_{A,\Omega_{A}}
the
k
-subalgebra of
{\mathcal{H}}
which is generated by
A
and
\{T_{\tilde{\omega}}\}_{\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{A}}
. To simplify notation, for any
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(B)
we will denote
{\operatorname{Res}}^{B}_{A}({\mathfrak{m}})
by
{\mathfrak{m}}|_{A}
.

We fix
\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{B}
and let
A^{\tilde{\omega}}:=T_{\tilde{\omega}^{-1}}AT_{\tilde{\omega}}\subseteq B
. We have
A^{\tilde{\omega}}=A
whenever
\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{A}
, and in general the map
a\mapsto T_{\tilde{\omega}^{-1}}aT_{\tilde{\omega}}
defines an isomorphism
A\cong A^{\tilde{\omega}}
of
k
-algebras. Thus
A^{\tilde{\omega}}
is also Gorenstein. This isomorphism induces an exact equivalence of categories
\mathcal{F}_{\tilde{\omega}}:{\operatorname{Mod}}(A)\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\operatorname{Mod}}(A^{\tilde{\omega}})
, where
\mathfrak{n}^{\tilde{\omega}}:=\mathcal{F}_{\tilde{\omega}}(\mathfrak{n})
is defined to have the same underlying
k
-vector space as
\mathfrak{n}
with
A^{\tilde{\omega}}
-action given by
x\cdot(T_{\tilde{\omega}^{-1}}aT_{\tilde{\omega}}):=xa
for all
a\in A
and all
x\in\mathfrak{n}^{\tilde{\omega}}
. Observe further that if
{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(B)
then the map
x\mapsto xT_{\tilde{\omega}}
defines a natural
A^{\tilde{\omega}}
-linear isomorphism
\nu_{\mathfrak{m}}:({\mathfrak{m}}|_{A})^{\tilde{\omega}}\xrightarrow{\cong}{\mathfrak{m}}|_{A^{\tilde{\omega}}}
, i.e. there is a natural isomorphism
\nu:\mathcal{F}_{\tilde{\omega}}\circ{\operatorname{Res}}^{B}_{A}\to{\operatorname{Res}}^{B}_{A^{\tilde{\omega}}}
.

Suppose now that
{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(B)
and let
f\in{\operatorname{Hom}}_{A}({\mathfrak{m}}_{1}|_{A},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}|_{A})
. By considering the image of
f
under
\mathcal{F}_{\tilde{\omega}}
and pulling back along the isomorphisms
\nu_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}}
(
i=1,2
), we obtain an
A^{\tilde{\omega}}
-linear map
f\cdot\tilde{\omega}:{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}|_{A^{\tilde{\omega}}}\to{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}|_{A^{\tilde{\omega}}}
defined by
(f\cdot\tilde{\omega})(x)=f(xT_{\tilde{\omega}^{-1}})T_{\tilde{\omega}}
for
x\in{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}
. Thus we have constructed a map

(-)\cdot\tilde{\omega}:{\operatorname{Hom}}_{A}({\mathfrak{m}}_{1}|_{A},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}|_{A})\to{\operatorname{Hom}}_{A^{\tilde{\omega}}}({\mathfrak{m}}_{1}|_{A^{\tilde{\omega}}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}|_{A^{\tilde{\omega}}})

which, by functoriality, is compatible with composition: if
{\mathfrak{m}}_{3}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(B)
, and
f:{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}|_{A}\to{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}|_{A}
and
g:{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}|_{A}\to{\mathfrak{m}}_{3}|_{A}
are
A
-linear maps then we have
(g\circ f)\cdot\tilde{\omega}=(g\cdot\tilde{\omega})\circ(f\cdot\tilde{\omega})
. If we put
\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{A}
in this construction, we then obtain an action of
\widetilde{\Omega}_{A}
on
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{A}({\mathfrak{m}}_{1}|_{A},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}|_{A})
. This action is trivial when restricted to
T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, and thus factors through an action of
\Omega_{A}
. Furthermore, we have
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{A,\Omega_{A}}}({\mathfrak{m}}_{1}|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{A,\Omega_{A}}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{A,\Omega_{A}}})={\operatorname{Hom}}_{A}({\mathfrak{m}}_{1}|_{A},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}|_{A})^{\Omega_{A}}
.

The above construction extends to morphisms in the homotopy category as follows. Since the functor
\mathcal{F}_{\tilde{\omega}}
is exact and preserves free and projective modules, it preserves all weak equivalences by
2.8
(i). Since
B|_{A}
and
B|_{A^{\tilde{\omega}}}\cong\mathcal{F}_{\tilde{\omega}}(B|_{A})
are free, we see in the same way that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{B}_{A}
and
{\operatorname{Res}}^{B}_{A^{\tilde{\omega}}}
also preserve all weak equivalences. Thus, these functors descend to homotopy categories and we get a diagram

(5.12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ho}}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ho}}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ho}}(A^{\tilde{\omega}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{B}_{A})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{B}_{A^{\tilde{\omega}}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{\tilde{\omega}}}

which commutes up to natural isomorphism (given by
\nu
). For
{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(B)
and
f\in[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{A}
, we now define
f\cdot\tilde{\omega}
to be the pullback of
\mathcal{F}_{\tilde{\omega}}(f)
along
\nu
. Again, this defines an action of
\Omega_{A}
on
[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{A}
. From (
5.12
), we immediately have the following:

Lemma 5.22
.

Suppose that
{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}(B)
and let
\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{B}
. Then the diagram

(5.13)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{A^{\tilde{\omega}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{B}_{A})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{B}_{A^{\tilde{\omega}}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)\cdot\tilde{\omega}}

commutes. In particular, we obtain that the image of the map

{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{B}_{A}):[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{B}\to[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{A}

lies in
[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{A}^{\Omega_{A}}
.

Remark 5.23
.

(i)

Assume that
{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}
is Gorenstein projective in
{\operatorname{Mod}}(B)
and let
\theta:\mathfrak{p}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}
be a surjection from a projective
B
-module. Then by
2.14
, we have a commutative diagram

(5.14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}_{B}({\mathfrak{m}}_{1},\mathfrak{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}_{B}({\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}_{A}({\mathfrak{m}}_{1}|_{A},\mathfrak{p}|_{A})^{\Omega_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}_{A}({\mathfrak{m}}_{1}|_{A},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}|_{A})^{\Omega_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{A}^{\Omega_{A}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

where the top row is exact. By construction, the dashed map
[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{B}\to[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{A}^{\Omega_{A}}
is given by
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{B}_{A})
and we see explicitly the last part of the Lemma here.

(ii)

Take
A={\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
and
B={\mathcal{H}}
so that
\Omega_{A}=\Omega
, and assume that
\mathbb{G}
is semisimple and that
p
does not divide
\left|\Omega\right|
. Then the functor of taking
\Omega
-invariants is exact and the bottom row of (
5.14
) is exact. Moreover, in that case the two leftmost vertical arrows in (
5.14
) are isomorphisms and it then follows from the 5-lemma that
[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{\mathcal{H}}\cong[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}^{\Omega}
for all
{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
with
{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}
Gorenstein projective. In particular, the functor
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
is faithful in that case. In general though, letting
\mathfrak{c}=\ker(\theta)
(with
\theta
as above) and recalling that
[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{\mathcal{H}}\cong{\operatorname{Ext}}^{1}_{\mathcal{H}}({\mathfrak{m}}_{1},\mathfrak{c})
and
[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}^{\Omega}\cong{\operatorname{Ext}}^{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}({\mathfrak{m}}_{1},\mathfrak{c})^{\Omega}
(cf.
2.16
), the exact sequence in
[
3
, §4.2, eq.(4.4)]
shows that the kernel of the map
[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{B}\to[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{A}^{\Omega_{A}}
is
H^{1}(\Omega,{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}({\mathfrak{m}}_{1},\mathfrak{c}))
and hence may fail to be zero when
\Omega
has non-trivial group cohomology. In general,
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}})
may thus fail to be faithful.

As an application, we highlight below a particular case of this construction which will be useful to us. In what follows, given
F\subseteq\overline{C}
and
\omega\in\Omega
, we write
F\omega
for the face satisfying
S_{F\omega}=\omega^{-1}S_{F}\omega
. In our earlier notation, we then have
{\mathcal{H}}_{F}^{\hat{\omega}}={\mathcal{H}}_{F\omega}
for any lift
\hat{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}
.

Corollary 5.24
.

Suppose that
{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}\in{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
and let
g\in[{\mathfrak{m}}_{1},{\mathfrak{m}}_{2}]_{\mathcal{H}}
. Let
\omega\in\Omega
and fix a lift
\hat{\omega}
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
. If we let
(g_{F})_{F\subseteq\overline{C}}:=({\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}})(g))_{F\subseteq\overline{C}}
, then we have
g_{F\omega}=g_{F}\cdot\hat{\omega}
for all
F\subseteq\overline{C}
.

Proof.

Apply
5.22
to
\hat{\omega}
, with
B={\mathcal{H}}
and
A={\mathcal{H}}_{F}
, to get the result.
∎

We now apply the above construction to study isomorphism classes of simple supersingular modules in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
. We let
\chi
be a supersingular character of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
and consider two irreducible, finite dimensional representations
V
and
V^{\prime}
of
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}
on which
T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
acts by
\chi
. Let
{\mathfrak{m}}=(\chi\otimes V)\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}=(\chi\otimes V^{\prime})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}}
, and fix
g\in[{\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}]_{\mathcal{H}}
. Recall that we have a decomposition
({\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}\cong\bigoplus_{\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}\backslash\widetilde{\Omega}}\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}\otimes(V^{\prime})^{\tilde{\omega}}
, and similarly for
{\mathfrak{m}}|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}
(cf. (
5.5
)). Correspondingly, we have
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}})(g)=(g_{\tilde{\omega}})_{\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}\backslash\widetilde{\Omega}}
, where
g_{\tilde{\omega}}\in[{\mathfrak{m}},\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}\otimes(V^{\prime})^{\tilde{\omega}}]_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}
.

For
F\subseteq\overline{C}
, we let
\widetilde{\Omega}_{F,\chi}:=\widetilde{\Omega}_{F}\cap\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}
and denote by
{\mathcal{H}}_{F,\chi}
the subalgebra of
{\mathcal{H}}_{F}^{\dagger}
generated by
{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
and
\{T_{\tilde{\omega}}\}_{\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{F,\chi}}
. The key result we need is the following:

Proposition 5.25
.

Let
F\subseteq\overline{C}
and suppose that
g
is an isomorphism. Then the map
g_{\tilde{\omega}}
above induces an
{\mathcal{H}}_{F,\chi}
-linear isomorphism
\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}\otimes V^{\tilde{\omega}}\cong\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}\otimes(V^{\prime})^{\tilde{\omega}}
for all
\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}\backslash\widetilde{\Omega}
such that
\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
is not projective. In particular, if
\chi|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
is not projective then
V\cong V^{\prime}
as representations of
\widetilde{\Omega}_{F,\chi}
.

Proof.

For
\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}\backslash\widetilde{\Omega}
, we let
\iota_{\tilde{\omega}}:\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}\otimes V^{\tilde{\omega}}\to{\mathfrak{m}}
denote the natural
{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}
-linear inclusion. Furthermore, let
l
denote the index of
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
. By (
5.5
), we may then regard elements of
[{\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}]_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}\cong\bigoplus_{\tilde{\omega},\tilde{\omega}^{\prime}}[\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}\otimes V^{\tilde{\omega}},\chi^{\tilde{\omega}^{\prime}}\otimes(V^{\prime})^{\tilde{\omega}^{\prime}}]_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
as
l\times l
matrices with entries in the Hom spaces
[\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}\otimes V^{\tilde{\omega}},\chi^{\tilde{\omega}^{\prime}}\otimes(V^{\prime})^{\tilde{\omega}^{\prime}}]_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
, and the image of any
g^{\prime}\in[{\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}]_{\mathcal{H}}
under
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}})
is the matrix
({\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}})(g^{\prime}_{\tilde{\omega}^{\prime}}\circ\iota_{\tilde{\omega}}))_{\tilde{\omega},\tilde{\omega}^{\prime}}
. Since the composite
g^{\prime}_{\tilde{\omega}^{\prime}}\circ\iota_{\tilde{\omega}}
has image under
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}})
equal to zero for
\tilde{\omega}\neq\tilde{\omega}^{\prime}
by
5.8
(ii) and
5.9
, we deduce that
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}})(g^{\prime})
is in fact a diagonal matrix.

Applying the above to
g^{\prime}=g
, we deduce that the diagonal matrix
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}})(g)
is an isomorphism and thus for all
\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}\backslash\widetilde{\Omega}
we have that
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}})(g_{\tilde{\omega}}\circ\iota_{\tilde{\omega}})
is an isomorphism in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}}_{F})
as well. By applying
5.22
with
A={\mathcal{H}}_{F}
,
B={\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}
and noting that
\widetilde{\Omega}_{A}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{F,\chi}
in that case, we see that this isomorphism is in fact in
[\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}\otimes V^{\tilde{\omega}},\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}\otimes(V^{\prime})^{\tilde{\omega}}]^{\widetilde{\Omega}_{F,\chi}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
. Finally, by applying
5.7
we see that this isomorphism is an actual
\widetilde{\Omega}_{F,\chi}
-equivariant isomorphism in the category
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{F})
whenever
(\chi^{\tilde{\omega}})|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
is not projective, as required.
∎

Theorem 5.26
.

Let
{\mathfrak{m}}=(\chi\otimes V)\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}=(\chi^{\prime}\otimes V^{\prime})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{H}}
be two simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules of infinite projective dimension over
{\mathcal{H}}
. Write
\xi=\chi|_{T(\mathbb{F}_{q})}
and
\xi^{\prime}=\chi^{\prime}|_{T(\mathbb{F}_{q})}
. Let
\Phi=\sqcup_{i=1}^{r}\Phi_{i}
be the decomposition of the root system
\Phi
into irreducible components, ordered such that
\mathrm{rk}(\Phi_{1})\geq\cdots\geq\mathrm{rk}(\Phi_{r})
, and correspondingly
S
decomposes as
\sqcup_{i=1}^{r}S_{i}
. In the case where
\Phi_{1}
is of rank two and
\Phi_{i}
is of rank 1 for all
i\geq 2
, and if
\xi=\xi^{\prime}
and
S_{\xi}=S
, assume further that one of the following conditions holds:

•

\chi(T_{\hat{s}})\neq\chi^{\prime}(T_{\hat{s}})
for some
s\in S_{i}
with
i\geq 2
; or

•

writing
S_{1}=\{s,s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\}
and up to permuting the elements of
S_{1}
, we do not have both
\chi(T_{\hat{s}})\neq\chi^{\prime}(T_{\hat{s}})
and
\chi(T_{\hat{s^{\prime}}})=\chi^{\prime}(T_{\hat{s^{\prime}}})\neq\chi(T_{\hat{s^{\prime\prime}}})=\chi^{\prime}(T_{\hat{s^{\prime\prime}}})
.

Suppose that
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
and that there is a face
F\subseteq\overline{C}
such that
\chi|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
is not projective and
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}\subseteq\widetilde{\Omega}_{F}
. Then
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
.

Proof.

Our assumptions say precisely that we are not in the setting of
5.8
(i). We may thus apply
5.19
to deduce that
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
are
\widetilde{\Omega}
-conjugate. By conjugating the pair
(\chi^{\prime},V^{\prime})
, we may therefore assume that
\chi=\chi^{\prime}
. We can then apply
5.25
to the face
F\subseteq\overline{C}
given in the statement to deduce that
V\cong V^{\prime}
as representations of
\widetilde{\Omega}_{F,\chi}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}
. This shows that
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
.
∎

In general, it may be that
\widetilde{\Omega}_{F,\chi}\neq\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}
whenever
\chi|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
is not projective so that the above Theorem does not apply (see e.g.
5.34
). However, our techniques still apply in many situations. To illustrate this, we finish this paper by classifying isomorphism classes of simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
for
G
an arbitrary finite product of general linear groups.

5.4.
Products of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
’s

In this subsection we assume that
\mathbb{G}=\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{r}}\times\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
, where
n_{1}\geq\ldots\geq n_{r}\geq 2
and
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\cong\mathbb{G}_{m}^{l}
is a split torus. Our motivation for considering this case is that all standard Levi subgroups of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
are of this form.

For
1\leq i\leq r
, write
T_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
for the
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-rational points of the diagonal matrices in
\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}}
. In any
T_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
and for
t\in\mathbb{F}_{q}
, write
{\operatorname{diag}}(t)_{j}
for the diagonal matrix with a
t
in the
j
-th diagonal entry and 1’s in every other diagonal entry. Since
\mathbb{G}
is a product of split reductive groups, we correspondingly have
\Omega=\Omega_{1}\times\cdots\times\Omega_{r}\times\Omega_{T^{\prime}}
where
\Omega_{T^{\prime}}\cong{\mathbb{Z}}^{l}
is central in
W
and, for each
1\leq i\leq r
,
\Omega_{i}=\langle\omega_{i}\rangle\cong{\mathbb{Z}}
where
\omega_{i}
is the image of the matrix
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&\cdots&\cdots&\varpi\\
1&\ddots&&\vdots\\
&\ddots&\ddots&\vdots\\
&&1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}}(F)
in
W
. We write
\hat{\omega}_{i}
for the image of that same matrix in
\widetilde{W}
. We also have a decomposition
S=S_{1}\sqcup\ldots\sqcup S_{r}
. We now let
\widetilde{\Omega}_{T^{\prime}}\cong\Omega_{T^{\prime}}\times T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
denote the pre-image of
\Omega_{T^{\prime}}
in
\widetilde{W}
, where here
\Omega_{T^{\prime}}
identifies with a central subgroup of
\widetilde{W}
. Note that
\hat{\omega}_{i}
acts on
T_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
by conjugation, with
\hat{\omega}_{i}^{-1}{\operatorname{diag}}(t_{1},\ldots,t_{n_{i}})\hat{\omega}_{i}={\operatorname{diag}}(t_{2},\ldots,t_{n_{i}},t_{1})
, so that
\widetilde{\Omega}\cong\widetilde{\Omega}_{T^{\prime}}\rtimes(\langle\hat{\omega}_{1}\rangle\times\cdots\times\langle\hat{\omega}_{r}\rangle)
.

We make a few preliminary remarks. In order to study simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules, we need to consider the stabiliser
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}
of a supersingular character.

Lemma 5.27
.

Let
\chi
be a character of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
. Then we have
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{T^{\prime}}\rtimes(\langle\hat{\omega}_{1}^{d_{1}}\rangle\times\cdots\times\langle\hat{\omega}_{r}^{d_{r}}\rangle)
for some
d_{1},\ldots,d_{r}\geq 1
such that
d_{i}
divides
n_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq r
.

Proof.

For each
1\leq i\leq r
, we write
{\mathcal{H}}_{{\operatorname{aff}},i}
for the
k
-subalgebra of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
generated by
\{T_{\hat{s}}\mid s\in S_{i}\}
and
T_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. Note that
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}\cong{\mathcal{H}}_{{\operatorname{aff}},1}\otimes_{k}\ldots\otimes_{k}{\mathcal{H}}_{{\operatorname{aff}},r}
as
k
-algebras, and that
\widetilde{\Omega}
normalises each
{\mathcal{H}}_{{\operatorname{aff}},i}
. Correspondingly,
\chi\cong\chi_{1}\otimes_{k}\cdots\otimes_{k}\chi_{r}
where
\chi_{i}:=\chi|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{{\operatorname{aff}},i}}
(
1\leq i\leq r
). We fix some
1\leq i\leq r
. The action of
\widetilde{\Omega}
on characters of
{\mathcal{H}}_{{\operatorname{aff}},i}
factors through
\Omega_{i}=\langle\omega_{i}\rangle\cong{\mathbb{Z}}
. Since
\hat{\omega}_{i}^{n_{i}}={\operatorname{diag}}(\varpi,\ldots,\varpi)
is central in
\widetilde{W}
, we deduce that the stabiliser
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi_{i}}
equals
\widetilde{\Omega}_{T^{\prime}}\rtimes\langle\hat{\omega}_{i}^{d_{i}}\rangle\times\prod_{j\neq i}\langle\hat{\omega}_{j}\rangle
for some
d_{i}|n_{i}
. Since
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}=\bigcap_{1\leq i\leq r}\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi_{i}}
, we deduce the claimed result.
∎

We continue to consider a character
\chi
of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
. If
V
is any finite dimensional representation of
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}
on which
T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
acts by
\chi
, we then have that
\hat{\omega}_{1}^{d_{1}},\ldots,\hat{\omega}_{r}^{d_{r}}
(with
d_{i}
as in
5.27
) and the generators of
\Omega_{T^{\prime}}
have a simultaneous eigenvector (since they commute) and this eigenvector spans a one dimensional subrepresentation. If
V
is irreducible it must therefore be one dimensional. When
\chi
is supersingular the corresponding simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-module
(\chi\otimes V)\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}}
then has
k
-dimension
d_{1}\cdots d_{r}
.

Lemma 5.28
.

Suppose that
\chi=(J,\xi)
is a character of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
and that
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{T^{\prime}}\rtimes(\langle\hat{\omega}_{1}^{d_{1}}\rangle\times\cdots\times\langle\hat{\omega}_{r}^{d_{r}}\rangle)
.

(i)

If there exist
s,s^{\prime}\in S_{i}
adjacent in the affine Dynkin diagram, for some
1\leq i\leq r
, such that
\chi(T_{\hat{s}})\neq\chi(T_{\hat{s^{\prime}}})
then
d_{i}>1
.

(ii)

If
S\neq S_{\xi}
then
d_{i}>1
for every
i
such that
S_{i}\nsubseteq S_{\xi}
.

In particular, if
\chi
is supersingular then
d_{i}>1
for all
1\leq i\leq r
.

Proof.

For (i), since the action of
\omega_{i}
permutes the nodes of the affine Dynkin diagram of
\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}}
cyclically, cf.
[
16
, §1.8]
, without loss of generality we may assume
s^{\prime}=\omega_{i}s\omega_{i}^{-1}
. Thus we have that
\chi(T_{\hat{s}})\neq\chi^{\hat{\omega}_{i}}(T_{\hat{s}})
and hence
\hat{\omega}_{i}\notin\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}
as required.

For (ii), suppose that we have
s\in S_{i}\setminus S_{\xi}
. Then there is some
1\leq j\leq n_{i}
such that the subtorus
T_{s}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
of
T_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
is equal to
\{{\operatorname{diag}}(t)_{j}\cdot{\operatorname{diag}}(t^{-1})_{j+1}\mid t\in\mathbb{F}_{q}\}
, where the subscripts are taken modulo
n_{i}
. The condition that
s\notin S_{\xi}
then corresponds to saying that there exists
t\in\mathbb{F}_{q}
such that
\xi({\operatorname{diag}}(t)_{j})\neq\xi({\operatorname{diag}}(t)_{j+1})
. Since
{\operatorname{diag}}(t)_{j+1}=\hat{\omega}_{i}{\operatorname{diag}}(t)_{j}\hat{\omega}_{i}^{-1}
, this shows that
\xi^{\hat{\omega}_{i}}\neq\xi
and thus
\hat{\omega}_{i}\notin\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}
as required.

Finally, if
\chi
is supersingular then it must be that for every
i
such that
S_{i}\subseteq S_{\xi}
, there exist
s,s^{\prime}\in S_{i}
as in (i). The last claim thus follows immediately from (i) and (ii).
∎

Remark 5.29
.

The arguments in the above proof show that
S_{\xi}=S
if and only if for all
1\leq i\leq r
, all
1\leq j\leq n_{i}
and all
t\in\mathbb{F}_{q}
,
\xi({\operatorname{diag}}(t)_{j})=\xi({\operatorname{diag}}(t)_{j+1})
. In particular, we see that
\xi^{\tilde{\omega}}=\xi
for every
\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}
whenever
S=S_{\xi}
. Since the image of
\xi
in
k
is contained in a subfield isomorphic to
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, we may view
\xi
as an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-valued character. Then we see from the above that
S=S_{\xi}
if and only if, for all
1\leq i\leq r
, there is some
0\leq a_{i}\leq q-2
such that
\xi|_{T_{i}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}=\det^{a_{i}}
.

Next, observe that for any face
F\subseteq\overline{C}
with
F\neq C
we have
\Omega_{F}\neq\Omega
. Indeed, we then have that
S_{F}\cap S_{i}
is a non-empty proper subset of
S_{i}
for some
i
and so, picking some
s\in S_{F}\cap S_{i}
and
s^{\prime}\in S_{i}\setminus S_{F}
, the fact that the action of
\Omega_{i}
on
S_{i}
by conjugation is transitive implies that
\omega_{i}^{-d}s\omega_{i}^{d}=s^{\prime}
for some
d
and thus that
\omega_{i}^{d}\notin\Omega_{F}
.

We begin our study of isomorphism classes of simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
in this setting. We first show that when two simple supersingular modules have the same underlying
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
-characters
\chi=\chi^{\prime}
(up to conjugation), then we can quickly reduce to the case where
S_{\xi}=S
.

Lemma 5.30
.

Let
\chi
be a supersingular character of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
of infinite projective dimension, and let
{\mathfrak{m}}=(\chi\otimes V)\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}=(\chi\otimes V^{\prime})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}}
be two simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules. Suppose that
S_{\xi}\neq S
. Then
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
if and only if
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
.

Proof.

We write
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{T^{\prime}}\rtimes(\langle\hat{\omega}_{1}^{d_{1}}\rangle\times\cdots\times\langle\hat{\omega}_{r}^{d_{r}}\rangle)
and we pick
s\in S\setminus S_{\xi}
, say with
s\in S_{i}
. Note that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
is not projective for any
F\subseteq\overline{C}
such that
s\in S_{F}
by
5.11
(i). In particular, this applies to the face
F
given by
S_{F}=\{\omega_{i}^{-d_{i}l}s\omega_{i}^{d_{i}l}\mid 0\leq l<n_{i}/d_{i}\}
, which is well-defined by
5.28
(ii). Also note that this
F
satisfies
\Omega_{F}=\langle\omega_{i}^{d_{i}}\rangle\times\Omega_{T^{\prime}}\times\prod_{j\neq i}\Omega_{j}
and thus we have
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}\subseteq\widetilde{\Omega}_{F}
. We may therefore apply
5.26
to obtain the result.
∎

Remark 5.31
.

When
\mathbb{G}
is a reductive group whose root system is irreducible and not of type
A
, then the action of
\Omega
on the affine Dynkin diagram can never be transitive except in type
D_{4}
(cf.
[
16
, §1.8]
). Hence, in those cases, we also have that two nonisomorphic simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules are still nonisomorphic in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
whenever they contain a common supersingular character
\chi=(J,\xi)
of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
such that
S_{\xi}\neq S
. The argument is the same as the above proof: if we pick
s\in S\setminus S_{\xi}
then the face
F
such that
S_{F}
is equal to the orbit of
s
under
\Omega_{\chi}
, which is well-defined by the non-transitivity of the
\Omega
-action, allows us to conclude using
5.26
.

In the case where
S=S_{\xi}
, the following result will be key in order to apply
5.26
.

Lemma 5.32
.

Let
\chi=(J,\xi)
be a supersingular character of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
such that
S=S_{\xi}
and fix
1\leq i\leq r
. For all
2<d
with
d
dividing
n_{i}
, if such a
d
exists, there is a face
F\subseteq\overline{C}
such that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
is not projective and
\Omega_{F}=\langle\omega_{i}^{d}\rangle\times\Omega_{T^{\prime}}\times\prod_{j\neq i}\Omega_{j}
.

Proof.

The requirements that
\chi
is supersingular and
S=S_{\xi}
ensure that there exist
s_{i},s_{i}^{\prime}\in S_{i}
, adjacent in the affine Dynkin diagram, such that
\chi(T_{\hat{s_{i}}})\neq\chi(T_{\hat{s_{i}^{\prime}}})
. If there is
d>2
dividing
n_{i}
then we automatically have
n_{i}>2
and we may define
F\subseteq\overline{C}
such that
S_{F}
is equal to the orbit of
\{s_{i},s_{i}^{\prime}\}
under the subgroup
\langle\omega_{i}^{d}\rangle
. By construction we have
\Omega_{F}=\langle\omega_{i}^{d}\rangle\times\Omega_{T^{\prime}}\times\prod_{j\neq i}\Omega_{j}
, and
5.11
(ii) gives that
{\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}(\chi)
is not projective as required.
∎

We will be unable to use the above with
d=d_{j}
(some
j
) in the case that
d_{i}=2
for all
1\leq i\leq r
. We deal with that case next, first introducing some notation. For each
1\leq i\leq r
we let
[r]_{i}:=\{1\leq j\leq r\mid j\neq i\}
, and for any
\mathbf{j}\subseteq[r]_{i}
we let
\hat{\omega}_{\mathbf{j}}:=\prod_{j\in\mathbf{j}}\hat{\omega}_{j}\in\widetilde{\Omega}
. We fix a generating set
\omega_{T^{\prime},1},\ldots,\omega_{T^{\prime},l}
for
\Omega_{T^{\prime}}
and write
\hat{\omega}_{T^{\prime},1},\ldots,\hat{\omega}_{T^{\prime},l}
for the corresponding lifts to
\widetilde{\Omega}_{T^{\prime}}
.

Proposition 5.33
.

Suppose that for every
1\leq i\leq r
there is some
m_{i}\geq 1
such that
n_{i}=2m_{i}
, and let
\chi=(J,\xi)
be a supersingular character of
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}
with
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{T^{\prime}}\rtimes(\langle\hat{\omega}_{1}^{2}\rangle\times\cdots\times\langle\hat{\omega}_{r}^{2}\rangle)
,
S=S_{\xi}
and
{\operatorname{pd}}_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}(\chi)=\infty
. Suppose that
V
and
V^{\prime}
are two one-dimensional representations of
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}
on which
T(\mathbb{F}_{q})
acts via
\chi
, and let
{\mathfrak{m}}=(\chi\otimes V)\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}=(\chi\otimes V^{\prime})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}}
. If
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
then
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
.

Proof.

For each
1\leq i\leq r
, we write
\lambda_{i}
and
\lambda^{\prime}_{i}
for the scalar by which
\hat{\omega}_{i}^{2}
acts on
V
and
V^{\prime}
, respectively. Similarly, for each
1\leq j\leq l
, we write
\nu_{j}
and
\nu_{j}^{\prime}
for the scalar by which
\hat{\omega}_{T^{\prime},j}
acts on
V
and
V^{\prime}
respectively. From our assumption on
\chi
and by
5.6
(iii), there is a face
F\subseteq\overline{C}
for which
\chi|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
is not projective. Note that we have
\widetilde{\Omega}_{T^{\prime}}\subseteq\widetilde{\Omega}_{F,\chi}
and
\hat{\omega}_{i}^{2}\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{F,\chi}
for each
i
such that
n_{i}=2
. Applying
5.25
we deduce that
\nu_{j}=\nu_{j}^{\prime}
for all
j
and that
\lambda_{i}=\lambda^{\prime}_{i}
whenever
n_{i}=2
.

We are thus left to show that
\lambda_{i}=\lambda^{\prime}_{i}
for all
i
for which
n_{i}>2
, i.e.
m_{i}>1
. Pick
s_{0}\in S_{i}
and write
s_{j}=\omega_{i}^{-j}s_{0}\omega_{i}^{j}
for
1\leq j\leq n_{i}-1
. By our assumptions on
\chi
, without loss of generality we may assume that
J\cap S_{i}=\{s_{2j}\mid 0\leq j\leq m_{i}-1\}
. Similarly
\chi^{\hat{\omega}_{i}}=(J^{\omega_{i}},\xi)
then satisfies
J^{\omega_{i}}\cap S_{i}=\{s_{2j+1}\mid 0\leq j\leq m_{i}-1\}
. Note that any
\widetilde{\Omega}
-conjugate of
\chi
is non-projective when restricted to any
{\mathcal{H}}_{F}
such that
S_{F}\cap S_{i}
contains two adjacent simple affine reflections in the affine Dynkin diagram, cf.
5.11
(ii). Fix an isomorphism
f:{\mathfrak{m}}\to{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
, write
V=kv
and
V^{\prime}=kv^{\prime}
, and for each
F\subseteq\overline{C}
let
f_{F}:{\mathfrak{m}}\to{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
be a lift in
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}}_{F})
of
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\operatorname{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}})(f)
.

Pick a prime factor
\ell
of
m_{i}
. Similarly to the proof of
5.32
, we let
F
be the face given by taking
S_{F}
to be the
\langle\omega_{i}^{2\ell}\rangle
-orbit of
\{s_{0},s_{1}\}
so that
\chi|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
and
(\chi^{\hat{\omega}_{i}})|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
are not projective. We also have that
\chi|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F\omega_{i}^{-1}}}
and
(\chi^{\hat{\omega}})|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F\omega_{i}^{-1}}}
are not projective.

Next, since
s_{0}\in S_{F}\cap S_{F\omega_{i}^{-1}}
it follows that
\left|S_{F}\cup S_{F\omega_{i}^{-1}}\right|<2\cdot\left|S_{F}\right|=4m_{i}/\ell\leq n_{i}
and hence there is a face
F^{\prime}\subseteq\overline{F}
with
S_{F^{\prime}}=S_{F}\cup S_{F\omega_{i}^{-1}}
. Again both
\chi
and
\chi^{\hat{\omega}_{i}}
have non-projective restrictions to
{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}
. Also note that for all
\tilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\Omega}
we have that
\chi^{\tilde{\omega}}|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}}
is equal to either
\chi|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}}
or
(\chi^{\hat{\omega}_{i}})|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F^{\prime}}}
. By the proof of
5.25
and
5.7
we deduce that, with respect to the bases
\bigcup_{\mathbf{j}\subseteq[r]_{i}}\{v\otimes T_{\hat{\omega}_{\mathbf{j}}},v\otimes T_{\hat{\omega}_{\mathbf{j}}}T_{\hat{\omega}_{i}}\}
and
\bigcup_{\mathbf{j}\subseteq[r]_{i}}\{v^{\prime}\otimes T_{\hat{\omega}_{\mathbf{j}}},v^{\prime}\otimes T_{\hat{\omega}_{\mathbf{j}}}T_{\hat{\omega}_{i}}\}
of
{\mathfrak{m}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
respectively,
f_{F^{\prime}}
is a block diagonal matrix
A
whose blocks are
2\times 2
-matrices, indexed by the subsets
\mathbf{j}\subseteq[r]_{i}
, equal to
\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{\mathbf{j}}&0\\
0&\mu^{\prime}_{\mathbf{j}}\end{pmatrix}
for some scalars
\mu_{\mathbf{j}},\mu^{\prime}_{\mathbf{j}}\in k^{\times}
. Similarly,
f_{F}
and
f_{F\omega_{i}^{-1}}
are also given by block diagonal matrices of the same form as above, and
5.7
ensures that both of these are actually equal to
A
since
f_{F}\sim(f_{F^{\prime}})|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
and
f_{F\omega_{i}^{-1}}\sim(f_{F^{\prime}})|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F\omega_{i}^{-1}}}
.

Now, with respect to the above bases, the action of
T_{\hat{\omega}_{i}}
on
{\mathfrak{m}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
is given by two block diagonal matrices
B
and
B^{\prime}
respectively, whose
2\times 2
-blocks are indexed by the subsets
\mathbf{j}\subseteq[r]_{i}
as above but with each block equal to
\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
\lambda_{i}&0\end{pmatrix}
and
\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
\lambda_{i}^{\prime}&0\end{pmatrix}
respectively (where we view these matrices as acting on the right). Using that
f_{F}=f_{F\omega_{i}^{-1}}\cdot\omega
, cf.
5.24
and
5.7
, we deduce that
A=B^{-1}AB^{\prime}
. Looking at each
2\times 2
-block, this tells us that

\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{\mathbf{j}}&0\\
0&\mu_{\mathbf{j}}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\lambda_{i}^{-1}\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{\mathbf{j}}&0\\
0&\mu_{\mathbf{j}}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
\lambda_{i}^{\prime}&0\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{\mathbf{j}}^{\prime}\lambda_{i}^{\prime}\lambda_{i}^{-1}&0\\
0&\mu_{\mathbf{j}}\end{pmatrix}

for all
\mathbf{j}\subseteq[r]_{i}
. We deduce that
\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{i}^{\prime}
as required.
∎

Remark 5.34
.

Assuming that
(n_{1},\ldots,n_{r})\neq(2,\ldots,2)
, note that
5.33
is the only situation where we could not appeal to
5.26
. Indeed, by the description of
\chi
as given in the proof we obtain from
5.11
(ii) that a face
F\subseteq\overline{C}
satisfies that
\chi|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{F}}
is not projective if and only if
S_{F}
contains two adjacent nodes in the affine Dynkin diagram. If those belong to
S_{i}
, say, then
F\omega_{i}^{2}\neq F
and we deduce that
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}\nsubseteq\widetilde{\Omega}_{F}
.

Using all of the above, we can finally establish a classification theorem for isomorphism classes in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
of simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules.

Theorem 5.35
.

Suppose that
\mathbb{G}=\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{r}}\times\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
, where
n_{1}\geq\ldots\geq n_{r}\geq 2
and
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\cong\mathbb{G}_{m}^{l}
is a split torus. Let
{\mathfrak{m}}=(\chi\otimes V)\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi}}{\mathcal{H}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{H}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi^{\prime}}}(\chi^{\prime}\otimes V^{\prime})
be two simple supersingular
{\mathcal{H}}
-modules of infinite projective dimension over
{\mathcal{H}}
, where
\chi=(J,\xi)
and
\chi^{\prime}=(J^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})
.

(i)

Suppose that
(n_{1},n_{2},\ldots,n_{r})\neq(3,2,\ldots,2)
. Then
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
if and only if
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
.

(ii)

Suppose that
(n_{1},n_{2},\ldots,n_{r})=(3,2,\ldots,2)
and
{\mathfrak{m}}\not\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
. Then
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
if and only if
\xi=\xi^{\prime}
,
S=S_{\xi}
,
J^{\prime}\subseteq J
such that
\left|J\cap S_{1}\right|=2
and
\left|J^{\prime}\cap S_{1}\right|=1
(up to permuting
{\mathfrak{m}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
), and
V\cong V^{\prime}
as representations of
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

We write
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{T^{\prime}}\rtimes(\langle\tilde{\omega}_{1}^{d_{1}}\rangle\times\cdots\times\langle\tilde{\omega}_{r}^{d_{r}}\rangle)
and
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi^{\prime}}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{T^{\prime}}\rtimes(\langle\tilde{\omega}_{1}^{d^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle\times\cdots\times\langle\tilde{\omega}_{r}^{d^{\prime}_{r}}\rangle)
. For
1\leq i\leq r
, let
\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i}^{\prime}\in k^{\times}
for the scalars by which
\tilde{\omega}_{i}^{d_{i}}
and
\tilde{\omega}_{i}^{d_{i}^{\prime}}
act on
V
and
V^{\prime}
respectively. Similarly, for each
1\leq j\leq l
, we write
\nu_{j}
and
\nu_{j}^{\prime}
for the scalar by which
\hat{\omega}_{T^{\prime},j}
acts on
V
and
V^{\prime}
respectively. Note that
{\operatorname{pd}}_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}(\chi)={\operatorname{pd}}_{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}(\chi^{\prime})=\infty
by
5.4
.

We first claim that the following holds:

(5.15)

If
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
are
\widetilde{\Omega}
-conjugate, then
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})\iff{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
.

One direction being clear, we suppose that
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
and we note that, replacing the pair
(\chi^{\prime},V^{\prime})
by some conjugate if necessary, we may in fact assume that
\chi=\chi^{\prime}=(J,\xi)
, say.

If
S\neq S_{\xi}
then
5.30
implies our claim, so that we only need to consider the case
S=S_{\xi}
. If
d_{i}>2
for some
1\leq i\leq r
then
5.32
and
5.26
together imply that
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
. If
d_{i}=2
for all
1\leq i\leq r
, we get
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
from
5.33
. This concludes the proof of (
5.15
).

When
(n_{1},n_{2},\ldots,n_{r})\neq(3,2,\ldots,2)
,
5.19
implies that
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
must be
\widetilde{\Omega}
-conjugate for
{\mathfrak{m}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
to be isomorphic in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
. Thus (i) follows immediately from (
5.15
). We are therefore left to consider the case where
(n_{1},n_{2},\ldots,n_{r})=(3,2,\ldots,2)
and
\chi,\chi^{\prime}
are not
\widetilde{\Omega}
-conjugate. Then
5.19
again implies that, up to permuting
{\mathfrak{m}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
, we must have
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
as in the statement in order for
{\mathfrak{m}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
to be isomorphic in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
. Note that we then have
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi^{\prime}}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{T^{\prime}}\rtimes(\langle\hat{\omega}_{1}^{3}\rangle\times\langle\hat{\omega}_{2}^{2}\rangle\times\cdots\times\langle\hat{\omega}_{r}^{2}\rangle)
.

We must finally show that under these assumptions on
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
,
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
if and only if
\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{i}^{\prime}
and
\nu_{j}=\nu_{j}^{\prime}
for all
1\leq i\leq r
and all
1\leq j\leq l
. Assume first that these equalities all hold. We argue similarly to the proof of
5.8
. By the product decomposition of
\mathbb{G}
, we have an algebra isomorphism
{\mathcal{H}}\cong{\mathcal{H}}_{1}\otimes_{k}{\mathcal{H}}_{\geq 2}
where
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
is the pro-
p
Iwahori-Hecke algebra of
\mathrm{GL}_{3}(\mathfrak{F})
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{\geq 2}
is the pro-
p
Iwahori-Hecke algebra of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathfrak{F})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathfrak{F})\times T^{\prime}
(with
r-1

\mathrm{GL}_{2}
-factors). There is a similar decomposition
{\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{aff}}}={\mathcal{H}}_{{\operatorname{aff}},1}\otimes_{k}{\mathcal{H}}_{{\operatorname{aff}},\geq 2}
and correspondingly we have
\chi=\chi_{1}\otimes_{k}\chi_{\geq 2}
. Also, we have a decomposition
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi}=\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi,1}\times\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi,\geq 2}
, and we may thus write
V=V_{1}\otimes_{k}V_{\geq 2}
with
V_{1}=kv_{1}
and
V_{\geq 2}=kv_{\geq 2}
one-dimensional representations of
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi,1}
and
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\chi,\geq 2}
respectively.

Write
v=v_{1}\otimes v_{\geq 2}\in V
. By considering the basis
\bigcup_{\mathbf{j}\subseteq[r]_{1}}\{v\otimes T_{\hat{\omega}_{\mathbf{j}}},v\otimes T_{\hat{\omega}_{\mathbf{j}}}T_{\hat{\omega}_{1}},v\otimes T_{\hat{\omega}_{\mathbf{j}}}T_{\hat{\omega}^{2}_{1}}\}
of
{\mathfrak{m}}
, we see by inspection that
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\otimes_{k}{\mathfrak{m}}_{\geq 2}
where
{\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\cong(\chi_{1}\otimes V_{1})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi,1}}{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\geq 2}\cong(\chi_{\geq 2}\otimes V_{\geq 2})\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\chi,\geq 2}}{\mathcal{H}}_{\geq 2}
. We note that these are simple supersingular modules of
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{\geq 2}
respectively, and that
{\operatorname{pd}}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{\geq 2}}({\mathfrak{m}}_{\geq 2})<\infty
by
5.4
. Completely analogously, we have a decomposition
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}_{1}\otimes_{k}{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}_{\geq 2}
where the factors are simple supersingular modules over
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{\geq 2}
respectively. Furthermore, by our assumptions on
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
we have
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\geq 2}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}_{\geq 2}
. We then deduce from
5.21
and
5.15
that
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
as required.

Conversely, suppose that
{\mathfrak{m}}\cong{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
. Write
\underline{\lambda}=(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{r})
,
\underline{\lambda^{\prime}}=(\lambda^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\lambda^{\prime}_{r})
,
\underline{\nu}=(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{l})
and
\underline{\nu^{\prime}}=(\nu^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\nu^{\prime}_{l})
. The modules
{\mathfrak{m}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}
are determined uniquely, up to isomorphism, in
{\operatorname{Mod}}({\mathcal{H}})
by the triples
(\chi,\underline{\lambda},\underline{\nu})
and
(\chi^{\prime},\underline{\lambda^{\prime}},\underline{\nu^{\prime}})
, and we write
{\mathfrak{m}}=(\chi,\underline{\lambda},\underline{\nu})
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime}=(\chi^{\prime},\underline{\lambda^{\prime}},\underline{\nu^{\prime}})
for short. Using this notation, we then have by the converse direction above that

(\chi,\underline{\lambda},\underline{\nu})\cong(\chi^{\prime},\underline{\lambda^{\prime}},\underline{\nu^{\prime}})\cong(\chi,\underline{\lambda^{\prime}},\underline{\nu^{\prime}})

in
{\operatorname{Ho}}({\mathcal{H}})
. By (
5.15
) this implies
\underline{\lambda}=\underline{\lambda^{\prime}}
and
\underline{\nu}=\underline{\nu^{\prime}}
, as required.
∎
